# Barkpark Connectors — Epic Charter

Make it a first-class, two-minute product action to connect a Barkpark agent to an external
service. Design paper: `/papers/personal-agent-provider-bridge`. Epic: `bp-connectors-epic`
(task `task-e640bb01fca6ea38`).

## Vision

A Barkpark workspace connects an agent to the services its people already use — you TALK TO the
agent from Slack/Discord/Telegram/Teams/WhatsApp/iMessage, and the agent ACTS ON GitHub/Linear —
through one provider-agnostic core, with a Barkpark **Session as the durable cross-surface memory**
(start on Slack Monday, continue on Discord Friday, same thread). Cloud multi-tenant from day one:
each workspace installs its own connectors, credentials isolated, the agent runs sandboxed with
NO host access. The brain and most of the plumbing already exist — this epic builds the *connect
experience* on top of them, not a new engine.

## Two directions, one catalog

- **Channel connector** = inbound, you talk to the agent → **Vercel Chat SDK** adapter (`chat` npm).
- **Tool connector** = outbound, the agent acts → **MCP** (Barkpark already serves MCP via `bp mcp serve`; the mirror is the runner consuming an external MCP).

## Decisions

Tagged **[RATIFIED]** = user/design decision, locked. **[VERIFY]** = must be proven by the verify
fleet with run output before a builder relies on it (distrust vacuous green). **[DECIDED]** = settled
at a wave's Decide with verify rounds in hand.

- **D1 [RATIFIED] — Cloud multi-tenant from day one.** Every workspace connects its own services; credentials + sessions isolated per workspace.
- **D2 [RATIFIED] — Channel-first.** The first shipped direction is Channel connectors; Tool connectors (GitHub/Linear via MCP) follow.
- **D3 [RATIFIED] — Six first-focus channels, grouped by ONBOARDING not code.** Easy trio (Telegram, Slack, Discord — light official adapters, prove the core) → heavy pair (Teams = Azure Bot reg + Bot Framework; WhatsApp = Meta Business + number + app review + 24h session window) → **iMessage = self-hosted operator profile ONLY** (community adapter, Apple has no server API, needs a Mac + Apple ID relay; does NOT fit Cloud multi-tenant).
- **D4 [RATIFIED] — External Node bridge on the Chat SDK, not three protocols in Elixir.** One codebase, swappable adapters, streaming piped from Barkpark SSE. The BEAM stays the engine; the bridge is a client of `/v1/chat`.
- **D5 [RATIFIED] — Two execution profiles behind one Connector/Session layer.** Self-hosted/operator = the `claude` CLI subprocess, full host access, `bypassPermissions` OK, gate = your user-id. Cloud tenant = a **sandboxed runner per workspace**, no host access, connector-scoped tools, gate = external user → workspace principal. This **reverses** `bypassPermissions` for Cloud (it survives only self-hosted).
- **D6 [RATIFIED] — Session = memory, keyed by the Claude session UUID (`chat_sessions.id`).** A thread ↔ session map (`thread_id → session_uuid`) is the only per-conversation state the bridge holds. The SAME session is reachable from any channel by its UUID (cross-surface continuity). See D31 for the wave-3 scope refinement (automatic thread→session mapping is per-CHANNEL; true cross-CHANNEL resumption needs identity-linking, deferred to P4).
- **D7 [VERIFIED — total leak] — Session ownership migration.** Proven: `chat_sessions`/`chat_messages` had ZERO owner/workspace column on origin/main; Slice A added `owner_workspace_id` (nullable `binary_id`, no FK, no default) + sealed the store funnels. See D14–D17.
- **D8 [VERIFIED — ALREADY MERGED] — `/v1/chat` HTTP+SSE transport is on main and live.** PR #2857 (commit `0b699edf`) merged an 8-route ChatController (CRUD + send/interrupt/approval + SSE) to origin/main; guerrilla answers 401 not 404. **All builders branch off freshly-fetched `origin/main`, never local HEAD** (the shared primary checkout drifts). See D18.
- **D9 [VERIFIED — premise CORRECTED, deferred to P1] — Per-workspace OAuth install + routing.** run-secrets is NOT workspace-scoped — it is a flat instance-global `name→value` KV (`secrets`, PK `:name`), gated global `:require_admin`. Real per-workspace scoping needs a new `workspace_id` column + scoped-admin gating — build work, filed P1 (`task-5766dc5ca985ddc8`), NOT a P2 gate. The `provider_team_id → workspace` map lands with P2/P3 routing (see D29 — the bridge owns it, no Elixir change).
- **D10 [RATIFIED — Wave 2, measured] — The sandbox runner = Vercel Sandbox (Firecracker microVM), snapshot-baked `claude` image + warm pool.** `Port.open` → a sandbox exec on the SAME `{exe,args,env,cwd}→NDJSON` contract. Measured headless: fresh create 7.54s; per-boot npm install 7.50s → per-boot-install DEAD; snapshot bake 5.52s (one-time); create-from-snapshot 3.02s + ~0.6s claude ≈ 3.6s claude-ready. Model first-token in-sandbox is human-gated (D23). Rejected: raw containers. Retained as documented complement: plain Messages-API agent.
- **D11 [RATIFIED] — Notion is OUT.** Not a messaging surface; if ever wanted, it is a Tool (MCP), never a channel.
- **D12 [RATIFIED] — Streaming cadence starts post-once-per-turn** (rate-limit-safe), live-edit-on-throttle is a later refinement. See D26 for the SDK mechanics: there is NO `thread.stream()`; post-once = accumulate the SSE reply and call `thread.post(finalText)` ONCE.
- **D13–D20 — Wave 1 = P0 decisions.** (Migration additive/greenfield; `scope_to_workspace/3` fail-closed semantics; external principal = workspace-bound `ApiToken` with a `chat` permission, NOT airdrop Grant/ClaimFlow; seal at the STORE layer; new `RequireChatAccess` plug resolves `:global` vs `{:workspace, ws}`; mandatory cross-tenant LEAK negative test; hold the line on D10.) SHIPPED grade A- (PR #2857). Full text preserved in the Wave 1 wave-log commits on `loop-epic/connectors-w1-wavelog`.
- **D21 [RATIFIED — Wave 2] — The 10s bar is a Barkpark UX goal, NOT a Linear correctness gate.** Split: (a) **ack-latency ≤10s** = the P2 bridge's job (ack-first, boot-async — a HARD P2 requirement, folded into the turn loop, see W3 Slice 3); (b) real-turn ≤30min = trivially cleared. Barkpark ADDS a self-imposed UX target (first real token ~10s of the ack on the warm path) as a product goal.
- **D22 [RATIFIED — Wave 2] — Warm pool is a UX-latency lever, not a correctness requirement.** Reuse the dwb-10 warm-pool mechanism + a new per-workspace/region shard column. Filed to P1-runner-build backlog.
- **D23 [RATIFIED — Wave 2] — Model first-token is human-gated; name the command, never fabricate.** Env-injection seam PROVEN in-sandbox (no key → `Not logged in`; bogus key → `Invalid API key`). Human-gated: `vercel sandbox exec <vm> --env ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<key> -- bash -lc 'time claude -p "say ok"'`.
- **D24 [RATIFIED — Wave 2] — Reversing `bypassPermissions` for Cloud is a 5-knob build**, not a flag flip (structural unreachability; Cloud `--disallowedTools`; connector-scoped `bp mcp serve --tools <subset>`; connector-grant-scoped token mint; unattended auto-approval policy). Depends on the D9 secrets-scope fix. Filed to P1-runner-build backlog.
- **D25 [VERIFIED — Wave 2] — The runner is a thin `chat`+`workspace_id` HTTP+SSE client; no new Barkpark-side API work.** Proven on origin/main (`chat_controller_test` 42/42; cross-tenant 404 oracle). Minimal credential = the `chat` permission + non-nil `workspace_id`.

## Wave map

- **P0 — Tenant seam + engine seam.** SHIPPED (Wave 1). D7 migration + D8 confirm `/v1/chat`.
- **P1 — Sandboxed tenant runner.** D10 RESOLVED (Wave 2, research + harness). The runner BUILD (`task-fe78ccb664308739`) OPENED Wave 11: execution-profile seam + one-shot sandbox shim + bogus-key isolated-turn proof (D107–D115); Wave 12 = the D24 permission reversal at the ARGV/POLICY layer (D116–D125); the LIVE turn + real deploy stay named human gates.
- **P2 — Provider-agnostic core.** THIS WAVE (Wave 3). The Chat SDK bridge: thread→Session map, streaming glue, connector/OAuth-install abstraction, per-tenant routing — designed now so P3's channels land with ZERO core change. Proven end-to-end with Telegram only.
- **P3a — Easy trio** (Telegram + Slack + Discord). **P3b — Heavy pair** (Teams + WhatsApp). **P3c — iMessage** (self-host only). Studio Connectors catalog UI. **THIS WAVE (Wave 4)** — plus the multi-tenant residue P2 left open (D35–D37) and the first real deploy (D34).
- **P4 — Tool direction + polish.** GitHub/Linear via MCP, permission cards, cross-CHANNEL identity-linking (D31), self-hosted operator profile.

## Wave 3 = P2 — decisions (D26–D33, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand)

The verify fleet scratch-installed `chat@4.34.0` + adapters and read the real `.d.ts`/`.js`, ran
`chat_controller_test` (42/42), and re-checked git/CI freshness. The keystone premise correction:
**there is NO `thread.stream()`.** Everything below is grounded in run output (see the wave Paper
`connectors-wave-3-2026-07-14`). The whole wave is a Node/TypeScript build; P2 ships ZERO `.ex`
changes (D33). The zero-core-change-for-P3 invariant IS the acceptance bar.

- **D26 [DECIDED — keystone SDK correction] — Streaming is `thread.post()`, not `thread.stream()`.** `thread.stream()` does NOT exist (proven: `ThreadImpl` in `messages-BSoJG691.d.ts:468-469` exposes only two `post()` overloads; the only public `stream` symbol is a `StreamingPlan` property). The public overload is `post(message: string | AdapterPostableMessage | AsyncIterable<string> | ChatElement)`. **For D12 post-once-per-turn: accumulate the `/v1/chat` `event:chat` frames into one final assistant string, then call `thread.post(finalText)` exactly ONCE per turn.** The clean turn-end signal is the stream-json `type:"result"` frame (has `.result` + `stop_reason`), NOT `event:exit` (exit = subprocess died; the stream stays open for lazy resume — never treat exit as turn-completion). Pin `chat`, `@chat-adapter/telegram`, `@chat-adapter/state-pg` **all at `4.34.0`** together (they dedupe to a single `chat@4.34.0`).
- **D27 [DECIDED — HOME] — Standalone `connectors/` Node service at repo root** (sibling to `web/`, `api/`, `js/`), `private:true`, its own `package.json` + `package-lock.json` (**npm**, proven installable — 184 pkgs, `tsc --noEmit` + `vitest run` both exit 0 against the real SDK types), its own `vercel.json` mirroring `web/`. NOT a `js/` package (js/ is a changesets + size-limit publish-only SDK monorepo; the bridge is a deployable service, not a published lib). **Kept OUT of the root `pnpm-workspace.yaml`** (which lists only `web` + `js/packages/core`) and **MUST NOT** add a nested `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (it shadows the root and breaks `pnpm install`). Tradeoff accepted: the bridge cannot free-ride `js/test-utils`; it vendors its own `vitest`+`msw` and copies the SSE-mock PATTERN from `js/packages/core/tests/fixtures` (pattern, not module). Keeps P3's zero-core-change promise scoped to files under `connectors/` only.
- **D28 [DECIDED — state] — The `thread_id → session_uuid` map is a BRIDGE-OWNED dedicated table** `chat_bridge.thread_session_map (workspace_id, thread_id, session_uuid, created_at, PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, thread_id))`, queried directly via the bridge's `pg.Pool`. **Do NOT ride `thread.setState`** — it hardcodes a 30-day TTL (`THREAD_STATE_TTL_MS = 30*24*60*60*1e3`, `index.js:510`), so an idle thread silently loses its Session. `@chat-adapter/state-pg` is STILL configured as the SDK's `StateAdapter` for the SDK's own internal bookkeeping (its 5 auto-created tables: subscriptions/locks/cache/lists/queues). Isolation: point state-pg at Barkpark's own Postgres via a `pg.Pool` whose connection pins **`search_path=chat_bridge`** (a dedicated schema), so the SDK's 5 UNQUALIFIED `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` land in `chat_bridge`, never Barkpark's `public`. `keyPrefix` is a ROW column, NOT a schema switch — it does not move the tables. The bridge runs `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS chat_bridge` + its own map/installs DDL on boot (idempotent). NO Ecto migration.
- **D29 [DECIDED — tenant routing] — Bring-your-own-bot per workspace; tenant resolved by CREDENTIAL/inbound-path, not a payload `team_id`.** Telegram has no team/org field in its payload, so the abstraction MUST NOT assume a payload-extractable tenant id. Bridge-owned `chat_bridge.connector_installs (provider, install_key, workspace_id, credential_ref, PRIMARY KEY (provider, install_key))`. Each Connector declares a `tenantResolution` strategy: `credential-bound` (Telegram — the bot token / webhook path IS the tenant binding, resolved BEFORE payload inspection) or `payload-team-id` (Slack — extract `team_id`). `provider_team_id → workspace` is thus ONE strategy among two; P3 Slack lands with zero core change. Reads scope fail-closed (mirror `Content.Scope.scope_to_workspace/3` semantics).
- **D30 [DECIDED — connector abstraction] — A `Connector` is a TS declaration in a plain registry, NOT an Elixir `Barkpark.Plugin`.** Shape: `{ id, direction: 'channel'|'tool'|'both', auth: 'oauth'|'token'|'signing-secret', tenantResolution, adapterFactory(creds) => Adapter, resolveTenant(event, ctx) => workspace_id }`, registered into a `connectorRegistry` map. The SDK's own `new Chat({ adapters: Record<string,Adapter>, state })` shape already delivers "register an adapter, no core change" — adding a P3 channel = one registry entry + its `@chat-adapter/*` dep. The core loop (resolve tenant → resolve-or-mint session → POST turn → stream SSE → post reply ONCE) never changes. Telegram registers as a first-class Connector, identical in shape to every future channel — the smoke adapter is an adapter swap, not a special case.
- **D31 [DECIDED — continuity scope] — This wave delivers per-CHANNEL continuity; cross-CHANNEL identity-linking is deferred to P4.** The thread key is the raw provider thread id namespaced by `(provider, workspace)`: resuming the same Telegram chat returns the same Session. True cross-CHANNEL continuity (one human on Slack + Discord → one Session) requires a cross-provider identity-linking step that does not exist. Corrects the design paper's "cross-surface continuity is free": the Session is reachable by UUID from anywhere, but the AUTOMATIC thread→session mapping is per-channel. Filed as backlog.
- **D32 [DECIDED — deploy shape] — Persistent always-on Node process, NOT Vercel serverless functions.** No `/v1/chat` turn-latency SLA exists to justify the 300–800s Fluid-Compute envelope; P3 Discord needs a persistent Gateway websocket; Telegram `getUpdates` polling wants a long-lived loop. The streaming glue is a long-lived fetch/SSE client loop. A `vercel.json` can deploy webhook-only providers later; P2's core assumes a persistent process. ~~Host is TBD~~ — **host CLOSED by D34 (Wave 4): guerrilla + a Caddy path route.**
- **D33 [DECIDED — zero-Elixir smoke] — The bridge authenticates per-workspace with an EXISTING workspace-bound `chat`-permission `ApiToken`.** `Auth.create_token/5` already mints `["chat"]`+`workspace_id` tokens (proven live in `chat_controller_test.exs:166-167`). NO HTTP mint endpoint is required for P2: the operator mints the smoke token out-of-band (mix/console), or uses the global-admin dev token (→ `:global` chat scope). **P2 ships ZERO `.ex` changes.** Gotcha carried: a `chat` token WITHOUT `workspace_id` is 403 — per-workspace tokens MUST carry `workspace_id`. A self-serve chat-token mint endpoint is optional P3 polish (backlog; if ever built it is an isolated Elixir slice behind the Elixir Test gate).

## Wave 3 slice plan (5 slices, all `builder_model: opus` — Fable is out of credits this run)

All under `connectors/` (greenfield). Slice 1 is GATING (creates the scaffold + shared contract);
2–5 branch off freshly-fetched `origin/main` and rebase onto Slice 1's branch before merge (their
files are disjoint from Slice 1). Proven gate: `cd connectors && npm ci && npm run typecheck && npm test`
(Slice 1 uses `npm install` to create the lockfile). Parent = epic `task-e640bb01fca6ea38`; the P2
rollup task `task-6c358e166b6c7eaf` is extended (not duplicated) to point at these 5 slices.

- **W3-1 — `connectors-p2-scaffold-state` (GATING).** Scaffold + isolated `pg.Pool` (`search_path=chat_bridge`, `CREATE SCHEMA`, bootstrap DDL for `thread_session_map` AND `connector_installs`) + `state-pg` StateAdapter wiring + the `resolveOrMint`/get/put map module + the `ChatClient` INTERFACE (typed contract slices 2–5 consume). Files: `connectors/package.json`, `connectors/tsconfig.json`, `connectors/vitest.config.ts`, `connectors/vercel.json`, `connectors/.gitignore`, `connectors/README.md`, `connectors/src/config.ts`, `connectors/src/db/pool.ts`, `connectors/src/db/schema.ts`, `connectors/src/state/state-adapter.ts`, `connectors/src/state/thread-session-map.ts`, `connectors/src/chat-client/types.ts`, `connectors/test/thread-session-map.test.ts`.
- **W3-2 — `connectors-p2-chat-client-sse` (⇐ W3-1).** Implement `ChatClient` (createSession/getSession/postMessage) + the SSE reader (fetch + `getReader()` + byte-exact frame parser for `message`/`chat`/`permission`/`exit` + `: keepalive` tolerance, `Last-Event-ID` resume, 401/403 no-retry). Files: `connectors/src/chat-client/client.ts`, `connectors/src/chat-client/sse.ts`, `connectors/test/sse.test.ts`, `connectors/test/fixtures/` (recorded frames copied from `api/test/fixtures/claude_chat`).
- **W3-3 — `connectors-p2-turn-loop` (⇐ W3-1, W3-2).** The per-turn glue: ack-first within ≤10s (D21, canned ack, boot async), consume the SSE stream, accumulate `event:chat` into the final assistant text, complete on stream-json `type:"result"` (NOT `event:exit`), call `thread.post(finalText)` ONCE (D12/D26). Files: `connectors/src/turn/turn-loop.ts`, `connectors/test/turn-loop.test.ts`.
- **W3-4 — `connectors-p2-connector-tenant` (⇐ W3-1).** The `Connector` type + `connectorRegistry` + `tenantResolution` strategies (`credential-bound` + `payload-team-id` declared) + `connector_installs` query + `resolveTenant`. The zero-core-change P3 seam. Files: `connectors/src/connector/types.ts`, `connectors/src/connector/registry.ts`, `connectors/src/tenant/installs.ts`, `connectors/src/tenant/resolve.ts`, `connectors/test/connector-registry.test.ts`, `connectors/test/tenant.test.ts`.
- **W3-5 — `connectors-p2-telegram-smoke` (⇐ W3-1..4).** Register `createTelegramAdapter` as a first-class Connector (`credential-bound`), assemble `new Chat({ adapters:{telegram}, state })`, wire `core/dispatch` (inbound → resolveTenant → map → turn-loop → reply), the runnable polling smoke harness (`TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` + `BARKPARK_CHAT_TOKEN` human-gated), and the honest human-gate doc. Files: `connectors/src/connectors/telegram.ts`, `connectors/src/core/dispatch.ts`, `connectors/src/index.ts`, `connectors/scripts/smoke-telegram.ts`, `connectors/docs/telegram-smoke.md`, `connectors/test/telegram-connector.test.ts`, `connectors/test/dispatch.test.ts`.

## Builder discipline

- Every builder isolates in a **worktree**, branches off **freshly-fetched `origin/main`** (`git fetch origin main` first — the shared primary checkout drifts, ~9 ahead/7 behind; never branch off local HEAD). Slices 2–5 rebase onto W3-1's branch before merge.
- P2 is Node/TypeScript only — no `.ex` change is expected. IF a slice ever adds one (it should not), it **WAITS for the Elixir Test gate** (`[[dont-merge-before-elixir-test]]`) and is its own isolated slice.
- **ONE pre-existing main-wide red, not two** (corrected at Wave 4): `task-9ad2d5988f7ca274` = stale `.sobelow-skips` `media.ex` line drift, and sobelow is ADVISORY (`continue-on-error: true`). **`docs/openapi.json` drift is NOT currently red** — `mix barkpark.openapi --check` exits 0 on origin/main, and the drift check is a step inside the REQUIRED Elixir Test job. Do not assume "openapi is already red, mine doesn't matter" — that merges real drift.
- **Distrust vacuous green** (`[[distrust-vacuous-green]]`): the map's mint-once/resume-after and the turn loop's post-ONCE must be proven by assertion, not asserted by prose. Live Telegram round-trip is an honest human gate (real bot token + running `/v1/chat`), delivered as a runnable harness + unit tests against a faithful recorded-SSE mock — never a fabricated "it works".
- Wire contract for `/v1/chat`, the SSE frame shapes, and the state/isolation model are FIXED in this charter (D26/D28) — parallel builders share one written truth.
- The wave Paper (`connectors-wave-3-2026-07-14`) is the live spine: workers report `coverage[]`, stamp evidence as they go.

## Wave 4 = P3 — decisions (D34–D47, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand)

Every decision below is grounded in run output recorded in the wave Paper
`connectors-wave-4-2026-07-14`. Three premises the strategy carried are **CORRECTED** here:
(a) `docs/openapi.json` drift is **NOT** a pre-existing main-wide red — `mix barkpark.openapi --check`
exits **0** on origin/main (only the `.sobelow-skips` red is real); (b) the SDK's `Adapter` interface
already **mandates** `handleWebhook(Request) => Response` and `Chat.webhooks` is a typed map — the
missing half of L4 is the *runtime* demux, not the adapter contract; (c) `@chat-adapter/shared`'s
`encryptToken` is **unfit** for our credential column (no AAD — a stolen row replays verbatim).

- **D34 [DECIDED — HOST, closes D32] — Persistent Node process on guerrilla, behind a Caddy PATH route. No DNS human gate.** Proven live: `curl https://guerrilla.barkpark.cloud/mcp` → `HTTP/2 405 · via: 1.1 Caddy` (the MCP process answering through public TLS on a loopback port). `dig connectors.barkpark.cloud` and a random subdomain are BOTH empty ⇒ **there is no wildcard DNS**, so a SUBDOMAIN would re-arm the separate barkpark.cloud Hetzner-DNS-token human gate for nothing. Shape: systemd `barkpark-connectors.service` (clone `barkpark-mcp.service` — a PLAIN unit, never the `barkpark-slot@` template) on loopback **:4020** (proven free + bindable; outside the blue/green slot ports {4000,4001} so the port-flip `sed` passes over it), armed by a new `arm_caddy_connectors_route()` cloned verbatim from `arm_caddy_mcp_route()` (marker guard / backup / `caddy validate` / auto-revert / never fail the deploy). Public URLs, zero DNS work: `https://guerrilla.barkpark.cloud/connectors/…`. **Caddy `handle` does NOT strip the prefix** — the bridge's own router owns the full `/connectors/...` path (`CONNECTORS_PATH_PREFIX`), or every webhook silently 404s. **`node` is NOT on the box's PATH** (asdf nodejs 26.5.0; bare `node -v` → command not found) — ExecStart and every deploy step use an ABSOLUTE path resolved via `asdf where nodejs`, and `nodejs` gets pinned in `.tool-versions`. Secrets ride `/etc/barkpark/connectors.env` at mode **0600** (NOT `mcp.env`'s 0644 — that is a deliberate no-secret exception). **Order matters: bring the unit up BEFORE registering any provider URL** — until then the path lands on Caddy's maintenance 503 and Slack/Meta disable a webhook after repeated non-2xx.
- **D35 [DECIDED — THE HONESTY GATE] — Per-install Barkpark chat token. `BARKPARK_CHAT_TOKEN` is DELETED from the request path.** Today `chatClientFor(_workspaceId)` ignores its argument and every tenant rides one operator token; because that token carries `admin`, `RequireChatAccess` resolves it to `:global` and `StudioChat` stamps `owner_workspace_id = NULL` — so every session the bridge has EVER created is **tenant-less**, not merely cross-readable, and any other `:global` caller reads all of them. Fix: `connector_installs` gains `chat_token_ref` (sealed, D37); the turn loop uses **that install's** token. The bridge-side routing key and the server-side token scope then agree **by construction** (both derive from the same install row), not by hope. **Fail-closed**: an install with no chat token cannot dispatch — a typed drop, never a fallback to an operator token.
- **D36 [DECIDED — mint] — A NEW admin-gated `ChatTokenController`, `@permissions ["chat"]` HARDCODED. Never widen `TokenController`.** `POST /w/:ws/p/:proj/v1/chat/tokens`, `:scoped_admin`. `Auth.create_token/5` already mints `["chat"]`+`workspace_id` at the data layer (no permission enum exists). The body must NEVER influence `permissions`: `chat_scope/1` checks `admin` **FIRST** → `:global` → `owner_workspace_id = NULL`, so a single `"admin"` entry silently re-opens this wave's headline bug (proven: mutating the constant to `["chat","admin"]` reds 4/5 tests with `expected owner_workspace_id=…, got nil` and `ws_A token READ ws_B's session — CROSS-TENANT LEAK`). Two load-bearing mechanics, both proven: **(i) the router `scope` block goes BELOW `router.ex` line 536** (use ~1954, beside the existing `/v1/tokens` mint) — `.sobelow-skips` is LINE-KEYED and inserting above shifts 11 entries, manufacturing fresh findings (12 → 23); **(ii) HTTP-only, no `capabilities.ex` entry** — `mix barkpark.openapi` transforms the hand-written manifest and never reads the Phoenix router, so the route produces ZERO openapi drift (regen byte-identical, `--check` exit 0). Cost accepted and filed: the endpoint is invisible to `/v1/capabilities`, `bp`, the SDK and MCP until a `core_cmd` entry lands (which then MANDATES a spec regen + commit). Local builds need `CC=/usr/bin/clang`.
- **D37 [DECIDED — crypto] — AES-256-GCM via `node:crypto` with a ROW-BOUND AAD. REJECT `@chat-adapter/shared`'s `encryptToken`.** It round-trips but calls `createCipheriv` and **never `setAAD`** — proven: a ciphertext minted for tenant A decrypts cleanly when pasted into any other install's row. This is not a fifth crypto path: `FieldCipher` already says verbatim *"AES-256-GCM with a fresh 12-byte IV per call; the scope is the AAD"* — D37 is Barkpark's own scheme, in the language that owns the column. Wire = `Base64(iv(12) ‖ tag(16) ‖ ct)` (byte-identical to `LocalKek`/cloud `Registry.Vault`; Node→Elixir interop proven, though **no Elixir decrypt is ever needed** — D38). AAD = `"bpc1|" + JSON.stringify([provider, install_key, workspace_id])` — the PK alone is insufficient (flipping `workspace_id` keeps the PK intact and would still decrypt). Key = a NEW independent `CONNECTORS_CREDENTIAL_KEY` (base64 32B, `required()` at boot — a missing key is a boot failure, never a plaintext fallback), plus optional `CONNECTORS_CREDENTIAL_KEY_PREVIOUS` for rotation. Seals BOTH `credential_ref` (provider secret) and `chat_token_ref`. Zero new deps. **Named residual risk (do not overclaim):** one service-wide key protects every tenant — a DB dump alone cannot impersonate a tenant bot (the actual property), but a key leak compromises all tenants; per-workspace DEKs are backlog. The task's "blocked on D9 run-secrets" framing is **STALE** — `Barkpark.Secrets` is a flat instance-global `name→value` KV with no `workspace_id`, and reading it from Node would need an ADMIN token: the exact anti-pattern this wave exists to kill.
- **D38 [DECIDED — answers D27's open question] — No Elixir will ever decrypt a connector credential.** `grep chat_bridge api/lib` → zero. `@schema_prefix` in `api/lib` → zero. Routing reads `workspace_id` ALONE (`SELECT_WORKSPACE`); only MOUNTING an adapter needs the secret. The Studio catalog lists `provider/install_key/workspace_id/created_at` and never the secret. The bridge stays Elixir-independent at runtime.
- **D39 [DECIDED — THE WEBHOOK SEAM] — ONE generic, dependency-free, fail-closed HTTP seam, keyed by the INSTALL ROW. Zero core-loop change (proven by spike: 21 tests, `git diff --stat HEAD` empty).** `connectors/src/http/webhook-server.ts` (`node:http` + global `Request`/`Response`; no express/fastify). Order: parse route (no body read) → `lookupInstall(provider, installKey)` → null ⇒ **opaque 404** → `mounts.handlerFor(install)` → undefined ⇒ the SAME opaque 404 (not an install-enumeration oracle) → buffer the body ONCE → rebuild a `Request` → **that install's OWN `chat.webhooks[provider](request)`**. `handlerFor` takes a DB ROW, so the cross-tenant leak is **unrepresentable**, not merely untaken. Six rules, each learned from a proof: **(1)** drive `chat.webhooks[provider]`, NEVER `adapter.handleWebhook` — Teams 500s `"No handler registered"` until `App.initialize()` runs, and `chat.webhooks` auto-initializes. **(2)** `waitUntil` is MANDATORY: without it the response blocks on the whole turn → Slack's 3s ack expires → the provider RETRIES → the turn runs TWICE (breaking D12 post-once). In a persistent process `waitUntil` = "don't await". **(3)** `allowedMethods = ["GET","POST"]` — Meta/WhatsApp's verification handshake is a **GET** (`hub.challenge`); POST-only breaks WhatsApp onboarding outright. Generic allowlist, never a provider branch. **(4)** the body is SINGLE-READ: any demux peek must `request.clone()` — a pre-read `Request` throws `Body is unusable`, which the Slack adapter catches and turns into a **misleading 401 "Invalid signature"**. **(5)** on body-cap overflow NEVER `req.destroy()` — it tears down the socket so the 413 never reaches the provider (reject an over-cap `content-length` before reading; drain-without-accumulating for chunked). **(6)** `publicBaseUrl` — behind Caddy the socket speaks plain http; adapters validating audience/URL (Teams' Bot Framework JWT) need the PUBLIC https URL in the reconstructed Request. Two demux key sources, ONE generic `Connector.webhook?: { keySource: 'path' | 'payload'; extractInstallKey?(rawBody, headers) }` member: **path** (`/connectors/webhooks/:provider/:installKey` — BYO-app providers that register their own URL: WhatsApp, Telegram-webhook) and **payload** (`/connectors/webhooks/:provider` — providers whose request_url is app-wide: Slack `team_id`, Teams tenant id). Demux-before-verify is SAFE: the key only SELECTS which secret to verify against, and a forged key is caught cryptographically (proven: an A-signed body handed to B's adapter → 401). **A connector's `extractInstallKey` and its `resolveTenant` extractor MUST be the same function** — Teams reads `conversation.tenantId ?? channelData.tenant.id`, and two independent derivations with different precedence is a confused-deputy vector. **Dynamic mount/unmount ships WITH the seam** (the spike's one real gap): the mount index is a boot snapshot, so a freshly-OAuth'd install would 404 until restart — which is precisely the two-minute north star. Path traversal must 404 on a LITERAL dot-segment target — and note `fetch` normalises it client-side, so a fetch-based traversal test is **vacuous**; use a raw socket.
- **D40 [DECIDED — Slack] — Webhook + OAuth (Add-to-Slack). Socket Mode is DISQUALIFIED for Cloud multi-tenant.** Not "worse" — structurally impossible, and it fails SILENTLY: `createSlackAdapter({mode:'socket', clientId…})` throws `Multi-workspace (clientId/clientSecret) is not supported in socket mode`, but the guard does **not** check `installationProvider` — so `socket + installationProvider + a static botToken` constructs happily and makes **both tenants reply with the SAME operator token while `getInstallation` is NEVER called** (proven). That is this wave's headline bug, in a second channel, with green tests. Ruling: `mode:"webhook"`, app-wide `signingSecret` + `clientId`/`clientSecret`, per-install token via `installationProvider.getInstallation` reading `connector_installs` (decrypted with D37). **NEVER pass `encryptionKey`, NEVER call `setInstallation`** — those write the bot token into a SECOND store (the state-pg tables) with the no-AAD blob. `resolveTokenForTeam` prefers `installationProvider` and returns `null` on a miss (fail-closed, no fallback). Use the SDK's exported `readSlackWebhook` (verify+parse, returns a typed `teamId`) — do not hand-roll. **Enterprise Grid: `install_key = enterprise_id ?? team_id`** (when `is_enterprise_install`, the key is the enterprise id).
- **D41 [DECIDED — Discord] — Gateway-only v1, `credential-bound` BYO-bot. No webhook-seam dependency.** `DiscordAdapterConfig` takes ONE static `botToken` with no `installationProvider`, so a shared-app + `guild_id` design would resurrect this wave's headline bug for a second channel. Each workspace creates its own Discord Application + bot token — Telegram-weight onboarding, **honestly named as NOT one-click**. `startGatewayListener` is **non-blocking by design** (proven: resolves 200 in 28ms; it hands the connection promise to `waitUntil` and returns synchronously) — it will NOT stall the sequential mount loop. Two traps: it **500s same-tick** if `waitUntil` is omitted or `chat.initialize()` hasn't run (looks like "nothing happened"), and the default `durationMs = 180_000` calls `client.destroy()` after 3 minutes — in a persistent process pass a very large `durationMs` and wire `stopListening` to the AbortController. Slash commands (which WOULD need the Ed25519 webhook path) are out of v1.
- **D42 [DECIDED — Teams] — ONE operator Azure app (`appType: "MultiTenant"`), payload-tenant routing, NO per-workspace provider credential.** `TeamsAdapterConfig` has no `installationProvider` and no per-tenant credential map; tenancy arrives in the payload (`activity.conversation.tenantId ?? activity.channelData.tenant.id`). **The Teams slice must NOT copy Slack's OAuth machinery** — it would add a credential surface for nothing. `connector_installs.credential_ref` is already NULLABLE, so a credential-less Teams install needs zero schema change; `install_key` = the Microsoft tenant id; `chat_token_ref` is still required (D35). Teams' `threadId` carries **no** tenant id (`teams:<b64(conversationId)>:<b64(serviceUrl)>`) — harmless only because `thread_session_map`'s PK is composite `(workspace_id, thread_id)`; never collapse it. Human gates: Azure Bot registration (appId + appPassword) + the messaging endpoint (now a REAL URL: `https://guerrilla.barkpark.cloud/connectors/webhooks/teams`) + **per-customer-org Teams admin consent, which Barkpark can never perform on a customer's behalf.** Runbook exists (`docs/ops/teams-azure-bot.md`) — its endpoint must be updated to the real URL.
- **D43 [DECIDED — WhatsApp] — `credential-bound` BYO Meta app; the 24h window is 100% OUR code, encoded as connector POLICY.** All four creds are per-install (`accessToken`, `appSecret`, `phoneNumberId`, `verifyToken`) ⇒ path-keyed webhook, `install_key = phone_number_id` (`entry[].changes[].value.metadata.phone_number_id`). The adapter's own doc says it will **not** auto-substitute templates — so the bridge tracks last-inbound-at per `(workspace_id, thread_id)` and, outside the window, refuses the free-form post and either calls `sendTemplate` or returns a typed `outside-24h-window` drop. A pure, unit-testable policy function — never a runtime discovery. `docs/ops/whatsapp-meta.md` does NOT exist and must be written (unlike Teams). Human gates: Meta Business verification → WABA phone number → System User token → app secret → **App Review for Advanced Access** (without it the number is capped to ~5 test recipients) → per-template approval.
- **D44 [DECIDED — iMessage] — a self-hosted-PROFILE registry entry, OFF by default, never Cloud.** No official adapter exists (`@chat-adapter/imessage` → npm 404; the whole `@chat-adapter/*` scope has none). The community `chat-adapter-imessage` **does** conform: `declare class iMessageAdapter implements Adapter`, and a probe assigning it to `Adapter<any,any>` type-checks with **0 errors** under `skipLibCheck: true` — which `connectors/tsconfig.json` already commits, so the 7 errors seen under `--skipLibCheck false` (all inside a transitive dep's own `.d.ts`, referencing an undeclared `hotscript` module) can never surface in our build. Ship: one registry entry, `tenantResolution: 'credential-bound'`, refused unless `CONNECTORS_PROFILE=self-hosted`, plus a doc that says loudly: needs a dedicated Mac + Full Disk Access + an Apple ID relay identity, ONE workspace per Mac, unofficial and breakable by any macOS update. **The adapter's "Spectrum Cloud" SaaS mode is FORBIDDEN** — it does not remove the Mac, it moves it to a third-party vendor holding real Apple credentials. That is a trust decision Barkpark has not made and D3 deliberately avoids.
- **D45 [DECIDED — CI] — `connectors.yml`, BLOCKING, with a Postgres service, `CONNECTORS_REQUIRE_DB=1`, AND a zero-skip assertion.** The vacuous green is sitting in the repo right now: with an unreachable Postgres and no flag, `npx vitest run` exits **0** and prints `Test Files 8 passed (8)` while silently skipping all 15 DB tests (`describe.skipIf`). Worse, the protective proof (stripping the `search_path` pin) surfaces as a SUITE-level FAIL **plus a skip-count anomaly**, not per-test reds. So the gate must assert on the **skip count**, not just the exit code. Zero of 29 workflows touch `connectors/` today.
- **D46 [DECIDED — deploy trigger] — merging `connectors/**` deploys NOTHING today.** `deploy.yml`'s `on.push.paths` is `cloud/api/internal/deploy` only and the instance job gates on `grep -qE '^(api|internal|deploy)/'`. Both must gain `connectors`, or the whole of D34 never runs. (`deploy/**` changes self-trigger, so the deploy slice bootstraps itself.) `deploy/instance-deploy_test.sh` is a PR gate — the new arm-function must keep it green (dry-run: `ALL PASS`).
- **D47 [DECIDED — Studio catalog, deferred to the NEXT wave but ruled NOW — W33 ANNOTATION: since SHIPPED. Wave 5 PR #3213 built the catalog + connect loop (`connectors_live.ex`, moduledoc "the catalog, and THE CONNECT LOOP (Connectors D47–D55)", at the D49 scoped route); Wave 10 PR #3429 extended it (tool cards); hardened W17/W19/W26. The "deferred" clause is history — the ruling below still stands] — Elixir READS `chat_bridge` via `@schema_prefix`; the bridge stays the only WRITER.** Mechanically proven (and it would be the FIRST `@schema_prefix` in the codebase — zero prior usage). Two hard rules a builder must not rediscover: **(a)** a non-superuser Postgres role gets `ERROR: permission denied for schema chat_bridge` — an explicit `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA chat_bridge` + `GRANT SELECT ON chat_bridge.connector_installs` deploy step must ship with it (no such step exists anywhere in the repo, and prod's DB role is unverified); **(b)** `connector_installs.workspace_id` is Postgres **TEXT** while `Workspace.id` is `:binary_id` — an uncast join raises `operator does not exist: uuid = text`; every compare needs `type(ci.workspace_id, Ecto.UUID)`. Install CREATION goes through the bridge's OAuth callback, never Studio.

## Wave 4 slice plan (8 slices, all `builder_model: opus` — Fable is out of credits this run)

Gate for every `connectors/` slice (dry-run PASSES on origin/main — 118/118):
`cd connectors && npm ci && npm run typecheck && CONNECTORS_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://postgres@localhost:5432/postgres CONNECTORS_REQUIRE_DB=1 npx vitest run`.
**W4-1 is GATING for W4-2/6/7; W4-2 is GATING for W4-6/8.** Publish the gating branch BEFORE dependents
start — this is the direct fix for W3's three-way contract fork. Parent = `task-e640bb01fca6ea38`.

- **W4-1 — `connectors-p3-multitenant-close` (GATING, the honesty gate).** D35+D37: `chat_token_ref` column + AAD-bound cipher + install write path + per-install `chatClientFor` + fail-closed drop + the BRIDGE half of the two-layer cross-tenant negative test.
- **W4-2 — `connectors-p3-webhook-seam` (⇐ W4-1, GATING for the webhook channels).** D39: the generic fail-closed demux + dynamic mount/unmount + `Connector.webhook?`.
- **W4-3 — `connectors-p3-chat-token-mint-endpoint` (Elixir, isolated, WAITS for the Elixir Test gate).** D36.
- **W4-4 — `connectors-ci-postgres-gate`.** D45.
- **W4-5 — `connectors-p3-deploy-guerrilla`.** D34+D46.
- **W4-6 — `connectors-p3-slack` (⇐ W4-2).** D40. The one-registry-entry diff is itself the proof.
- **W4-7 — `connectors-p3-discord-imessage` (⇐ W4-1).** D41+D44. The two socket/local channels; no seam dependency.
- **W4-8 — `connectors-p3-teams-whatsapp` (⇐ W4-2).** D42+D43. Code + runnable harness + the EXACT named human gate — never a fabricated live install.

## Wave 5 = P4 — decisions (D48–D60, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand)

Every decision is grounded in run output recorded in `connectors-wave-5-2026-07-14`. Two premises the
strategy carried are **CORRECTED**: (a) the connect-ticket-as-bridge-authority lean is **not
buildable** (`Auth.verify_token/1` filters `kind == "api"` — a ticket can never be a Bearer); (b) the
GRANT mandate in D47(a) is a **no-op on every path we run** — the real blocker is a missing TABLE.

- **D48 [DECIDED — mint authority] — Studio mints IN-PROCESS through ONE shared context fn. A new ticket-auth plug is REJECTED.** The mint route is `[:scoped_api, :scoped_admin]` = pure Bearer + `RequireWorkspaceRole` (membership `owner|admin` in THAT workspace; a global-`admin` token with no membership row is a flat 403, pinned). `verify_token/1` is `where kind == "api"` (`auth.ex:44-46`) — the fail-closed ticket-key boundary every consumer rides — so option (A) would widen the auth choke point for nothing. The bridge holds no Barkpark credential (BridgeConfig has no token field) and under (B) never needs one. **Trap, proven and load-bearing:** `Auth.create_token/5` does **NOT** hardcode `["chat"]` — the hardcode is a CONTROLLER constant (`chat_token_controller.ex:45`); called in-process with `["read","write","admin"]` it mints exactly that, and `role_for_permissions/1` escalates the membership row to **admin** (a token that can mint more tokens). So the ONLY safe shape is `Barkpark.Auth.create_chat_token(label, dataset, workspace_id)` — the `["chat"]` literal in ONE place — with `ChatTokenController` **delegating to it**. D47 holds intact: Studio writes `api_tokens` (Barkpark's own table); the bridge is still the only writer of `chat_bridge`.
- **D49 [DECIDED — the gate delta] — Studio's mount gate is NOT the mint's gate. RE-GATE on `workspace_admin?/2`, fail-closed, for BOTH principal kinds.** Proven: a token that passes `LiveAuth(:admin)` + `LiveScope(:resolve)` — i.e. mounts the catalog — reports `has_permission?(admin)=true`, `workspace_admin?=false`, `authorize(:read)=:ok`, and is a 403 on the HTTP mint. An in-process mint without a re-check is a privilege escalation relative to the endpoint. `LiveAuth` assigns EITHER `:api_token` (`%ApiToken{}`) or `:current_user` (`%User{}`) — both work through `TenancyAuth.workspace_admin?/2`, and the handler must accept both. **The catalog MUST live on the SCOPED route** `/w/:ws/p/:proj/studio/connectors` (`live_session :scoped_admin_studio`, router.ex ~1091-1104): the flat `/studio/*` session has no `LiveScope` hook and therefore no `current_workspace` — filing it flat would pin every install to the seeded Default workspace (the exact bug that moved `/studio/settings` to the scoped spelling).
- **D50 [DECIDED — THE CONNECT SEAM] — a signed CONNECT TICKET authorizes; the raw chat token rides the LOOPBACK, never the OAuth `state`.** The primitive already exists and is wired to nothing: `slack-oauth.ts`'s `signOAuthState`/`verifyOAuthState` (HMAC-SHA256, 10-min TTL, length-check before `timingSafeEqual`, verified BEFORE the code is spent) has **zero HTTP call sites** — the bridge serves only `/health` and `/webhooks/:provider[/:key]`. Generalise it into `connectors/src/connect/ticket.ts`. **Wire, FIXED here so two builders build apart:** payload JSON in EXACT key order `{"w":<workspace uuid>,"p":<provider>,"n":<nonce>,"t":<issued_at_ms>}` (no spaces) → `ticket = base64url(json) "." base64url(HMAC-SHA256(secret, base64url(json)))`, no padding; TTL 600 000 ms, future skew ≤ 30 000 ms. **Golden vector** (secret `golden-connect-secret`, ws `11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555`, provider `telegram`, nonce `0123456789abcdef`, t `1752480000000`) — pinned by a test on BOTH sides, or a silent cross-language mismatch 401s every connect: `eyJ3IjoiMTExMTExMTEtMjIyMi0zMzMzLTQ0NDQtNTU1NTU1NTU1NTU1IiwicCI6InRlbGVncmFtIiwibiI6IjAxMjM0NTY3ODlhYmNkZWYiLCJ0IjoxNzUyNDgwMDAwMDAwfQ.lKEgXtxTstQoQlBxCdvvPhY23Cq0reI9BHzzPzpdxO0`. The raw chat token NEVER rides the ticket: the state payload is base64url JSON and **deliberately readable** ("it is not a secret — it is a workspace id the operator already knows") and `state` traverses Slack's servers, the URL bar and every proxy log. It rides the POST body over **loopback** — free, because the bridge listens on `127.0.0.1:4020` on the SAME box as the BEAM. **Loopback-only by construction:** the connect routes 404 (opaquely) on any request carrying `x-forwarded-for`/`x-forwarded-host`, even with a valid ticket — Caddy always appends them, so the public path can never reach them. **Secret custody:** `CONNECTORS_CONNECT_SECRET` (bridge) == `:connect_secret` (Elixir), generated once by `instance-deploy.sh` (like `CONNECTORS_CREDENTIAL_KEY`) into `/opt/barkpark/.env` and copied into `/etc/barkpark/connectors.env`. It is **OPTIONAL at boot**: absent ⇒ the connect routes are NOT MOUNTED (loud log) — never a boot failure, never an unauthenticated route. That also removes the merge-order hazard between the bridge slice and the deploy step.
- **D51 [DECIDED — two-step connect] — VALIDATE (no write) → CONNECT (one write) → DISCONNECT (unmount + DELETE + revoke).** `Connector` gains ONE optional member: `connect?: { mode: "paste"; credentialLabel: string; helpUrl?: string; validate(credential): Promise<{ok:true; installKey; displayName} | {ok:false; reason}> }` — Telegram validates via `getMe`, Discord via `GET /users/@me`. A provider WITHOUT `connect` is catalog-visible but not connectable (Slack = OAuth, backlogged; Teams = per-org admin consent; iMessage = self-hosted). Validate-first exists for two reasons, both load-bearing: **(i)** a bad/revoked token is refused at paste time instead of at the next boot (D53); **(ii)** Studio learns `install_key` BEFORE it mints, so the chat token's label can be `connector:<provider>:<install_key>` — which is the ONLY thing that makes DISCONNECT able to revoke it (`Auth.revoke_token/1` exists; the bridge holds no token id). Routes: `POST {prefix}/connect/validate`, `POST {prefix}/connect`, `POST {prefix}/disconnect`. Connect = `upsertInstall` (both secrets, ONE write) → `bridge.addInstall` (live mount, **no restart**). Disconnect = ticket-workspace-checked lookup (a cross-tenant disconnect is an opaque 404) → unmount → `deleteInstall` → Studio revokes the labelled token.
- **D52 [DECIDED — the install write path] — preserve siblings, refuse cross-tenant carry, and be able to DELETE.** `upsertInstall`'s `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` sets all three columns unconditionally and `isBlankSecret(undefined)` is true, so the Slack OAuth callback (which builds its install with NO `chatToken` key — the ONLY production caller) silently **wipes `chat_token_ref` to NULL** on re-auth: routing keeps working, the agent goes unreachable, nothing errors. **REJECT COALESCE** (proven *worse than the bug*: on a workspace change it retains a blob bound to the OLD workspace → un-openable → `toInstall()` fails closed → the ENTIRE install disappears, an unrecoverable ciphertext replacing a recoverable NULL). **REJECT the filed task's criterion 3** ("changing workspace_id still RE-SEALS both blobs"): re-sealing a workspace-bound chat token under B's AAD hands B a token that **authenticates as A** — D35's headline bug rebuilt inside the patch, with the AAD defence disabled because we re-sealed it ourselves. The answer is a targeted conditional preserve — preservation is safe exactly where it is possible (AAD unchanged), so the two constraints collapse onto one predicate and it is ONE atomic statement (no decrypt, no read-modify-write, no lost-update race): `credential_ref = CASE WHEN EXCLUDED.credential_ref IS NOT NULL THEN EXCLUDED.credential_ref WHEN connector_installs.workspace_id = EXCLUDED.workspace_id THEN connector_installs.credential_ref ELSE NULL END` (symmetric for `chat_token_ref`). Consequences: `credentialRef` becomes OPTIONAL in the TYPE (today it is a required key, so a caller literally cannot say "don't touch it"), SQL NULL now means PRESERVE — and therefore **revoke needs its own verb**: `deleteInstall(provider, install_key)`. There is **no SQL DELETE anywhere in the bridge today** — `removeInstall` → `takeDown` is purely in-memory, so a disconnect is undone by the next restart.
- **D53 [DECIDED — blast radius] — ONE BAD TOKEN MUST NOT TAKE THE FLEET DOWN.** Proven with the real `startBridge`: `bringUp` awaits `chat.initialize()` and there is **zero try/catch anywhere in `startBridge`** (index.ts 340-470); a revoked Telegram token throws `AuthenticationError` out of `initialize()`, rejects `startBridge`, `process.exit(1)` → systemd `Restart=on-failure` crash-loops → `instance-deploy.sh` runs `systemctl disable --now barkpark-connectors` → `/connectors` is the maintenance **503 for every tenant**. `listInstalls` is `ORDER BY install_key`, so the lowest-sorting bad row poisons every later install (proved with two tenants: the healthy one never mounted). This ships WITH the connect loop, not as hardening: paste-a-token makes bad tokens routine, and it fails at the NEXT RESTART, not at paste time. Fix: per-install try/catch at boot — log, skip that install, keep serving everyone else; a bridge with zero healthy installs still comes up and still answers `/health`.
- **D54 [DECIDED — corrects D47(a)] — The GRANT is a NO-OP on every path we run; the REAL blocker was the missing TABLE.** Proven: prod's Repo role **owns** `chat_bridge` (the bridge ran `CREATE SCHEMA` at its own boot), GitHub CI runs Postgres as **superuser** (both `elixir.yml:97` and `connectors.yml:58`), local is superuser, and the prod-host test role owns its own DB and self-owns any schema it creates (`USAGE=true`, no grant). `barkpark_test_runner` appears **zero times in the repo**. What a builder actually hits is `ERROR 42P01 (undefined_table)` — **no Ecto migration creates `chat_bridge`** (D28 forbids one), so the Elixir TEST DB has never had the table and an `@schema_prefix` read has no fixture path. **The recipe, proven from a genuinely absent schema with ZERO per-file fixture:** `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS chat_bridge` + `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chat_bridge.connector_installs (…)` in `api/test/test_helper.exs`, through **`Repo`** (so the test role owns it — a raw Postgrex conn re-introduces the creator≠reader split), **before** `Sandbox.mode(:manual)`. The DataCase sandbox rolls back across the non-public prefix. Ship the GRANT anyway as one **guarded, non-fatal** line in `instance-deploy.sh`'s connectors block, after the health gate (no-op for an owner; load-bearing the day a second role appears) — but never budget it as the blocker. **Named cost:** the fixture TRANSCRIBES `connectors/src/db/schema.ts:67-76` into Elixir — a second source of truth for bridge-owned DDL, which has already drifted once (`ADD_CHAT_TOKEN_REF_SQL` exists precisely because `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` was a no-op against the older 4-column table). Mitigation is a criterion (cross-referenced comments in BOTH files + a test pinning the exact column set the catalog reads); a real drift gate is backlog.
- **D55 [CONFIRMED — D47(b) holds] — every `workspace_id` compare needs `type(ci.workspace_id, Ecto.UUID)`.** Proven both ways: the cast renders `c0."workspace_id"::uuid` and joins green; without it, `ERROR 42883 operator does not exist: uuid = text`.
- **D56 [DECIDED — cross-epic, the ledger's integrity] — the stamp guard FAILS CLOSED on `--met`; indexing stays 0-based.** Root cause is neither of the bug task's two hypotheses. cf48aeeb7 (#3064) IS on origin/main and IS live on guerrilla — proven by a live probe on a scratch task: a wrong-index stamp WITH `--criterion-text` is **REJECTED** (`criteria_mismatch`, exit 2, no write); the SAME wrong-index stamp WITHOUT the text is **silently applied** (`ok:true`, exit 0). The guard is opt-in **by design** ("an unguarded stamp stays permissive so #3039's index-only autostamp is untouched") and nothing forces the flag. Fix: a criteria update flipping `met: true` with NO `"criterion"` guard is `{:error, :criterion_text_required}` (`--miss` stays permissive — it flips nothing). **MANDATORY companion in the SAME PR:** `close.ex`'s `autostamp_merge_gate/6` builds `%{"index"=>i,"met"=>true,"evidence"=>…}` with no `"criterion"` key (the `entry` that holds the text is bound and discarded as `_entry` at :346) — thread the stored text in, or fail-closed 409s #3039's autostamp the day it ships. NOT the 1-based flip: cf48aeeb7 just rewrote three surfaces to teach 0-based forcefully, and the guard is what makes the index safe.
- **D57 [DECIDED — hardening is ONE live item, not four] — time bound YES; fanout, poll-supervision, teardown NO.** Byte bound **already shipped** (1 MiB, drains, never destroys — the reviewer is wrong on that half). Time bound genuinely absent — but not "forever": `node:http`'s stock defaults are headersTimeout **60s** / requestTimeout **300s** / keepAlive 5s / `connectionsCheckingInterval` **30s**. Two traps a naive fix walks into, both proven: setting `headersTimeout` alone does **not** evict promptly (it rides the 30s sweep — measured eviction at 30 006 ms), and once headers have arrived node evicts at **max(headersTimeout, requestTimeout)** (ht=5000/rt=300 fired at 5083 ms) — so `requestTimeout >= headersTimeout` is an INVARIANT. Ship 20s/30s/5s, all overridable. **Mount fanout: real but DORMANT** — the unit is `Type=simple`, explicitly "NEVER the `barkpark-slot@` template", and `deploy/` has no replica pattern anywhere ⇒ backlog. **Telegram poll supervision: the VENDOR loop is resilient** (per-update try/catch, exponential backoff capped at 30s, self-heals); what is missing is a Barkpark-owned watchdog like `gateway.ts`'s re-arm ⇒ backlog. **Teardown flake: DONE** — `pool.on("error")` + a mutation-proving test + `afterAll` awaits `pool.end()` before `DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE)`; 3× and 5× clean full runs, zero 57P01 ⇒ CLOSED, not rebuilt.
- **D58 [DECIDED — item (5) is TWO WAVES, not two slices] — tool connectors and identity-linking are backlogged with their real shape named.** **Tool connectors:** `ConnectorDirection` has `"tool"` as a TYPE TAG ONLY (zero users); the `Connector` interface is channel-shaped (`adapterFactory`/`InboundEvent`/`webhook`); the runner is actively hostile — `claude_chat.ex:337-350` hardcodes ONE MCP server (`bp mcp serve`) and passes `--strict-mcp-config` so the CLI *must never* merge another; nothing in the repo plays the MCP **client** role; D24's 5-knob scoping is unbuilt; and a THIRD per-workspace secret kind (a GitHub PAT / Linear key) would need its own AAD-bound seal. **Identity-linking (D31):** `InboundEvent` carries **no sender id at all** — it needs per-connector extraction across six connectors, a new fold table (pattern exists in `cloud/…/external_identity.ex`, but as a pattern, not a reuse), a re-key of the ONE piece of state the bridge holds (`thread_session_map`), and a linking-PROOF protocol that is a product-design question with no analog in the repo.
- **D59 [DECIDED — PROCESS, honestly] — gating is NOT mechanically enforced, so this wave shrinks the gating surface to ZERO and budgets an EXPLICIT integration slice.** W4 declared W4-1/W4-2 gating and W4-2/6/7/8 branched off main anyway — that single deviation produced all three wave-blocking integration bugs. So: the whole bridge half is **ONE slice, one builder, one branch** (write path + ticket + routes + containment + transport bound), not a gating slice with dependents. The Elixir half builds against the wire contract FIXED in D50/D51 (golden vector included) and needs no branch from it. **W5-4 is an explicitly budgeted integration slice** that rebases onto current `origin/main` after both merge, runs both gates, and drives the live channel. A cost paid openly beats a discipline pretended.
- **D60 [DECIDED — the live proof] — TELEGRAM, and the human gate is EXACTLY ONE INCH: the BotFather token.** Proven against the real `startBridge` with a fake token: seal → write → open (BOTH refs) → tenant resolve → adapterFactory → `Chat.initialize` → network all execute; the single failure is `api.telegram.org` rejecting the fake token. Discord costs FIVE Developer-Portal actions including a Message Content Intent checkbox that fails **silently**, and has no smoke script. The builder MAY spend ONE `ask-orchestrator` question to request a token from the human. On timeout: deliver everything up to the gate, hand over the copy-paste runbook, and leave `task-6c358e166b6c7eaf` criterion index 3 **OPEN** with the gate named. **Never fabricate a passing live proof.**

Keystone correction proven by scratch-install: **`thread.stream()` does not exist** — streaming is `thread.post()`, post-once = accumulate + one `post(finalText)` on `type:"result"` (D26). HOME = standalone `connectors/` npm service (D27, gate proven: `npm install` + `tsc --noEmit` + `vitest run` all exit 0 against the real `chat@4.34.0` types). Five opus slices (W3-1 gating scaffold+state; W3-2 chat-client+SSE; W3-3 turn-loop ack-first+post-once; W3-4 connector abstraction+tenant routing; W3-5 Telegram first-class + smoke harness). Live Telegram round-trip is an honest human gate. Parent = `task-e640bb01fca6ea38`; P2 rollup `task-6c358e166b6c7eaf` extended, not duplicated. Paper: `connectors-wave-3-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P3 (Wave 4) DECIDE — every channel, one registry entry; the tenant gap closes
Two verify rounds turned three carried premises inside out. **The multi-tenant hole is WORSE than stated**: the single operator token carries `admin`, so `RequireChatAccess` → `:global` → `owner_workspace_id = NULL` — every session the bridge ever created is TENANT-LESS, and any `:global` caller reads all of them (D35). **`@chat-adapter/shared`'s `encryptToken` binds no AAD** — a stolen credential row replays verbatim under another install, so we use Barkpark's OWN scheme (FieldCipher's "the scope is the AAD") in `node:crypto` (D37). **Slack Socket Mode is a silent trap**: `socket + installationProvider + a static botToken` constructs happily and makes both tenants reply with the SAME token while `getInstallation` is never called — the headline bug, in a second channel, with green tests (D40). The host question is CLOSED: guerrilla + a Caddy path route, proven live through `/mcp`, with NO wildcard DNS and therefore NO DNS gate — provided we take the path, not a subdomain (D34). The webhook seam is buildable dependency-free and fail-closed, keyed by the install ROW so a cross-tenant leak is unrepresentable, with ZERO core-loop change (D39, spike-proven 21 tests). Teams needs no per-workspace credential at all (D42); Discord and iMessage need no seam (D41/D44); WhatsApp's 24h window is entirely our code (D43). The CI vacuous green is sitting in the repo right now — 15 DB tests skip silently and the suite still reports green (D45), and merging `connectors/**` deploys nothing (D46). Eight opus slices; the Studio catalog is RULED (D47) but backlogged. Paper: `connectors-wave-4-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P3 (Wave 4) BUILT + REVIEWED — every channel lands; the tenant gap is closed (grade A−)

**Eight slices, all green, all merged into one integrated branch** (`loop-epic/w4-integration`; the five
`connectors/` slices all branched off `main` rather than off each other, so they had to be reconciled
before they could merge). Gate on the integrated tree: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `prettier --check` clean,
**329/329 vitest against a real Postgres** with `CONNECTORS_REQUIRE_DB=1`, `instance-deploy_test.sh`
ALL PASS, `docs-anchors-check` PASS, and the Elixir slice's 67/67 + `mix barkpark.openapi --check` exit 0.

**THE ACCEPTANCE BAR HELD, and it is now measurable rather than asserted.** From the post-gating
baseline (W4-1 deliberately rewrites `dispatch.ts` to close the multi-tenant gap — that is not "adding
a channel"), landing **Slack + Discord + Teams + WhatsApp + iMessage** changed **zero lines** of

```
$ git diff --stat <W4-1 tip> HEAD -- connectors/src/core/ connectors/src/tenant/resolve.ts \
    connectors/src/connector/registry.ts connectors/src/turn/
(empty)
```

Between them the five channels exercise **both** tenant strategies (credential-bound *and*
payload-team-id), **both** credential models (per-install *and* one-operator-app), and **both**
transports (webhook *and* socket). The `Connector` CONTRACT grew by two generic members
(`webhook.{keySource,extractInstallKey,handleUnkeyed}`) — but the LOOP did not move, and no member
names a provider. That is the honest statement of the invariant.

**The multi-tenant gap is CLOSED (D35/D37).** `BARKPARK_CHAT_TOKEN` is deleted from the request path
(the only mention left in `src/` is a loud deprecation warning). Both `credential_ref` and
`chat_token_ref` are sealed AES-256-GCM with the ROW IDENTITY as AAD, so a blob moved to another
tenant's row does not open. `chatClientForInstall(install)` mints the client from the same row that
routed the event, and a tokenless install is a typed drop (`dropped_no_chat_token`) with **zero HTTP
calls** — never an operator-token fallback. The two-layer cross-tenant negative test is real on both
sides: bridge (`cross-tenant-token.test.ts` — msw records every `Authorization`; token-A appears in
ZERO of ws-beta's requests) and server (`chat_token_controller_test.exs` — ws_A's minted token gets the
404 oracle on a ws_B session). Mutation-proven on both.

**THREE BUGS THE INDIVIDUAL SLICE GATES STRUCTURALLY COULD NOT SEE**, found and fixed at integration —
each one green in isolation, broken in combination:

1. **Slack could never have been configured.** Slack pings the Events `request_url` with
   `url_verification` BEFORE any workspace has installed the app, so it carries no `team_id`:
   `extractInstallKey` correctly returned null, the demux correctly found no install, and the seam
   404'd. "Verify URL" would have failed forever. Fixed generically — `ConnectorWebhook.handleUnkeyed`,
   consulted ONLY on a null key, forbidden from touching a tenant credential, absent ⇒ the same opaque
   404. Not an `if (provider === "slack")` in the router.
2. **The deploy and the bridge disagreed about the address.** W4-5 writes
   `CONNECTORS_HTTP_ADDR=127.0.0.1:4020` and points Caddy at `:4020`; W4-2's config read
   `CONNECTORS_WEBHOOK_PORT` and bound `:3000` on **every interface**. Green deploy, active unit, clean
   journal — and every provider webhook on the maintenance 503 until Slack/Meta disabled the endpoint.
   `CONNECTORS_HTTP_ADDR` is now the one contract; it binds **loopback** (the seam trusts
   `x-forwarded-*`, which is only trustworthy from a proxy) and **throws** on a malformed value.
3. **Three incompatible webhook contracts.** W4-2 declared `extractInstallKey(rawBody, headers)`, W4-6
   shipped `(request)`, W4-8 shipped `(payload)` in locally-declared descriptors. Collapsed onto D39's
   raw-bytes shape (widened to allow async — Slack's HMAC verify is a promise).

Also landed at review: `GET {prefix}/health` (the deploy's post-restart probe could previously only
ever log `000`) — tenant-less, enumerating nothing. All three fixes are **mutation-proven**: reverting
each one reds its tests.

**LEDGER.** `connectors-p3-slack` carried the wave's off-by-one live: every evidence string sat one row
below the criterion it proved, the headline zero-core-change criterion read `met=false`, and **"PR
merged" was falsely `met=true`** — the exact false-done pattern. Repaired and republished. (`bp task
stamp --criterion` is ZERO-based; **five of eight builders hit this**, most caught it on readback. The
