`connectors-chat-token-mint-endpoint` was a duplicate of the slice that actually built it — closed.

**HONEST GAPS, none fabricated away.** No live install exists for ANY channel: Slack needs an app at
api.slack.com, Teams needs an Azure Bot + **per-customer-org admin consent Barkpark can never perform**,
WhatsApp needs Meta Business verification + App Review, Discord needs a BYO bot token, iMessage needs a
dedicated Mac. Every one is named exactly, with a runnable harness, and marked NOT PASSED. The 24h
WhatsApp window is a tested policy library that **nothing yet calls** (the reply path is always inside
the window; the proactive path does not exist). One service-wide `CONNECTORS_CREDENTIAL_KEY` protects
every tenant — a DB dump alone cannot impersonate a bot, but a key leak compromises all of them
(`connectors-per-workspace-dek`, filed).

**NEXT WAVE takes the Studio Connectors catalog (D47, ruled and not built)** *(W33 annotation: this happened — Wave 5 PR #3213 shipped it, Wave 10 PR #3429 extended it; W33's premise smoke caught a fresh wish citing this line as still-unbuilt)* — and with it the one loop
nothing closes today: *nobody automates minting a chat token and sealing it into
`connector_installs.chat_token_ref`*. Until that exists, every install is a manual seal, and the
two-minute north star is not real. D47's two traps are already ruled: a non-superuser role needs an
explicit `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA chat_bridge`, and `workspace_id` is TEXT while `Workspace.id` is
`:binary_id`, so every join needs `type(ci.workspace_id, Ecto.UUID)`. Second: **drive one channel to a
live round-trip** — Telegram or Discord, the two with no corporate gate — because five channels proven
offline and zero proven live is the wave's real risk. Paper: `connectors-wave-4-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P4 (Wave 5) DECIDE — the two-minute claim becomes a loop, and ONE channel goes live
Two verify rounds killed the strategy's own keystone lean. **The connect ticket cannot be the bridge's authority**: `verify_token/1` is `where kind == "api"`, so a signed ticket can never be a Bearer, and the mint route demands owner/admin MEMBERSHIP in that exact workspace — so Studio mints IN-PROCESS, through ONE shared context fn, and the bridge still does every `chat_bridge` write (D48). The mint's `["chat"]` hardcode is a CONTROLLER constant, not a property of `create_token/5` — an in-process caller can mint `["admin"]` and escalate its own membership row, so the literal moves to one place and the controller delegates (D48). **Studio's mount gate is NOT the mint's gate** (global permissions vs per-workspace membership: `has_permission?(admin)=true` / `workspace_admin?=false` on the same token) — the connect handler re-gates, fail-closed, on both principal kinds (D49). The connect-ticket PRIMITIVE already exists in `slack-oauth.ts` and is wired to **zero** HTTP routes; generalised, with the raw token moved off the readable `state` and onto the loopback, it becomes the whole seam (D50/D51). **A new trap, worse than the two pre-ruled ones**: `upsertInstall`'s unconditional `ON CONFLICT` silently WIPES the sibling sealed secret, and the fix the filed task prescribed (re-seal both blobs on a workspace change) would hand tenant B a token that authenticates as A — COALESCE is worse than the bug; the answer is a workspace-guarded conditional preserve (D52). **A bad bot token is a FLEET OUTAGE**: `startBridge` has no try/catch, so one revoked token crash-loops the unit and the next deploy disables it for every tenant (D53). The GRANT mandate is a **no-op on every path we run** — the real blocker is that no migration creates `chat_bridge`, so the Elixir test DB has never seen the table; a 14-line `test_helper.exs` fixture through `Repo` fixes it, sandbox rollback and all (D54). The stamp guard is deployed and works — it is **opt-in by design**, and an unguarded stamp lands silently: it fails closed on `--met`, with the `close.ex` autostamp taught the text in the same PR (D56). Hardening is ONE live item (the time bound, with a `connectionsCheckingInterval` trap) and three latent (D57). Tool connectors and identity-linking are WAVES, not slices (D58). Gating is not enforceable, so the bridge half is ONE branch and an integration slice is budgeted openly (D59). Live = Telegram; the gate is exactly one inch wide and it is a human's (D60). Five opus slices. Paper: `connectors-wave-5-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P4 (Wave 5) BUILT + REVIEWED — the connect loop is a PRODUCT; the wire is proven ACROSS LANGUAGES; no channel is live (grade B+)

**Four of five slices green.** `connectors-p4-connect-seam` (the whole bridge half, one branch),
`connectors-p3-studio-catalog` (the Elixir half + THE LOOP), `bp-task-stamp-criterion-off-by-one`
(the ledger's integrity), `connectors-ci-format-check`. The fifth — `connectors-p4-live-telegram` —
did NOT retire the wave's real risk, and said so.

**THE TWO-MINUTE CLAIM IS NOW A LOOP.** Click Connect → paste a token → the bridge asks the PROVIDER
(`getMe` / `users/@me`) and names the bot → the operator confirms THAT bot → Barkpark mints a chat
token labelled `connector:<provider>:<install_key>` → the credential + the token ride ONE POST over
LOOPBACK → the bridge seals both against the row identity and MOUNTS the adapter live, no restart →
the card flips to Connected *because the install was read back out of the database*. A failed
`/connect` REVOKES the token it just minted (a minted-but-unattached chat token is a live workspace
credential with no owner). Disconnect unmounts, DELETEs the row (the bridge had **no SQL DELETE at
all** — a disconnect was previously undone by the next restart) and revokes the labelled token.

**THE CROSS-LANGUAGE WIRE IS PROVEN LIVE — at review, not by a test double.** This was the wave's
loudest named risk (two halves, two languages, one wire that existed only in this charter). On a tree
with both halves merged: the Elixir signer reproduces D50's GOLDEN VECTOR byte-for-byte, and the REAL
Node bridge (real Postgres, real listener) accepted an ELIXIR-signed ticket — `POST
/connectors/connect/validate` → `422 {"error":"invalid_credential","reason":"Unauthorized"}`, where
`Unauthorized` came from the **real api.telegram.org**, so the Node verifier accepted the Elixir HMAC,
resolved the connector and ran `validate()`. One flipped signature byte → `401`. A valid ticket
carrying `x-forwarded-for` → the opaque `404` (loopback-only *by construction*, on a live server).
Closed through the real `BridgeClient` too. Pinned as a regression test
(`api/test/barkpark/connectors/bridge_client_test.exs` — a raw socket answering `connect.ts`'s exact
bytes), because the golden vector pins the ticket BYTES and pinned nothing about the request paths,
the body keys or the response shapes.

**TWO CROSS-TENANT HOLES FOUND AND CLOSED AT REVIEW**, both in the new connect seam, both
mutation-proven:

1. **The Discord install key was CALLER-CHOSEN.** `validate` took it from the pasted `applicationId`
   and never checked it against the bot the token authenticates as. An application id is **public** —
   it is in every bot's invite URL — so workspace B could paste A's app id beside B's OWN valid bot
   token: validation passed (B's token really does authenticate), the install key was A's, and
   `UPSERT_INSTALL`'s `ON CONFLICT` repointed A's row to B. Discord gives a bot user the same
   snowflake as its application; demand that they agree.
2. **`connect` never checked the incumbent.** `workspace_id = EXCLUDED.workspace_id` is
   unconditional, so a connect against a key another workspace owns silently repointed the row, took
   their adapter down and replaced their sealed chat token. Now a typed `409 install_owned_elsewhere`,
   checked with `resolveWorkspace` (workspace_id ALONE) so a row whose seals no longer open still
   guards its tenant instead of becoming free real estate.

Also at review: the bridge's deliberately-opaque wire codes were reaching operators as UI copy
("Could not disconnect: **not_found**"), and a `502 mount_failed` was mapped to `{:http, 502}`,
discarding the one sentence that explains it. Translated at the wire boundary, where the wire is owned.

**THE STAMP GUARD FAILS CLOSED.** The repo carried a GREEN test literally named *"no text guard → an
in-range wrong index still flips the neighbour, no error"*. It is now inverted: any criteria update
flipping `met: true` without a non-empty `"criterion"` text is `{:error, :criterion_text_required}` —
in `merge_criteria`, which close and stamp SHARE, so there is one rule with **zero internal
exemptions** (close's own merge-gate autostamp threads the stored text in rather than riding the old
hole). The 409 TEACHES: an actionable `message` names the exact flag to type, across seven surfaces
including the epic-cycle spawn prompt that taught the index-only shape in the first place. **Blast
radius, accepted and named: every agent that flips a criterion by index alone now gets a 409 — on
CLOSE as well as stamp.** A lead closing a merge-gated criterion must pass `"criterion"` per entry.
The audit sweep found three confirmed +1 shifts in the published ledger and REPORTED them
(`ledger-stamp-shift-repair-2026-07-14`) rather than quietly repairing rows another worker holds.

**WHAT DID NOT HAPPEN: no channel is live, and the reason is structural, not an excuse.** The live
slice was dispatched IN PARALLEL with the two slices it was told to integrate "only after they are
merged" — D59 named gating as unenforceable and budgeted an integration slice, and the loop then ran
that slice concurrently with what it integrates, which is the same bug wearing a different hat. The
one `ask-orchestrator` question for a throwaway BotFather token timed out (300s). No live proof was
fabricated. The integration and cross-language criteria of `connectors-p4-live-telegram` were stamped
AT REVIEW from real run output; the deploy and the round trip stay OPEN, with the gate named to the
inch.

**NEXT WAVE.** (1) **Deploy and drive Telegram live** — everything up to the BotFather token now
exists and is proven on one tree; this needs a merged main, a deploy, and one human token. It is the
ONLY thing keeping `task-6c358e166b6c7eaf` open. (2) **Add to Slack** (`connectors-slack-oauth-connect-loop`,
filed) — the one catalog card that still shows a gate instead of a button, and the channel most teams
actually want; its real design question (an OAuth install cannot know `install_key` before the
redirect, and the token's label is disconnect's only handle) belongs in a Decide, not a builder's
head. (3) The availability backlog D57 left standing (webhook mount fanout, Telegram poll supervision)
plus `connectors-per-workspace-dek`. **Process: if a slice integrates other slices, it cannot be
dispatched in the same parallel wave as them — either the loop enforces the ordering or the
integration is the REVIEWER's job, which is where it actually landed this wave.**
Paper: `connectors-wave-5-2026-07-14`.

## Wave 6 = P4b — decisions (D61–D68, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand)

Every decision is grounded in run output recorded in `connectors-wave-6-2026-07-14`. The verify fleet
RE-PROVED the baseline (`npm ci` from scratch, real Postgres) and turned FOUR carried premises inside
out — the charter follows the evidence, not the task text.

- **D61 [DECIDED — D52 is ALREADY FIXED; the pending-connect rationale is minting-authority, NOT a live sibling-wipe.]** V1 re-proved it with real output: full suite **387/387, ZERO skips** with `CONNECTORS_REQUIRE_DB=1` (an unreachable DB becomes a HARD FAILURE, not a silent skip), and `install-write-path.test.ts` (11 tests, real ephemeral Postgres) drives the REAL `handleSlackOAuthCallback` with NO chat-token key and asserts `chat_token_ref` is **preserved, not null** — the per-column CASE at `installs.ts:145-149` (`WHEN EXCLUDED.chat_token_ref IS NOT NULL THEN … ELSE connector_installs.chat_token_ref`), a real fix, not a stub. So the task text (and D52's own framing) citing an *unconditional-overwrite wipe* as a LIVE hazard is **stale** — that bug shipped fixed in the P4 merge (`45973c7d7`). S1 still writes the install ONCE with both secrets, but for the REAL reasons: the bridge **cannot mint** a Barkpark chat token (a public OAuth callback has no synchronous channel back to Studio) and a fresh Slack install lands `chat_token_ref` **NULL** today (`slack-oauth.ts` builds its install with no `chatToken` key — the only production caller). The single write closes the fresh-install-NULL gap, not a wipe.
- **D62 [DECIDED — S1 mount: a FOURTH route class, public-by-state, never loopback-gated.]** Mount the OAuth callback as a NEW branch inside `webhook-server.ts`'s dispatcher, **between** the connect-family block (~`:591`) and the webhook demux (~`:599`), reusing the UNEXPORTED `decodeSegments`/`segmentsUnderPrefix` helpers (traversal hardening is not duplicated). Two structural non-negotiables, both proven by the survey: **(a)** it is **GET**, not the connect-family's POST-only (WhatsApp's `hub.challenge` GET is the precedent) — a POST-only gate opaque-404s every real Slack redirect; **(b)** it MUST NOT be `isProxied`-gated — Slack redirects a browser through Caddy, so `x-forwarded-*` is guaranteed present, and the loopback 404 would make the callback **permanently unreachable**. It authenticates by SIGNED STATE (HMAC), not by network topology. `url_verification`'s `team_id`-less pre-install ping stays on the separate `handleUnkeyed` arm at `/webhooks/slack` (a distinct path — do not regress it).
- **D63 [DECIDED — the pending-connect join: two requests, one public write.]** A NEW bridge-owned `chat_bridge.pending_connect` table (`nonce` PK, `workspace_id`, `provider`, sealed `chat_token_ref`, `expires_at`; TTL reuses `OAUTH_STATE_TTL_MS` = 600 000 ms). The flow crosses a redirect: **(1)** Studio, over LOOPBACK (same trust boundary as the existing `/connect` route), mints the `["chat"]` token, seals it, and POSTs it to STAGE a `pending_connect` row keyed by `nonce`; **(2)** Studio hands the browser the Add-to-Slack URL whose signed `state` embeds that `nonce`; **(3)** Slack redirects the PUBLIC callback (`code` + signed `state`) back; **(4)** the callback verifies `state` (crypto, not IP), exchanges `code` → bot token, JOINS the pending row by `nonce`, and writes the install ONCE with BOTH secrets — supplying non-blank `credentialRef` AND `chatToken` to the EXISTING `upsertInstall` fires the `EXCLUDED IS NOT NULL` arm for both columns in one atomic statement, **zero `installs.ts` change** — then CONSUMES/deletes the pending row (single-use, replay-proof). Missing/expired pending row ⇒ **fail closed**, no install. The raw chat token NEVER rides the readable OAuth `state`; it rides the loopback stage.
- **D64 [DECIDED — S1 MUST add the incumbent-owner check; it is ABSENT today (the load-bearing verify finding).]** V4 SPLIT VERDICT: server-derive `install_key` is **already present** (`installKeyFromOAuth` reads Slack's own `oauth.v2.access` — `enterprise_id ?? team_id`, never caller-chosen; inherited for free). But the `resolveWorkspace → 409 install_owned_elsewhere` guard is **ABSENT** — the callback runs `slack-oauth.ts:391` (install-key) straight to `:410 upsertInstall` with no owner check, and `SlackOAuthCallbackDeps` carries no `resolveWorkspace`/`installs` dep. D61 fixed the SECRET columns, NOT `workspace_id`: `ON CONFLICT` still repoints `workspace_id = EXCLUDED.workspace_id` unconditionally, so an attacker holding a legit signed state for their OWN workspace can re-auth a Slack team another tenant owns and REPOINT the victim's row (takedown). This vector is MORE load-bearing on Slack's PUBLIC callback than on the loopback connect routes. S1 MUST REPLICATE `connect.ts:288-303` inside the callback: add `resolveWorkspace(provider, installKey)` to the deps; `owner !== null && owner !== workspaceId` ⇒ typed **409 `install_owned_elsewhere`** BEFORE `upsertInstall`. It CANNOT route through `connect.ts` (loopback-only, POST-only, different entry) — **replicate, do not reuse**.
- **D65 [DECIDED — fold the ticket unify into S1; the golden vector is INLINE constants, no JSON file.]** `connectors-connect-ticket-unify-slack-state` folds into S1 (it owns `slack-oauth.ts` + `slack-connector.test.ts`; a concurrent slice on the same file would collide). It is NOT optional: the Elixir signer emits `{w,p,n,t}` while `slack-oauth`'s `verifyOAuthState` expects `{w,n,t}`, so a Studio-minted "Add to Slack" state can only verify once `slack-oauth`'s two self-uses (`buildSlackInstallUrl` `:208`, callback `:360`) are repointed onto `signConnectTicket`/`verifyConnectTicket({provider:'slack'})` and the state carries `p:'slack'`. Repoint the two TEST importers (`install-write-path.test.ts:39`, `slack-connector.test.ts:40/42`); there is **zero** `src/` importer outside `slack-oauth.ts`. **Correction, twice-proven** (V1 `find` + V4 `find`): `test/fixtures/connect-ticket-golden.json` **DOES NOT EXIST** — the golden vector is inline constants `GOLDEN_TICKET`/`GOLDEN_TICKET_INPUT`/`GOLDEN_TICKET_SECRET` at `connect/ticket.ts:75-83` (provider `telegram`). Any "read the golden json" instruction is wrong. Name the ONE behavior delta in the PR: the unify tightens Slack's future-skew tolerance from 10 min to 30 s (`CONNECT_TICKET_MAX_SKEW_MS`); no existing test exercises future-dating. The Elixir `connect_ticket.ex` needs NO change (it already signs an arbitrary `provider`).
- **D66 [DECIDED — the Studio card flip needs a NEW `connect_mode` + an external-link render branch, no hex fallbacks.]** `catalog.ex`'s Slack entry: `gate → nil`, and add a distinguishing field (`connect_mode :oauth` vs `:paste`) — today's single `connectable?` boolean assumes paste → credential-modal and its `open_connect`/`validate_credential`/`confirm_connect` handlers CANNOT be reused for OAuth. `connectors_live.ex` gets a NEW render branch: an external `<a>` "Add to Slack" button carrying the signed state (Elixir mints a `{w,p:'slack',n,t}` ticket via `ConnectTicket.sign` — the format `verifyConnectTicket` now expects), NOT `phx-click` into the paste LiveView. `studio-literal-check` is **BLOCKING**: no inline color literals, no `var(--x, #hex)` fallbacks — real tokens only (`--warn/--ok/--danger/--info/--primary` + `-soft/-fg/-hsl`; NEVER `--warning/--success`). Studio changes are `.ex` ⇒ the slice **WAITS for the Elixir Test gate** (~8 min).
- **D67 [DECIDED — S2 = per-workspace DEK + rewrap as ONE slice; key_version is BLOB-INTERNAL; rewrap CAS pins old ciphertext bytes; three tasks dedup to ONE.]**
  - **Dedup (V3 ruling):** keep `connectors-per-workspace-dek` (#3182 lineage, the ONLY one the charter ever named); RETIRE `connectors-credential-key-rewrap-sweep` (#3186) and `connectors-credential-rewrap-sweep` (#3199) — filed 5.5h later, 16 min apart, each re-discovering the same gap #3173 already carries. Fold the sharpest AC wording (#3199's idempotent/resumable + old-key-drop proof + doc-fix; #3186's custody) into the survivor. Repeating W4's duplicate-task mistake if we don't.
  - **Key-versioning is BLOB-INTERNAL, not a physical column (V2 ruling, PROVEN).** No Elixir code decodes the cipher blob: `Barkpark.Connectors.Install` is read-only, `readable_columns/0` omits both secret columns, `Catalog.installs_for_workspace/1` uses an explicit `select:` naming neither, and `install_schema_test.exs` asserts `inspect(row)` never contains `"SEALED"`. A PHYSICAL `key_version` column would force a 3-file cross-language DDL edit (`schema.ts` + `install.ex` + `test_helper.exs`) — touching S1's `schema.ts` and colliding with S3. A version tag INSIDE the sealed AAD/ciphertext envelope needs NONE of them. DEK derivation (e.g. `HKDF(masterKey, workspaceId)`) lives entirely inside `createCredentialCipher`'s closure — seal/open SIGNATURE unchanged (`identity` already carries `workspaceId`), **zero call-site edits**, keeping S2 fully disjoint from S1.
  - **Rewrap concurrency: pin old ciphertext bytes, NOT `key_version` alone.** The strategy's own "CAS on `key_version`" WOULD SHIP A BUG (Digest correction 2): a `WHERE key_version=1` UPDATE clobbers a concurrent `upsertInstall` that didn't bump the version, rolling a fresh secret back to stale. With blob-internal versioning there is no column to CAS on anyway; the rewrap UPDATE pins the OLD bytes it read: `… AND credential_ref IS NOT DISTINCT FROM $old_cred AND chat_token_ref IS NOT DISTINCT FROM $old_token` — ANY concurrent secret write invalidates the CAS, the sweep skips that row and retries it next pass. `installs.ts` stays untouched (matches the partition).
  - **"Bridge is the only writer" holds:** the rewrap is a bridge-owned module (`connectors/src/tenant/rewrap.ts` + a thin runnable entry) that imports the bridge's OWN pool + cipher and adds its own cross-provider list query + byte-pinned UPDATE — never an external raw-SQL tool.
  - **Custody + doc-fix:** `instance-deploy.sh` auto-generates `CONNECTORS_CREDENTIAL_KEY` via `openssl rand -base64 32` into `.env` with ZERO backup (V3 confirmed). Add a human-backupable custody story (documented in the runbook: where it's backed up, how rotation triggers, what breaks if lost). Fix the FALSE "Rotating it makes every stored connector credential undecryptable" caveat at `docs/ops/connectors-deploy.md:99-102` AND the identical copy baked into `deploy/instance-deploy.sh:311-312` (V3 found both — refuted by `credential-cipher.ts:221` `const keys = [current, ...previous]`).
- **D68 [DECIDED — S3 = DDL drift gate, static text comparison, wired into a both-paths CI job.]** A NEW `scripts/connectors-ddl-drift-check.sh` (modeled on `docs-anchors-check.sh`, bash 3.2, NO DB): extract the `connector_installs` column-name set from `connectors/src/db/schema.ts`'s `CREATE_CONNECTOR_INSTALLS_SQL` (`:84`) + `ADD_CHAT_TOKEN_REF_SQL` (`:103`) and from `api/test/test_helper.exs`'s `CREATE TABLE chat_bridge.connector_installs` (`:99`) + its `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` (`:114`); sort both; fail with a NAMED diff if they differ. Wire as one step in the existing `.github/workflows/doc-gates.yml` job (its triggers already include `**/*.ts` and `**/*.exs`, so both files trip it — the "runs on BOTH paths" criterion is satisfied for free). S3 READS `schema.ts` but does NOT edit it — S1's NEW `pending_connect` table is a SEPARATE table that does not change `connector_installs`'s column list, so no merge conflict. Files disjoint from S1/S2.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P4b (Wave 6) DECIDE — the last gated card becomes a button; the shared master key retires
Two verify rounds RE-PROVED the baseline (`npm ci` from scratch, real Postgres — **387/387, zero skips**) and corrected FOUR carried premises. **D52 is already fixed** (V1: `install-write-path.test.ts` drives the REAL callback and asserts `chat_token_ref` preserved) — so S1's pending-connect exists for MINTING-AUTHORITY (the bridge cannot mint a chat token, the public callback has no channel to Studio) and fresh-install-NULL, not a live wipe (D61). The Slack callback mounts as a FOURTH route class inside `webhook-server.ts` — GET, state-authenticated, NEVER loopback-gated (Caddy always adds `x-forwarded-*`, which the loopback gate 404s) (D62). A bridge-owned `pending_connect` row joins the loopback-staged sealed token with the public `code` in ONE write (D63). **The load-bearing verify finding**: server-derive `install_key` is already present, but the `resolveWorkspace → 409 install_owned_elsewhere` guard is ABSENT — S1 must replicate `connect.ts:288-303` inside the callback, because `workspace_id` is still repointed unconditionally and this is a PUBLIC entry (D64). The ticket unify folds in and is not optional (`{w,n,t}` vs `{w,p,n,t}`); the golden vector is INLINE constants, `connect-ticket-golden.json` does not exist (D65). The Studio flip needs a new `connect_mode` + external-link render branch, real tokens no hex (D66). S2 takes per-workspace-DEK + rewrap as ONE slice with a BLOB-INTERNAL version tag (V2: no Elixir decodes the blob, so no cross-language column edit) and a byte-pinned CAS (the strategy's `key_version`-CAS would ship a rollback bug); three duplicate DEK tasks dedup to one; the false "undecryptable on rotate" claim is fixed in BOTH the doc and the deploy script (D67). S3 is a DB-free static drift gate on the twice-transcribed DDL (D68). Three opus slices, file-partitioned, true parallel. Paper: `connectors-wave-6-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P4b (Wave 6) BUILT + REVIEWED — the LAST gated card is a button; the shared master key retires (grade A−)

**Three slices, all green, all file-partitioned with zero write-write overlap** (no integration
branch needed — the D59 law held this wave). The reviewer re-ran every gate independently and made

`loop-epic/chat-bridge-ddl-drift-gate-a-db-free-che-2` (W6-3).

**W6-1 — Add to Slack (the headliner).** The built-but-unrouted OAuth callback is now a FOURTH route
class inside `webhook-server.ts` — GET, state-authenticated by the signed connect ticket, NEVER
`isProxied`-gated (D62), reusing the unexported traversal-safe path helpers. A bridge-owned
`pending_connect` table (sealed under a DISTINCT `pending:<nonce>` identity, single-use
`DELETE…RETURNING`, TTL-checked in SQL, fail-closed) joins the loopback-staged chat token to the
public `code` in ONE `upsertInstall` write (D63) — zero `installs.ts` edit. **The load-bearing
verify finding was honoured**: the ABSENT `resolveWorkspace → 409 install_owned_elsewhere` guard is
now present, checked BEFORE the pending row is consumed, so a public re-auth cannot repoint a victim's
row (D64). The ticket unify folded in cleanly — `signOAuthState`/`verifyOAuthState` deleted, one
`signConnectTicket`/`verifyConnectTicket({provider:'slack'})` path, provider mismatch enforced,
future-skew tightened 10min→30s, golden vector preserved (D65). Studio's Slack card flips to a real
external Add-to-Slack `<a>` carrying an Elixir-signed `{w,p:'slack',n,t}` ticket, `connect_mode:oauth`
disjoint from the paste handlers, honest not-configured note, real tokens no hex (D66). Node gate
**397/397 zero skips + format clean** (independently re-run on real Postgres); `studio-literal-check`
PASS (356 files, independently re-run — bash, no Elixir); the Elixir tests are real and pin the
security-critical behaviour (raw token NOT in the URL, HMAC signature match, `["chat"]`
workspace-bound token, D49 non-admin gate mints/stages NOTHING, ≤1 live OAuth token). Human gate named
exactly in `docs/ops/slack-app.md`; live Slack round trip left OPEN, no install fabricated.

**W6-2 — per-workspace DEK + rewrap (the one with teeth).** Each workspace's DEK is
`HKDF-SHA256(instance KEK, salt, "workspace|"+wsId)` derived INSIDE the cipher closure — seal/open
signature byte-stable, ZERO call-site edits, `schema.ts`/`install.ex`/`test_helper.exs` verified
untouched. Key-version is the blob-internal AAD prefix (`bpc1|`/`bpc2|`), recovered by trying each
scheme; **V1 backward-compat verified against main** (the pre-wave cipher sealed `bpc1|` + instance
key directly; the new V1 open path reproduces it byte-for-byte). The rewrap sweep is a bridge-owned
module with a byte-pinned CAS (`IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` on the OLD ciphertext) — idempotent, resumable,
concurrency-safe; real-Postgres tests PROVE old-key-drop (seal under A → rewrap → open with ONLY B),
second-run no-op, resume, a mid-sweep concurrent write surviving un-rolled-back, and unopenable rows
left alone. Custody story added and the FALSE "rotating makes credentials undecryptable" claim fixed
in BOTH the doc and the deploy script. Gate fully green: **395/395 zero skips + format +
`instance-deploy_test.sh` ALL PASS**. Canonical marker unique and preserved.

both sources of truth, NAMED diff on mismatch, bundled `--selftest` tripwire, wired into

## Wave 7 = P5 — decisions (D69–D76, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand)

The epic's OTHER direction opens: **TOOL connectors** (outbound — the agent ACTS on GitHub/Linear via
MCP), the dual of the six channel connectors. Every decision is grounded in run output recorded in
`connectors-wave-7-2026-07-14`. The verify fleet RAN the real `claude` binary and re-proved the Node
baseline; the crux the whole wave rested on is PROVEN, not asserted.

- **D69 [DECIDED — THE CRUX IS PROVEN LIVE; a tool connector is a SECOND `mcpServers` key.]** V1 RAN the actual runner binary (`claude 2.1.209`, the one `/Applications/cmux.app/.../bin/claude` resolves to): a mixed **stdio (`barkpark`) + http (tool)** pair connects under `--strict-mcp-config`, BOTH tools are discoverable (`mcp__srvA__stdio_ping`, `mcp__srvB__http_ping`), and the http tool CALL round-trips via `--print`. **Strict mode restricts SOURCES, not COUNT** — it means "use only the servers in `--mcp-config`," and within that one file `mcpServers` is a record accepting any mix of stdio+http entries. So mechanically a tool connector is exactly a SECOND key in `claude_chat.ex` `mcp_config/1`'s (~:337-350) `mcpServers` map — `mcp_args/1` (~:164-165) already passes `--strict-mcp-config` unchanged, independent of server count. The approach does NOT collapse. Exact accepted http shape: `{"type":"http","url":"…","headers":{…}?,"headersHelper":"<shell cmd>"?}`.
- **D70 [DECIDED — GitHub first, static PAT, hosted remote MCP. Linear (OAuth) is follow-on.]** Both GitHub and Linear are hosted remote HTTP MCP servers (no local process), so that axis is a wash. GitHub takes a **static fine-grained PAT** as `Authorization: Bearer <PAT>` to `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, which reuses the PROVEN paste `connect_mode` (`getMe`-style validate → derive `install_key`) with ZERO new OAuth UI. Linear defaults to OAuth 2.1 (dynamic client registration) — re-paying Slack's just-shipped cost for the epic's other half, buying nothing generic. Prove end-to-end with a read-only tool (`list_issues` / `get_file_contents`). REJECT Linear this wave; file it as follow-on.
- **D71 [DECIDED — THE CREDENTIAL FLOW = Architecture D (`headersHelper`), D38-clean and PROVEN.]** A GitHub PAT must reach the subprocess's request headers while **no Elixir decrypts a connector credential (D38)**. V1 PROVED `headersHelper` is real in `claude 2.1.209`: it is a **shell command** (`hA(cmd,[],{shell:true,timeout:10s})`) whose trimmed stdout must be a JSON `string→string` map, merged as the request headers — and it fires at MCP-connect time **even without `--dangerously-skip-permissions`** (the trust gate applies only to `scope=project/local` servers, i.e. `.mcp.json`; a `--mcp-config`-FILE server carries no `scope`, so the gate is skipped). So: the bridge returns a descriptor `{type:"http", url, headersHelper:"<curl the bridge loopback tool-headers route with a scoped ticket>"}`; at connect the SUBPROCESS runs the command, the bridge opens its OWN sealed install and emits `{"Authorization":"Bearer <pat>"}`; **the PAT NEVER transits Elixir** (Elixir writes only the command string + a non-secret ticket). REJECT **arch A** (bridge returns the real header → PAT lands in BEAM memory + the 0600 temp mcp-config file: weakens D38's spirit, widest blast radius), **arch C** (bridge-fronted MCP proxy → the most code + the bridge on the hot path of every tool call), **arch B** (Elixir decrypts → violates D38 outright), and **`${ENV_VAR}` header interpolation** (works — proved — but Elixir sets the subprocess env, so Elixir holds plaintext: arch-B-flavored). Arch D is the only candidate BOTH lean AND fully D38-preserving. Cross-tenant injection is blocked twice: the ConnectTicket binds `workspace+provider` and the seal's AAD carries `workspace_id`.
- **D72 [DECIDED — the tool install rides `connector_installs` with ZERO schema change; the ONE real gap is a by-workspace query.]** V6 confirmed green baseline (**405/405 zero-skip**) and that the seal is provider-agnostic: `provider` is unconstrained `text`, `credentialAad` binds `[provider, installKey, workspaceId]` generically, so a GitHub PAT is the **THIRD secret kind** through the SAME `credential-cipher.ts` seal — never a second credential path (epic law). A tool install: `provider="github"`, `credential_ref` = sealed PAT, `chat_token_ref` **permanently NULL** (a tool has no inbound event, so no chat token is ever minted — legal today, like Teams' NULL case). **Direction lives on the registry entry (`ConnectorDirection`, already typed `tool`/`both` with zero consumers), NOT on the row** → the W6 DDL drift gate is untouched. The ONE genuine gap: `installs.ts` has NO by-`workspace_id` query (all reads key on `(provider, install_key)` or provider alone); add `WHERE provider=$1 AND workspace_id=$2` (~15 lines mirroring `listInstalls`, opens the seal for the tool-headers route). The connect flow branches on `connector.direction` (a tool's `/connect` seals only the credential and skips chat-token staging) — **branched on the declared direction, NEVER on a provider id**, so the "zero provider special-casing" bar holds.
- **D73 [DECIDED — THE SEAM: bridge returns non-secret descriptors over loopback; the subprocess fetches the secret.]** Elixir must know WHICH tool servers to inject without owning direction-truth or the secret. At `setup_mcp` (`claude_chat.ex` ~:1101), Elixir mints a workspace-scoped ConnectTicket and calls a NEW `BridgeClient.fetch_tool_descriptors(ticket)` (loopback POST, a 5th method beside validate/connect/disconnect/stage_pending) → a NEW bridge route `POST {prefix}/connect/tool-descriptors` (loopback-only, 404 on `x-forwarded-*`) filters the workspace's installs to **tool** direction (the bridge owns `registry`, so it filters — Elixir never mirrors direction) and returns `[{provider, type:"http", url, headersHelper}]`, carrying **NO secret**. `mcp_config/1` folds each descriptor as a second `mcpServers` key. The descriptor's `headersHelper` curls a second NEW bridge route `POST {prefix}/connect/tool-headers/:provider` (loopback-only) which opens the sealed install (D72 query) and returns `{"Authorization":"Bearer <pat>"}`. **TTL:** the tool ticket is minted with a **session-length TTL** (default 8h, overridable) rather than the connect flow's 600s, because it is baked into the per-session mcp-config file and the http server may (re)connect minutes into a session; it is loopback-only, single-purpose (unlocks a header FETCH the agent can already trigger via the mounted tool), and dies with the temp file — a named, bounded widening, not a blocker. Both new routes reuse the existing loopback gate + ticket verifier.
- **D74 [DECIDED — D24 tool-scoping is NAMED not built; the live GitHub call is a one-inch human gate.]** D24's 5-knob Cloud scoping is **0/5 built** and the Cloud runner does not exist (D10) — so a self-hosted tool turn runs through the SAME `Port.open` spawn with full host trust (`Bash/Read/Write/Edit`) alongside a live PAT in one turn. Headliner criterion 3 is satisfied by **naming the widening in writing** (here + the wave Paper + the task evidence): a self-hosted tool connector inherits the runner's existing trust level, and the GitHub PAT's blast radius is bounded ONLY by the PAT's own scope — which is why the human gate demands a **fine-grained, read-only, single-repo PAT**. Cloud tool-scoping (D24) is follow-on. **THE HUMAN GATE (V7-pinned, hand to a human verbatim):** "Create a fine-grained PAT at github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens/new → Repository access → Only select repositories → one repo → Repository permissions → Contents: Read-only and/or Issues: Read-only (Metadata: Read-only is auto-added) → send as `Authorization: Bearer <PAT>` to `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`." The STUB proves the wire (no token). A live GitHub call needs this PAT; deliver everything up to it and **NEVER fabricate a live tool call** — ZERO live installs exist, tool OR channel.
- **D75 [DECIDED — `connectors-catalog-connectable-drift` = a STATIC drift gate, NOT a live route.]** The wish says "model the fix on the DDL drift gate." That gate is a **static text comparison** of two source transcriptions — and `catalog.ex`'s own moduledoc (:17-30) DEFENDS the "deliberately NO catalog endpoint" design and accepts the drift as an honest fail-safe. A live `/connect/providers` route (V5's lean) would REVERSE that documented decision AND a `providers[]` on `/health` violates `webhook-server.ts`'s explicit "no provider list" invariant (:257-264). So: a NEW `scripts/connectors-catalog-drift-check.sh` (modeled on `connectors-ddl-drift-check.sh`, bash+awk, NO DB, `--selftest` tripwire) that extracts the connectable-provider SET from `catalog.ex` (`connect_mode` ≠ `nil`: paste ⇒ telegram/discord, oauth ⇒ slack) and from the bridge (paste providers declare `connect:` in `connectors/src/connectors/*.ts`; oauth providers have an `*-oauth.ts` callback) and reds on a NAMED diff, wired into `doc-gates.yml` (its `**/*.ts` + `**/*.exs` triggers trip on either source). This keeps catalog-drift **fully disjoint** from the headliner (it READS `catalog.ex` but edits only `scripts/` + `doc-gates.yml`) and matches `catalog.ex`'s design law. The filed task's "expose providers over loopback" AC is REWRITTEN to this static shape.
- **D76 [DECIDED — `connectors-telegram-token-shape-guard` = FINISH the filed task (#3200), NOT a guard from scratch.]** V4 resolved the two-survey contradiction: the task IS filed and github-synced (#3200), and the connect-time regex `if (!/^\d+:.+$/.test(botToken.trim()))` ALREADY shipped at `telegram.ts:85` (#3213) — its own comment (:78-84) names this task as the follow-up. The REMAINING scope is exactly three edits: (1) tighten `telegramBotIdFromToken` (`telegram.ts:145-151`, today only rejects an EMPTY first segment) to require a digits-only first segment; (2) DELETE the now-duplicated regex at `telegram.ts:85`, relying on the tightened function's throw surfaced as the same human-readable `reason` string (do NOT regress the connect-time UX to a raw stack trace); (3) add ONE test for a non-digit-prefixed token (`"not-a-token"` throws). Only 2 callers repo-wide (`telegram.ts:92`, `scripts/smoke-telegram.ts:155`) — small blast radius. **Fillers STAY backlog, NOT dispatched:** `connectors-webhook-mount-fanout` is UNFALSIFIABLE live (no second-replica deploy exists) and `connectors-telegram-poll-supervision` COLLIDES with this slice on `telegram.ts` — sequence, never co-dispatch.

### Wave 2026-07-14 — P5 (Wave 7) DECIDE — the epic's OTHER direction opens; the crux is PROVEN LIVE
Two verify rounds PROVED the fact the whole wave rested on: V1 RAN `claude 2.1.209` (the runner's real binary) and a mixed **stdio + http** `mcpServers` pair connects under `--strict-mcp-config`, both tools discoverable, the http tool round-tripping via `--print` — strict mode restricts SOURCES not COUNT, so a tool connector is mechanically a SECOND key in `mcp_config/1`'s map (D69). GitHub wins over Linear: hosted remote MCP with a static fine-grained PAT reuses the proven paste `connect_mode`; Linear's OAuth re-pays Slack's cost (D70). The D38 credential crux is settled by a PROVEN mechanism: `headersHelper` is real in the runner binary and fires at connect even without skip-permissions (the trust gate exempts `--mcp-config`-file servers), so the bridge injects `Authorization` and the PAT NEVER transits Elixir — arch D beats A/C/B/env-interp (D71). The tool install rides `connector_installs` with ZERO schema change (third secret kind, same seal, `chat_token_ref` NULL, direction on the registry) — the one real gap is a by-workspace query (D72). The seam: bridge returns non-secret descriptors over loopback, the subprocess fetches the secret via `headersHelper`, tool ticket carries a session-length TTL (D73). D24 scoping is NAMED not built; the live GitHub call is a one-inch human gate (a fine-grained read-only single-repo PAT); the stub proves the wire; never fabricate (D74). Catalog-drift is a STATIC gate matching `catalog.ex`'s no-endpoint law, not a live route (D75). Telegram-guard FINISHES the filed #3200 (tighten `telegramBotIdFromToken`, delete the duplicate regex, one test); the two fillers stay backlog (D76). Three opus slices, file-partitioned, true parallel; the headliner is ONE branch spanning both languages. Paper: `connectors-wave-7-2026-07-14`.

### Wave 2026-07-15 — P5 (Wave 7) BUILT + REVIEWED — the epic's OTHER direction ships; the wire is proven, no live install exists (grade A−)
The three slices landed green as they will merge. **Headliner `connectors-p4-tool-connectors-mcp` (branch `…tool-connectors-via-mcp-the-epic-s-other-0-r`)** delivers the crux with ZERO provider special-casing: a tool connector is a SECOND `mcpServers` key. Node — `Connector.toolDescriptor` + `registry.tools()`; `github.ts` (`direction:'tool'`, paste-PAT validate via `GET /user` → `install_key=login`, MCP at `api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, `adapterFactory` THROWS so it is never mounted as a channel); `installs.lookupByWorkspace` fail-closed; two loopback-only routes `/connect/tool-descriptors` + `/connect/tool-headers/:provider` (opaque 404 on `x-forwarded-*` and on GET); `connect` branches on `connector.direction` (a tool seals ONLY the PAT as the third secret kind through the SAME cipher, `chat_token_ref` NULL, no mount) — never on provider id. Elixir — `connect_ticket.sign_tool` (8h tool-session ticket mirroring the bridge constants); optional `bridge.fetch_tool_descriptors/1`; `claude_chat.mcp_config/2` folds descriptors via `Map.put_new` (cannot shadow the loopback `barkpark` server), `setup_mcp` mints a workspace-scoped tool ticket and fetches fail-soft (default instances short-circuit, ZERO HTTP). D38 held and PROVEN: a test runs the real `headersHelper` curl over loopback and gets back `{Authorization: Bearer <pat>}`; Elixir never decrypts. **`connectors-catalog-connectable-drift` (branch `…catalog-connectable-drift-static-gate-tr-1-r`)** — a DB-free static drift gate (bash+awk, `--selftest`) modeled on the DDL gate, wired into `doc-gates.yml`, honoring `catalog.ex`'s no-endpoint law. **`connectors-telegram-token-shape-guard` (branch `…telegram-token-shape-guard-finish-the-fi-2`, reviewer-unchanged)** — finished #3200: tightened `telegramBotIdFromToken` to `/^(\d+):.+$/`, deleted the duplicate connect-time regex, preserved the typed refusal via try/catch, added one test. **Reviewer fixed TWO defects both slice-gates missed, each of which would have RED a required CI gate:** (1) a dead `\\ []` default on `write_mcp_config/3` that reds `mix compile --warnings-as-errors` (the headliner's gate ran only one test file); (2) a latent cross-slice drift collision — `github.ts` carries a `connect:{` paste member, so once the headliner + drift-gate merge together the gate reds on `bridge implements github paste, catalog omits`; fixed by excluding `direction:'tool'` files from the channel-catalog set (a `both` connector still counts), with a new selftest case. Merged-tree verification: drift PASS + selftest, 424/424 vitest (real Postgres, zero skips), typecheck + format clean; Elixir 97/0 + clean `--warnings-as-errors`. **Ledger** honest — all three tasks `in_progress` (merge-gated), the headliner's real-claude `:real_binary` probe (criterion 1) correctly left `met=false` as the one-inch human gate, all three merge-gate criteria open for the lead. What the NEXT wave should take: **Linear as the SECOND tool connector** (proves the abstraction by repetition — OAuth this time, no core-loop change) + the **Studio tool-connector catalog card** (this wave was API/CLI-first) + a tool-set companion to the drift gate when that card lands. Standing human gate for a LIVE proof: a fine-grained, read-only, single-repo GitHub PAT — everything up to it is built and unit-proven; NEVER fabricate the live call. Paper: `connectors-wave-7-2026-07-14`.

## Wave 8 — decisions (D77–D88, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand; ALL PURE connectors/ Node/TS + bash, ZERO .ex)

Wave 8 makes the tool-connector abstraction TRUE instead of asserted: Linear lands as the SECOND MCP
tool connector, and its auth is OAuth — proving the tool seam survives a non-paste connect. Every
decision is grounded in run output recorded in `connectors-wave-8-2026-07-15`. The connectors gate was
re-proven green on origin/main at Decide (426 → 425/425, zero skips, real Postgres, ~17s wall) and the
vacuous-green failure mode (unreachable DB + no `CONNECTORS_REQUIRE_DB` → exit 0 with 83 silent skips)
was positively reproduced — the two env vars are both load-bearing, keep them.

- **D77 [DECIDED — Linear = the SECOND tool connector; OAuth-APP flow; pure Node; NO connect member.]** `connectors/src/connectors/linear.ts` mirrors `github.ts`: `direction:"tool"`, `toolDescriptor:{type:"http", url:"https://mcp.linear.app/mcp"}` (Linear's hosted Streamable-HTTP MCP explicitly accepts a static `Authorization: Bearer <token>` from "an existing Linear OAuth application without an extra authentication hop" — its docs, verbatim), `adapterFactory` THROWS, `resolveTenant` → null. Probe-compiled: `Connector.connect` is OPTIONAL (`types.ts:390 connect?:`), so Linear registers with NO connect member at all — no paste mode, no fake mode. OAuth-ONLY this wave: the personal-API-key alternative is REJECTED because Linear's GraphQL header format differs by credential kind (`Bearer <token>` for OAuth vs raw `<API_KEY>` for personal keys) and supporting both forks the header-building code for zero epic value. The merged Elixir injection consumes the resulting install with ZERO change — `mcp_config/2` folds descriptors keyed only on provider/type/url, and `tool-headers` prints `Bearer ${credentialRef}` provider-agnostically. ZERO .ex touched.
- **D78 [DECIDED — the OAuth callback writes the install DIRECTLY via `deps.upsertInstall`; NO pending-connect row.]** Slack's pending-connect row exists SOLELY to stage the workspace-bound CHAT token across the redirect (D48) — a TOOL install has no chat token (`chat_token_ref` NULL, `connect.ts:533-535`). So Linear's callback needs no pending row and no `consumePendingConnect` dep: the HMAC-SHA256 signed connect ticket riding `state` carries the WHOLE workspace binding. Callback order (mirrors `slack-oauth.ts` minus pending/mount): verify ticket BEFORE spending the code → exchange at `api.linear.app/oauth/token` (authorize is `linear.app/oauth/authorize` — DIFFERENT hosts, don't unify) → derive installKey → D64 incumbent-owner 409 BEFORE any write → ONE `upsertInstall` (`credentialRef` = sealed token, NO `chatToken` key) → NO mount (a tool is never a channel). Route mounting mirrors Slack's dedicated surface: new `linearOAuth?` deps on `WebhookServerOptions`, `isLinearOAuthCallbackRoute`, a fifth route block (public, GET, ticket-authenticated), deps wired in `index.ts` mirroring `:604-618` MINUS consumePendingConnect/mountInstall.
- **D79 [DECIDED — installKey = Linear `organization.id` (immutable); `urlKey` is display-only.]** The GraphQL analogue of GitHub's `GET /user` is the root Query field `organization { id urlKey }` (argument-free, one round trip; schema.graphql:38100). `Organization.id` is `ID!` and absent from `OrganizationUpdateInput`; `urlKey` IS in that input (admin-renameable) — an install key must never silently change under a workspace (D64 depends on it), so the key is `id`, `urlKey` feeds `displayName` only. This deliberately diverges from GitHub's login-as-key.
- **D80 [DECIDED — seal the BARE access_token as `credential_ref`; refresh is DEFERRED with a NAMED criterion.]** Storing `{access_token, refresh_token, expires_at}` JSON in `credential_ref` would make `tool-headers` (connect.ts:382, prints `Bearer ${credentialRef}` RAW) emit the blob as the bearer — breaking MCP auth unless the SHARED generic route learns to parse, which violates "second connector = zero core change", the very thing this wave proves. So: seal the bare access_token; NAMED consequence — Linear access tokens live ~24h (`expires_in: 86399`), an expired install opens fine and 401s invisibly AT THE PROVIDER until re-connected. Refresh is filed as its own backlog task (`connectors-linear-token-refresh`): Linear's refresh grant is well-specified (POST `grant_type=refresh_token`, refresh_token ROTATES per call — proven by two distinct sample tokens in Linear's own docs — with a 30-min replay grace window), buildable later without guessing.
- **D81 [DECIDED — `scope=read` only; PKCE OMITTED with a named rationale.]** Linear's docs: `read` is the always-present default and they discourage broad `write`. The stub proof is read-shaped. PKCE is optional-additive in Linear's flow ("To use this flow, you'll need to include two additional parameters") and the bridge is a confidential client (client_secret held server-side, code leg is server-to-server) — the interception threat PKCE targets does not apply on this path. Cheap to add later; omitted now.
- **D82 [DECIDED — the drift-gate oauth-loop fix is MANDATORY, ATOMIC with the linear files, and gets a NEW selftest case.]** PROVEN: `scripts/connectors-catalog-drift-check.sh`'s oauth loop (L106-113) lacks the tool-direction exclusion its paste loop got in W7 — a `linear-oauth.ts` reds the BLOCKING doc-gates drift check (`FAIL … bridge implements, catalog omits: linear oauth`, exit 1) even when `linear.ts` declares `direction:"tool"`. The fix (~8-10 lines of pure bash: skip `<prov> oauth` when `connectors/<prov>.ts` exists and declares `direction:"tool"`) is proven to green the real slice shape and stay a no-op on today's main. COUPLING: the exclusion only fires when the registry file EXISTS — so `linear.ts` + `linear-oauth.ts` + the script fix MUST land in ONE PR; splitting wall-blocks. AND the bundled `--selftest` is BLIND to this branch (passes identically with and without the fix) — the slice must add a companion selftest case (a tool+oauth pair the catalog omits), mirroring the W7 paste-exclusion case at script lines ~178-192/219-221.
- **D83 [DECIDED — mount-fanout = periodic DB reconcile in `index.ts` + a new module; LISTEN/NOTIFY evaluated and REJECTED this wave.]** Live probes on the pinned `pg` (8.22.0) proved LISTEN/NOTIFY works — and proved WHY not to build it now: (1) NOT DURABLE — a NOTIFY fired while no listener is connected is lost forever (proven), so a reconcile backstop is mandatory regardless; LISTEN/NOTIFY buys only latency on top of machinery you still need. (2) LISTEN needs a dedicated long-lived `pg.Client` (Pool recycling drops the registration) — a lifecycle class the codebase lacks. (3) PROVEN LANDMINE: that Client with no `error` listener CRASHES the whole Node process on any connection drop (uncaught error event, exit 1) — the exact hazard `db/pool.ts:65-70` documents as MANDATORY for Pool, never ported to a bare Client. The reconcile is a thin interval loop diffing `installs.listInstalls` against the mount index, mounting/unmounting via the EXISTING `addInstall`/`removeInstall` paths — no new Client, no crash class, and it does NOT touch `webhook-server.ts` (the opaque-404 miss path stays untouched per D39), killing the feared file collision with Linear's callback route. The stored criterion "a test with two bridges over the same database" is REWORDED to its honest satisfiable shape: two independently-constructed mount-index+reconcile instances in ONE test process over one real Postgres, with a row INSERTed via a separate pg connection (exactly how "another replica wrote it" looks) proven stale-then-routable, and the DELETE side proven to unmount. HONEST SCOPE: this fixes webhook-mount staleness ONLY — Discord double-posts (per-process socket Map), Telegram 409s on a second poller, iMessage same class; those are NOT replica-safe after this slice and are filed as `connectors-replica-socket-poll-safety`. If a future task picks LISTEN/NOTIFY for latency: dedicated Client (never the Pool), `error`+`end` handlers wired FIRST, reconcile kept as the backstop — skipping any one reintroduces a proven bug.
- **D84 [DECIDED — Telegram watchdog = interval health-check on `isPolling` + re-arm, PLUS retry-with-backoff on the INITIAL arm.]** The vendor `pollingLoop` self-heals (per-iteration try/catch, capped backoff) — but the adapter exposes NO event and NO promise (only the `isPolling` boolean getter; `pollingTask` is private), so Discord's race-a-promise `startSupervisedGateway` shape cannot be copied verbatim. The watchdog is an interval that reads `adapter.isPolling` and re-invokes `startPolling()` with gateway.ts-shaped knobs (minBackoff/maxBackoff/healthyAfter — shared supervisor vocabulary) when it unexpectedly reads false; an explicit `stopPolling` (unmount) DISARMS the watchdog — no zombie re-arm. Second gap closed: `startPolling()` can throw SYNCHRONOUSLY at mount (resetWebhook failure → `pollingActive=false` + rethrow), today permanently killing that install's polling with zero retry — wrap the initial arm in the same bounded backoff. `telegram.ts` + its test ONLY (sole telegram.ts toucher this wave); tests use the injectable-sleep/arm-counter pattern from `discord-connector.test.ts:345-492`, no real timers.
- **D85 [DECIDED — `connectors-tool-connector-studio-card` defers WHOLE; there is no Node half to carve.]** All three of its stored criteria name `catalog.ex`/`connectors_live.ex`/the Elixir Test gate. The strategize leaning "(e) carve the Node part" found NOTHING to carve — the honest action is defer the whole task until the Elixir gate is fixed, not ship a sliver. (Known landmine for that future wave, recorded now: `connectors_live.ex`'s OAuth render branch is Slack-hardcoded — flat `slack_add_url`/`slack_gate` assigns render for ANY `connect_mode: :oauth` provider — so a second OAuth provider in the Studio catalog needs per-provider-keyed assigns or Linear's card would show Slack's add-URL.)

Closed with rationale before Wave 8 appends to the same charter file — removing the cherry-pick conflict source.
- **D87 [CORRECTED — the Elixir gate FIRES on every PR; merge DISCIPLINE, not path filters, exempts pure-Node PRs.]** `elixir.yml` has NO path filter; it reds every Wave 8 PR with the inherited p0 break (task-8df445f5c4482c9f). But main has no branch protection (live-verified 404) and the precedent is explicit: #3366 merged with Test=FAILURE. The http entries were registered-not-handshaken (placeholder URLs by design); a live triple-CONNECT rides the same human gate as the live Linear call.

## Wave 9 — decisions (D89–D96, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand; ALL PURE connectors/ Node/TS, ZERO .ex)

- **D89 [DECIDED — dual-shape credential_ref: ONE generic read-path discriminator, additive forever.]** `toolHeaders()` (connect.ts:355-382, the SOLE runtime Bearer builder — verified; github.ts:129 is validate-only, untouchable) learns one shape-blind parse: trimmed value starts with `{` AND `JSON.parse` succeeds AND `.access_token` is a non-empty string → bundle (Bearer = `.access_token`); ANY other input — including malformed JSON — passes through byte-for-byte as the bare bearer, and the parse can NEVER throw on legacy input. GitHub's paste PAT stays byte-identical, pinned by `tool-descriptors.test.ts:303/:317` — PROVEN to bite (probe-break of connect.ts:382 reds exactly those two tests). Zero schema change: `credential_ref` stays `text`; installs.ts/rewrap.ts/credential-cipher.ts are shape-agnostic pass-throughs (verified), untouched.
- **D90 [DECIDED — bundle shape + trigger = header-build-time skew window; serve-stale-LOUD on failure; NO on-401 leg.]** Linear's OAuth callback (linear-oauth.ts:424) seals JSON `{access_token, refresh_token?, expires_at?}` — `expires_at` = now + `expires_in`*1000 (epoch-ms, the whatsapp-window shape); `LinearTokenResponse` gains `refresh_token` (untyped ANYWHERE today) and stops discarding `expires_in`. Refresh fires inside `toolHeaders` when `expires_at − now < LINEAR_TOKEN_REFRESH_SKEW_MS` (named constant, mirror `CONNECT_TICKET_MAX_SKEW_MS` style) or already past. Cadence is per MCP (re)connect — the helper is a curl the `claude` runner execs with a hard 10s timeout (D71), and ticket TTL 8h < token life 24h covers the common case (verified). The bridge only PRINTS headers into mcp_config, so a provider 401 is never observable at the bridge — there is NO on-401 leg; on refresh FAILURE the route serves the stored access_token AND logs loudly (named degraded mode): a failed refresh never turns a maybe-stale header into a hard connect failure.
- **D91 [DECIDED — write-back via `upsertInstall` (blind LWW), NOT a pinned CAS; in-process single-flight INVENTED.]** The refresh exchange is NON-REPLAYABLE (Linear rotates the refresh_token per call) — this inverts rewrap's safety logic: a pin-old-bytes UPDATE losing to a concurrent rewrap sweep would DISCARD the fresh bundle, leaving a stale bundle whose refresh_token was just consumed → dead install on next refresh. `upsertInstall` never bricks against the sweep (the fresh bundle always lands; the sweep's own pin then skips; a later sweep sees `alreadyCurrent`). The refresher passes the PLAINTEXT bundle JSON (upsertInstall does the ONE seal — pre-sealing would double-seal), SAME workspaceId, NO chatToken key (D52's CASE preserves the NULL chat_token_ref). AAD contradiction SETTLED: seal() always uses CURRENT_VERSION=2 → `bpc2|` automatically (`bpc1|` is legacy V1; the shallow reads saw a doc-comment) — re-seal through the existing cipher needs zero version handling. Primary same-process defense = a promise-cache single-flight keyed `(provider, install_key)` — NO prior art in the tree, invented this wave. Cross-replica double-exchange residual is exactly what Linear's 30-min replay grace models; the stub token endpoint tests BOTH branches (second-use-succeeds-within-grace AND second-use-rejected); the LIVE refresh rides `connectors-linear-live-oauth-gate`, never faked.
- **D92 [DECIDED — refresh capability is CONNECTOR-OWNED (registry hook), never a Linear field on shared ConnectDeps.]** New OPTIONAL member on the connector registry entry (`refreshCredential?(opened, ctx) → fresh plaintext bundle JSON | null`), implemented by new `connectors/src/oauth/linear-token-refresh.ts` (parse / needs-refresh / POST `api.linear.app/oauth/token` `grant_type=refresh_token` / return bundle). Linear's client creds thread through `registerBuiltinConnectors`' deps bag exactly as Slack's do: `linearApp = linearDepsFromEnv(env)` HOISTS from index.ts:718 to beside `slackApp` (:443) — before `registerBuiltinConnectors` (:445) and `connectDeps` (:643); the existing linearOAuth block keeps referencing the same now-earlier variable. `toolHeaders` calls the hook generically (`connector.refreshCredential?.()`) — the shared route stays provider-agnostic, satisfying the stored criterion; write-back needs NO new ConnectDeps surface (`deps.upsertInstall` already in scope with the full install object — verified).
- **D93 [DECIDED — lease = NEW table `chat_bridge.connector_install_leases`; ONE-statement atomic claim/renew/steal; owner = boot-time randomUUID.]** DDL appended to `BRIDGE_TABLE_DDL` — RUN-PROVEN gate-safe: the DDL drift gate scans ONLY `connector_installs` by hardcoded name (lease-table append stays green with ZERO test_helper.exs edit; negative control reds — not vacuous), the catalog gate ignores option fields; the wave's one genuinely gating unknown resolved GREEN, pure-Node premise confirmed. Columns mirror execution_lease.ex's VOCABULARY only (PK `(provider, install_key)`, `owner_id text`, `expires_at timestamptz`, `taken_at`/`renewed_at`) — its claim machinery does NOT exist to copy (epoch is a static fence set once; heartbeat is bulk host-scoped; NO steal-on-lapse anywhere in the tree), so the claim SQL is INVENTED: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (provider, install_key) DO UPDATE SET owner_id=EXCLUDED.owner_id, expires_at=EXCLUDED.expires_at WHERE connector_install_leases.owner_id = EXCLUDED.owner_id OR connector_install_leases.expires_at < now() RETURNING owner_id` — claim + renew + takeover-on-lapse in one atomic write; 0 rows = another replica holds it, stand by. Replica identity = `crypto.randomUUID()` minted once per process (container-safe; hostname collides across templated pods). LAW: the lease table stays bridge-internal, NEVER Elixir-read — a Studio read would reintroduce the D54 transcription cost (guard written into the task).
- **D94 [DECIDED — lease coordinator mirrors mount-reconcile verbatim; `listen()` lease-gated; `shutdown()` refactored through `takeDown`.]** New module (`connectors/src/tenant/install-lease.ts` + coordinator wiring in index.ts): side-effect-free construction, `start()`/`stop()`, UNREF'D interval, `inFlight` overlap guard, per-install error containment, `onError` reporting, on `createBridgePool` ONLY (mandatory `pool.on('error')`; LISTEN/NOTIFY stays REJECTED per D83). Scope = channel connectors WITHOUT `.webhook` — the exact complement of mount-reconcile's filter (Discord socket, Telegram poll; the two are reachable into bringUp only via boot loop + paste /connect — verified exhaustively). Each tick per install: claim → on acquire `listen()`; holder renews; on lose/lapse `stopListening()` (standby takeover). NEW FINDING FOLDED: `Bridge.shutdown()` (index.ts:805-816) BYPASSES `takeDown` — a duplicate inline teardown loop that would strand the lease until TTL lapse on every graceful restart. shutdown() is REFACTORED to call `takeDown()` per install, and takeDown releases the lease — one teardown path, immediate standby takeover on rolling restart.
- **D95 [DECIDED — honest replica-safety scope, named in module doc + PR.]** COVERED: Discord (Gateway socket — silent double-POST) + Telegram (getUpdates — 409 Conflict). NOT COVERED, NAMED: iMessage (same code shape, one-Mac self-hosted operator profile — rides the same lease path if ever registered, not built-for, not tested); webhook channels (Slack/Teams/WhatsApp) already replica-safe via W8 mount-reconcile. PROOF = two coordinators + two pools over ONE real Postgres (mount-reconcile.test.ts's exact ephemeral-DB harness): exactly-one-owner per install, standby takeover on lapse, release-on-shutdown — NEVER a live two-replica deploy (none exists; ZERO live installs remains the honest state).
- **D96 [DECIDED — wave hygiene: the tracked vitest-results.json landmine; co-dispatch build, sequence merge.]** `connectors/vitest-results.json` is git-TRACKED (committed at 02c28b7a8 BEFORE its .gitignore rule; gitignore is inert on tracked paths) and regenerates on every vitest run — the ONE shared touchpoint the file partition missed. BOTH builders: `git checkout -- connectors/vitest-results.json` before staging; NEVER commit it. Untracking filed as backlog (`connectors-untrack-vitest-results`). File partition otherwise CONFIRMED: only `index.ts` is shared, at disjoint regions — co-dispatch the BUILD, sequence the MERGE (second-to-land rebases; W8 precedent). Branch off origin/main `8b7482ebf`+ (local checkout is 2 commits stale). Merge past the two inherited main-reds (elixir.yml fires on every PR per D87; doc-gates' sole red is studio-link-lint on chat_live.ex:377 — merger confirms the ONLY red doc-gates step is that one, and that both connectors drift steps are green). EXPECTED test rewrites (not regressions): `linear-oauth.test.ts` :273 AND :461 become bundle-parse assertions (:273 is the upstream webhook-route sibling a builder fixing only :461 will miss), docstring :435 + stale comment :457-459 reworded, seedLinearInstall gets a SECOND bundle-shaped fixture so the bundle branch of the read path is actually exercised (the bare seed alone would mask it).

### Wave 2026-07-15 — Wave 9 DECIDE — the honest remainder is fully specified; every fork closed by run-proof, zero .ex
Two verify rounds closed every open fork (paper `connectors-wave-9-2026-07-15`): the one gating unknown — whether the DDL drift gate reds on a new bridge table — resolved GREEN by run (gate scans only `connector_installs` by hardcoded name; negative control bites), so the lease table is pure-Node by construction (D93); the bare-PAT pin BITES (probe-break of connect.ts:382 reds tool-descriptors :303/:317 — the compat green is not vacuous, D89); the refresh write-back goes through `upsertInstall` NOT a pinned CAS because a rotating refresh exchange is non-replayable and a lost pin would brick the install — single-flight promise-cache invented as the same-process defense (D91); the refresh hook is connector-owned via the registry so the shared tool-headers route stays provider-agnostic (D92); refresh triggers at header-build inside a skew window with serve-stale-LOUD on failure — the bridge can never see a provider 401 (D90); execution_lease.ex has NO steal-on-lapse machinery to copy (epoch = static fence) so the one-statement claim/renew/steal SQL is invented, owner = boot randomUUID (D93); `Bridge.shutdown()` was FOUND to bypass takeDown — refactored through it so a rolling restart releases the lease immediately (D94); scope honest: Discord+Telegram covered, iMessage named-out, webhooks already safe (D95); tracked `vitest-results.json` named as the one shared builder landmine, untrack task filed (D96). Two slices, both opus, co-dispatched build with sequenced merge on the one shared file. Gate baseline run-proven at Decide: 464/464 zero-skip on real Postgres, prettier, tsc, both drift gates + selftests. Paper: `connectors-wave-9-2026-07-15`.

### Wave 2026-07-15 — Wave 8 DECIDE — Linear is buildable, every fork closed by run-proof, zero .ex
Two verify rounds closed every open fork with run output (paper `connectors-wave-8-2026-07-15`): the minimal linear.ts (tool, no connect member) COMPILES against origin/main (tsc 0, negative-control proven); the drift-gate trap is REAL (linear-oauth.ts reds the blocking gate) and the 8-line bash exclusion greens it, but only atomically with the registry file — and the selftest is blind, so the slice adds a case (D82); the OAuth callback needs NO pending-connect row (nothing to stage for a chat-token-less tool install — D78); installKey = immutable `organization.id`, never the renameable urlKey (D79); refresh is deferred-with-named-criterion because refresh-JSON in credential_ref would break the RAW `Bearer ${credentialRef}` generic route — bare token sealed, 24h staleness named, refresh task filed (D80); scope=read, PKCE omitted as a confidential client (D81); mount-fanout is a periodic reconcile because LISTEN/NOTIFY was PROVEN non-durable across listener absence and PROVEN to crash the process without an error listener on a dedicated Client (D83) — and it stays out of webhook-server.ts, killing the Linear collision; the Telegram watchdog is an isPolling interval + initial-arm retry because the vendor adapter exposes no promise to race (D84); the Studio card defers WHOLE (all criteria are .ex — D85); stale PR #3285 closed as byte-identical to what main carries (D86); the Elixir red on every PR is inherited and merge-past-able by discipline, with connectors.yml + doc-gates.yml + pr-task-gate as the real bars (D87); and claude 2.1.210 accepts the exact THREE-server strict-mcp map the end state needs (D88). Three slices: Linear (gating, opus), mount-reconcile (sequenced after Linear on index.ts), telegram watchdog (parallel). Gates dry-run green at Decide: 425/425 zero-skip vitest on real Postgres, prettier, tsc, drift+selftest. Paper: `connectors-wave-8-2026-07-15`.

## Wave 10 — decisions (D97–D106, DECIDED with two verify rounds in hand; the epic's FIRST .ex wave — the Studio tool-connector catalog card)

Wave 10 is the connect-loop's LAST buildable piece: the Studio TOOL-connector catalog card (D85's deferred
task, now unblocked — the tenancy p0 is fixed). Every decision below is grounded in run output recorded in
`connectors-wave-10-2026-07-15`. Baseline dry-run at Decide on `05ba48f54` (== origin/main): connectors
drift gate + selftest PASS, studio-literal-check PASS (360 files), studio-link-lint PASS (+selftest bites),
`mix test connectors_live_test.exs install_schema_test.exs` → **35/35**.

- **D97 [CORRECTED — the merge ceiling is ONE inherited failure, not two.]** The wish's premise "main is red on exactly two failures" is HALF WRONG by fresh run-proof: the 3 most recent completed `elixir.yml` runs on main each show `27 doctests, 10976 tests, 1 failure` — always `Barkpark.Sites.DeployRunnerTest` `runtime_target` (deploy_runner_test.exs:581/593, byte-identical assertion). Golden-parity is GREEN in all 3 runs (`gen_golden_parity` regenerates 13×3 mirrors, `git diff --exit-code` passes silently, zero `::error::`). **THE MERGE CONTRACT: the failing set at merge must be EXACTLY `{deploy_runner runtime_target}` — nothing W10 adds.** Copying the stale two-item ceiling would let a REAL golden-parity regression slip through as "expected". The isolation constraint stands unchanged: never touch paper components, golden fixtures, deploy_runner, or any `/api` route (openapi drift-check runs BEFORE mix test; local regen OOMs). Lead re-checks `gh run list --workflow=elixir.yml --branch main` immediately before merge (freshest moves).
- **D98 [DECIDED — ONE slice, ONE PR; claim-and-WIDEN `connectors-tool-connector-studio-card` (#3287).]** The Studio surface is one coupled file family — `catalog.ex` + `connectors_live.ex` + `connectors_live_test.exs` + `scripts/connectors-catalog-drift-check.sh` (the script edit is ATOMIC with catalog.ex, D82 precedent: a split wall-blocks) + two small enablers (`bridge.ex` typespec widen, `connectors_bridge_stub.ex` direction-aware upsert). Never fan two builders onto connectors_live.ex. The existing open task is claimed and widened from its stale paste-only title to paste + OAuth + installed-list + disconnect (Linear landed OAuth-only in W8 after the task was filed at W7). Builder = opus (lead-pinned; two verify rounds made the slice fully specified).
- **D99 [DECIDED — catalog shape: a SEPARATE `@tool_providers` block (drift-gate variant B), never entries merged into `@providers`.]** RUN-PROVEN: folding github/linear into `@providers` false-reds the drift gate (`extract_catalog` is direction-blind — the naive-merge probe leaked `github paste linear oauth` into the channel set while the bridge correctly excludes them). The separate block keeps the existing channel awk byte-untouched (proven: channel extraction output identical with the block present). `@tool_providers` carries EXACTLY `github (connect_mode: :paste, connectable?: true)` + `linear (connect_mode: :oauth, connectable?: false — disjoint from paste, Slack's pattern)`, each `direction: :tool` — NEVER speculative future providers (a wired tool-set gate reds immediately on any catalog-only entry). Public surface: new `Catalog.tool_providers/0`; `Catalog.providers/0` stays channel-only (keeps the two `providers()` test loops at :134/:159 valid without direction filtering); `Catalog.provider/1`/`connectable?/1` cover both lists; new `Catalog.direction/1` → `:tool | :channel`.
- **D100 [DECIDED — drift gate = variant B: purely ADDITIVE `extract_catalog_tools()`/`extract_bridge_tools()` + a `CATALOG_TOOL_SET` vs `BRIDGE_TOOL_SET` comparison, with NEW biting selftest cases.]** Run-proven at Verify against the real tree: tool sides today are `[] vs [github paste, linear oauth]` — exactly the gap the catalog entries close; agreeing sets → green; a catalog that drops linear while `linear-oauth.ts` exists → red. Variant A (direction-flag state machine inside the existing extractors) works but touches FOUR live, green, CI-blocking code paths and adds a fragile entry-boundary invariant — REJECTED. The bundled selftest is BLIND to the tool branch (D82's lesson, verbatim): the slice adds cases proving (1) the channel set is byte-unchanged by a `@tool_providers` block, (2) agreeing tool sets green, (3) a dropped tool entry reds.
- **D101 [DECIDED — `do_connect` branches on direction; the tool arm mints NO chat token and passes `nil` to the bridge.]** Today's `do_connect` unconditionally mints a channel-style chat token that the bridge IGNORES for tools — a minted-then-orphaned workspace credential on every GitHub connect. RUN-PROVEN (throwaway vitest probe driving `handleConnectRequest`): a `direction:"tool"` connect with `chat_token` absent AND with `chat_token:null` both return `200 {ok:true, mounted:false}`, write the install with `credentialRef` set and NO `chatToken` key, and never call `mountInstall` (`connect.ts:560/:568/:624/:632-643`; `stringField` maps absent+null→null). The fix: `do_connect` dispatches on `Catalog.direction(provider)`; `connect_tool` (~12 lines) skips `Auth.create_chat_token`, calls `Connectors.bridge().connect(ticket, credential, nil)`, keeps the 3-branch result handling (match/mismatch/error — no revoke arms, nothing was minted); the channel arm is today's body verbatim. This is the wave's ONE legitimate sliver of new connect logic. Enablers: widen `bridge.ex:35`'s callback spec to `String.t() | nil` (dialyzer honesty); make the stub's upsert direction-aware (`chat_token_ref` NULL for tools — today it hardcodes 'SEALED-CHAT-TOKEN', which would make the protective test assert a lie). `revoke_install_tokens` no-ops safely for tools (generic label clause + `live_tokens_by_label` → `[]` → "0 tokens revoked", proven by reading auth.ex:348-363).
