posted-guard re-verified live at index.ts:320/329/348), `connectors-p4-cross-channel-identity` (D58: InboundEvent carries no sender id), `connectors-transcript-reseed-fallback` (AC demands amending the never-store-the-transcript boundary first), `connectors-cloud-sandbox-reaper` (D183 re-verified: teardownSandbox :348-359 turn-scoped only, +36 uniform drift from D183's cites, no reap-by-id mode in 717 lines), `connectors-cloud-interactive-multiturn` (no stdin/control channel into a one-shot sandbox). **2 process gates** — `connectors-chat-token-mint-capabilities-entry` (D162: openapi regen OOMs locally; elixir.yml:177-190 still hard-fails on diff; the CI-artifact recipe stays wave-banned), `bp-task-stamp-select-by-criterion-text` (D184's TRUE blockers re-verified byte-for-byte: stamp.ex:90/113-115 int-index-only, mcp_tasks.go:530/545-548/602-604 hard-requires int, AC2's literal server 409). The adversarial hunt (buildable-residue-hunt + closeout-evidence-pack, both with fresh greps/curls) COULD NOT break the mined-out claim. FINAL: after W20-1 merges, the epic's buildable-offline work is COMPLETE — 22 open children remain, every one gated; no further offline hardening wave gets cut without new external evidence (a credential arriving, a design ratified, or a real defect report).
- **D188 [DECIDED — recordInbound error semantics BOUND: awaited catch-and-swallow inside dispatchInbound; unbound by D178 and the task ACs until now.]** The write sits BEFORE the credential/session/turn steps, so an unguarded throw from recordInbound (Postgres down, pool exhausted) would propagate to mountInstall's handle() try/catch (index.ts:322-357), post the failure notice, and suppress the real reply — persistence infrastructure taking down the reply path, directly falsifying "replies were never at risk". And a bare fire-and-forget `.catch(()=>{})` in the onAck style (index.ts:337) races the test's waitUntil drain — an un-awaited write can still be in flight at read time, a flaky green. BINDING: `try { await deps.recordInbound?.(…) } catch { /* warn log */ }` — awaited, swallowed, before routedInstall (:200). A second test proves a REJECTING recordInbound still yields outcome `['posted']`. Added as a fifth AC on the task.
- **D189 [BANKED — the red probe is RUN-PROVEN on pure main; the adapter-spy law is mandatory; baseline corrected 515→517.]** The composed wiring test (ack-first's real mountInstall + HMAC Meta webhook × whatsapp-window-pg's throwaway-DB restart pattern) TYPECHECKS on unmodified origin/main (`npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0) and REDS for exactly the right reason — `expected null to be 1752451200000` at the store assertion with both harness-sanity assertions (turn ran, outcome=['posted']) passing first. THE TRAP (hit and de-risked at Verify): without `vi.spyOn(mounted.adapter, "startTyping"/"postMessage")` after mount, the real adapter POSTs to the dead Graph, MSW's error strategy throws, and the test reds pre-wiring AND would green post-wiring for a NON-STORE reason — a vacuous pass; the spies are mandatory in the real test. The full probe file is banked VERBATIM in the task description (and the wave Paper); hook shape FINAL: flat `DispatchDeps.recordInbound?(workspaceId, threadId, atMs)` (task self-adjudicates; file idiom confirms; the windowStore sub-object sketch is dead). Full-suite baseline is 517 tests / 0 skipped across 34 files (run-proven twice; the charter's 515 was stale by +2, provenance unknown, not load-bearing). Zero anchor drift 227619ba9→origin/main for connectors/ + scripts/connectors/ (empty diff).
- **D190 [EXECUTED AT DECIDE — ledger hygiene WIDENED beyond D186 and closed; the sealed-ledger write path is PROVEN, with a syntax law.]** PROVEN at Verify by executing D186(a) live: guerrilla ACCEPTS `bp doc patch task <id>` + `bp doc publish` against lifecycle=done tasks — sealed `task-6672a70bea19dc11` crit-4 restamped citing PR #3028/3cbbe26bf (read-back 5/5, crits 0-3 byte-identical) and TTFB `task-680d3f4abf20964d` crit-3 restamped citing PR #3824/ca4f6f6bf (4/4, attempts preserved). SYNTAX LAW: the `--set` key is FLAT (`acceptance_criteria:=…`) — a `content.`-prefixed key silently creates a bogus literal field (dry-run-proven); `bp doc get` returns fields at root while `bp task get` nests them under doc.content; `--file` on doc patch is still the W18 stub — pass `--set key:=json` via argv (subprocess, never shell `$(cat)` — template literals expand). WIDENED at Decide: the two PR #3832 merge gates restamped (`connectors-oauth-return-live-refresh` → 5/5, `connectors-cloud-claude-version-pin` → 3/3, evidence 30c48d196); `task-fe78ccb664308739` crit-0 REWORDED to the shipped D109/D127 deliberate-divergence contract (met stays FALSE, W15-3's refusal-to-fabricate note preserved + lead note appended) — D186(b) executed; `connectors-ledger-hygiene-w19` claimed, both criteria stamped with revs, CLOSED done by connectors-w20-decide. The strategize direction's "TTFB lead-close" action DROPPED — found already closed (close_reason cites merged #3824). DISCOVERED + FILED STANDALONE: `bp-task-ls-pagination-stalled` (page 2 repeats page 1; --all errors pagination_stalled; workaround = the epic's children[] field) — deliberately NOT an epic child: a CLI bug is out-of-epic scope, and parenting it would falsify D187's exhausted count.

## Wave 21 — decisions (D191–D198, DECIDED — DESIGN WAVE: run-secrets per-workspace scoping (D9) ratified with 4 verify assignments carrying live Postgres run-proofs; one build slice)

- **D191 [DECIDED — surrogate-PK two-tier schema; the strategize lean "additive column, NOT a PK rewrite" is RETRACTED as self-contradictory.]** Verify proved (pk-fork-necessity ruling + live migration probe) that a Postgres PK is total — keeping `name` as PK forbids the very `UNIQUE(workspace_id,name) WHERE NOT NULL` arm the direction leaned on, AND ships a cross-tenant ENUMERATION ORACLE (ws-A's `put("slack_bot_token")` collides with ws-B's globally-unique name → A learns B holds it — the exact non-enumeration property this wave exists to build). RATIFIED SHAPE: `ALTER TABLE secrets DROP CONSTRAINT secrets_pkey`; add surrogate `id :binary_id` PK (backfill `gen_random_uuid()` — built-in PG13+; prod's single real row `ingest_token` auto-backfills and stays global); `name` demoted to ordinary NOT NULL column; nullable `workspace_id`; two partial unique indexes — `secrets_global_name_index UNIQUE(name) WHERE workspace_id IS NULL` (byte-identical to today's uniqueness for the global arm) and `secrets_workspace_name_index UNIQUE(workspace_id,name) WHERE workspace_id IS NOT NULL` (per-tenant arm, same name may coexist per workspace + global). NO backfill of workspace_id — every existing row IS genuinely instance-global. Data-nondestructive: zero rows deleted, zero values touched. ALL mechanics run-proven live at Verify: migration 0.0s clean; coexistence count=3 (ws_a+ws_b+global same name); dup rejection per arm; `psql \d secrets` shows PK(id) + both partial indexes. Migration timestamp must be > `20260716120000` — builder re-checks `ls api/priv/repo/migrations | tail` immediately before writing the file (concurrent waves collide). NOTE: no fork here is "D9 wrong" — the flat-global premise held at every layer (name-PK migration 20260629140000, unfiltered list/0, flat `:require_admin`, no workspace dim in secrets_audit).
- **D192 [DECIDED — FK to workspaces, `on_delete: :delete_all`, on BOTH secrets.workspace_id and secrets_audit.workspace_id.]** The two in-repo precedents genuinely disagree and the assigned verifier read both rationales: data_keys workspace attribution (20260714010000) uses `references(:workspaces, type: :binary_id, on_delete: :delete_all)` — and data_keys is the closer sibling (encrypted, key-adjacent store); the E1 cascade law (20260527170000) put 21 FKs across 7 tables incl. mutation_events + paper_events on delete_all — FK+cascade is the codebase MAJORITY law. chat_sessions' no-FK is the deliberate minority exception for data with standalone retention value; a LIVE CREDENTIAL has zero legitimate reason to outlive its tenant — a never-cascaded orphan encrypted secret is a growing forgotten-secret surface (still crackable if the Cloak key ever leaks). Isolation itself is FK-independent (the scope law is pure SQL equality), so this is lifecycle hygiene, decided on hygiene grounds. CONSEQUENCE for tests: isolation tests create REAL workspaces via `Barkpark.TenancyFixtures.create_workspace!/0-1`, never bare `Ecto.UUID.generate()` (the FK rejects garbage ids — the Verify probe used bare UUIDs pre-FK; the real test must not). There is no "audit outlives tenant" carve-out anywhere in this codebase to imitate; secrets_audit cascades too.
- **D193 [DECIDED — two explicit tiers, scope-arg threading (studio_chat precedent 1:1), fail-closed, no silent cross-tier fallback.]** Every `Barkpark.Secrets` public function gains a `scope` argument: `:global` (default — every existing caller byte-identical) | workspace_id binary. Private 3-clause gate mirrors `scope_sessions/2` (studio_chat.ex:89-94): `:global` → `WHERE workspace_id IS NULL` (NOT untouched — the global tier is now an explicit arm, never all-rows); binary → `WHERE workspace_id == ^ws`; anything else (nil) → `where(query, false)` / `:not_found` / `[]` — fail-closed per `scope_to_workspace/3` law (scope.ex:118, tenancy.md:52). Scoped reads NEVER see NULL-owner globals (no `OR is_nil` carve-out — instance creds must not leak to tenants; the D35-class NULL-owner leak); global reads NEVER see scoped rows (no tenant shadowing of `ingest_token`). `get/1` therefore stays global-tier-pinned in the SAME slice as the migration — safe: its SOLE caller repo-wide is `ingest_token/0` (require_ingest_token.ex:45), structurally instance-global, checked before any workspace resolution exists. `put` stamps workspace_id from scope (`:global` → NULL). `list(:global)` lists global-only; `list(ws)` lists that workspace only. Failure direction of a forgotten scope arg: reads/writes the GLOBAL tier — never a foreign tenant (the chat_sessions-ratified direction).
- **D194 [DECIDED — the three upsert/changeset corrections, all RUN-PROVEN at Verify; the bug class has a named in-repo precedent.]** (1) Today's `conflict_target: :name` RAISES Postgrex 42P10 once the PK drops (proven live) — `put` branches by tier on the partial-index arbiter: global → `conflict_target: {:unsafe_fragment, "(name) WHERE workspace_id IS NULL"}`; workspace → `{:unsafe_fragment, "(workspace_id, name) WHERE workspace_id IS NOT NULL"}` (both proven idempotent, value v1→v2, count stays 1). (2) `on_conflict` NARROWS from `{:replace_all_except, [:name]}` to `{:replace, [:value, :updated_at, :updated_by]}` — never replace id/name/workspace_id on rotation (precedent bug: `connectors-upsert-install-conflict-wipes-sibling-secret` — unconditional ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE wiping a sibling secret column). (3) `SecretRecord.changeset` MUST declare BOTH named unique_constraints (`unique_constraint(:name, name: :secrets_global_name_index)` AND `unique_constraint(:name, name: :secrets_workspace_name_index)`) — proven: without them a dup INSERT raises Ecto.ConstraintError (a 500), with them `{:error, changeset}` (a 409). Not optional.
- **D195 [DECIDED — secrets_audit gains the workspace dimension; action vocabulary unchanged.]** Nullable `workspace_id :binary_id` + FK delete_all (D192) + new index `[workspace_id, inserted_at]` (mirrors existing `[name, inserted_at]`). The 3-value action vocab (set/reveal/delete, secret_audit.ex:22) stays closed — NULL vs non-NULL workspace_id already carries the tier distinction. `log_audit` stamps the acting scope on every reveal/set/delete.
- **D196 [DECIDED — named residuals + explicit exclusions; zero scope creep.]** (a) THE WALL IS SQL-ONLY, NOT CRYPTOGRAPHIC: Cloak AES-GCM's AAD is the hardcoded constant `"AES256GCM"` (deps/cloak/lib/cloak/ciphers/aes_gcm.ex:12) — a raw-SQL row-swap of workspace_id decrypts cleanly under the other tenant; ciphertext carries no proof of owner. Named residual, kept (Cloak EncryptedBinary stays this wave); the FieldCipher/DataKeys scope-as-AAD upgrade (real crypto binding, D51-D54 machinery already exists) is FILED as `connectors-secrets-aad-binding`. (b) The W6 "per-workspace DEK" is Node-only `credential-cipher.ts`, deliberately walled off by D38 ("no Elixir decrypt is ever needed") — a CLOSED DOOR, not a reuse target; the Elixir analog is DataKeys/FieldCipher. (c) `plugin_settings` stays instance-global BY DESIGN (singleton plugin config: one GitHub App, one Bokbasen, one Indx per instance) — a named exclusion, not a latent leak. (d) NO consumer reads run-secrets scoped today (the Cloud runner bypasses by D23/D110; sole get/1 caller is global ingest_token) — this wave must never imply the runner consumes the scoped store; first-scoped-consumer FILED as `connectors-secrets-first-scoped-consumer`.
- **D197 [DECIDED — S2 (scoped-admin HTTP surface) is a FOLLOW-UP, provably not landable this wave.]** The codebase's ONE proven pattern for per-workspace admin CRUD is a `scope "/w/:workspace_slug/p/:project_slug"` block on `[:scoped_api, :scoped_admin]` (5 sibling precedents: schemas, webhooks, token mint, chat-token mint, media search-admin) — net-new router.ex lines, and router.ex is off-limits this wave. The in-controller workaround (derive workspace inside SecretController) would hand-reimplement ResolveWorkspace + RequireWorkspaceRole without their guarantees — a worse shape, declined. FILED `connectors-secrets-scoped-admin-http` (also owns the bp `secret.*` manifest auth_tier decision — today blanket `admin` in capabilities.ex; flip-to-`scoped_admin` vs scoped-variants-alongside decided THERE). `task-5766dc5ca985ddc8`'s criterion 1 is AMENDED accordingly: the "scoped-admin gate replaces flat :require_admin" clause moves to the follow-up; W21 delivers the context-layer wall (schema + scoped read/write + isolation proof). `/v1/secrets` + `bp secret *` remain global-tier admin surfaces, functionally unchanged, until the follow-up.
- **D198 [DECIDED — knob-4 wording law: D9 landing does NOT unblock knob-4; cite D148, never D125.]** The ledger's own task-fe78ccb664308739 attempt history still carries the STALE W12 phrase "stays D9-gated (D125)" — but D148 (later, controlling) corrected it: the real blockers are the D132/D133 credential-config-embed design gap + the HARDCODED NON-OVERRIDABLE `~w(read write)` mint permissions (auth.ex:678/731) + the fact that NO grant object exists anywhere to correlate against (connector_installs has zero permission/scope/grant field). Honest claim after W21 lands: "D9 removed one necessary-but-not-sufficient precondition for knob-4; the sufficient blockers remain open." NO new knob-4 task is filed — it would duplicate task-fe78ccb664308739 crit-2 + the open `connectors-cloud-credential-config-embed`. Any future wave that touches knob-4 quotes D148's correction verbatim.

**W21 OPERATIONAL LAWS (from the Verify probe's lived failure):** the builder NEVER runs the real migration against the shared `barkpark_test` in place — `MIX_TEST_PARTITION=<unique>` gives a dedicated DB and the `test` mix alias auto-runs ecto.create+migrate (proven at Decide: 21/21 green on a fresh `barkpark_test_w21decide`). `mix ecto.rollback` REPORTED success in the probe but did NOT revert the DDL — verify schema state via `psql \d secrets`, never trust the rollback log. CI is a real migration exerciser: elixir.yml runs ecto.create+ecto.migrate against postgres:15 before mix test (lines 174-175/243); CI does NOT exercise the migration's `down` — write it carefully, it ships unproven.

### Wave 2026-07-17 — Wave 21 DECIDE — the design wave that D187 called for: D9's tenant wall ratified from live Postgres proofs, one fable slice, three honest follow-ups
Four verify assignments ALL carried run output (paper `connectors-wave-21-2026-07-17`): the entire build mechanism proven live in a fresh worktree — surrogate-PK migration migrates 0.0s clean, both partial unique arms enforce, today's `conflict_target: :name` raises 42P10 (the unsurveyed trap, REAL), the working unsafe_fragment incantations upsert idempotently, and the missing-unique_constraint 500-vs-409 trap surfaced and closed (D194); the PK fork ruled REQUIRED by the isolation capability's own non-enumeration property — no connector consumer forces it (credential_ref holds sealed bytes inline, the run-secret-name pointer is an explicit hypothetical; schema.ts:87-90) but the strategize leans 1+2 were mutually exclusive and lean 1 lost (D191); the FK fork settled FK+delete_all on the data_keys/E1-majority precedent with chat_sessions' no-FK understood as the retention-value exception secrets don't merit (D192); the ledger pinned knob-4's truth — task-5766dc5ca985ddc8's own ACs carry zero knob-4 wording, and D148 (not D125) is the controlling record: D9 is necessary-but-not-sufficient (D198); runway clean (no rival PR, no local diff, task unclaimed, D191 slot free); Cloak's hardcoded `"AES256GCM"` AAD makes the wall SQL-only — named residual, filed, not silently absorbed (D196). ONE build slice on FABLE (PK-drop on the prod secrets table + fail-closed two-tier semantics), gate dry-run green at Decide on a dedicated partition DB (12/12 + 21/21). Three follow-ups filed: scoped-admin HTTP (router-wave, D197), AAD binding (D196a), first scoped consumer (D196d). Paper: `connectors-wave-21-2026-07-17`.

### Wave 2026-07-17 — Wave 21 BUILT + REVIEWED — the run-secrets tenant wall SHIPS; D9's design gate closes with a red-first isolation proof (grade A)
One slice, one fable builder, reviewed as the last hands before merge in a fresh worktree off the built commit (deea1129b). **W21-1 `task-5766dc5ca985ddc8` — SHIPPED, grade A.** The gate re-ran GREEN on the reviewer's final state — **31 tests, 0 failures** (12 secrets_test back-compat + 10 isolation + 9 secret_controller) on a dedicated partition DB that auto-created + migrated the surrogate-PK schema clean; `mix format --check-formatted` passes on all five changed files; **no code fixes needed** — the slice landed correct and byte-identical for every pre-W21 caller (get/2, list/1, reveal/put/delete default `scope: :global`), so the final branch is the builder's original (no `-r` branch). What landed: migration 20260717070000 (DROP secrets_pkey → surrogate `id :binary_id` PK w/ gen_random_uuid backfill; nullable `workspace_id` FK→workspaces on_delete :delete_all on secrets AND secrets_audit; two partial unique indexes; data-nondestructive, no backfill); `Barkpark.Secrets` threads scope through every public fn behind a fail-closed 3-clause `scope_secrets/2` gate (`:global`→explicit IS NULL arm, binary→equality, else→`where(false)`); `fetch/2` replaces every Repo.get-by-name; `put` branches the upsert arbiter on the tier's partial index via `{:unsafe_fragment,…}` with `on_conflict {:replace,[:value,:updated_at,:updated_by]}`; SecretRecord declares BOTH named unique_constraints (dup→409 not 500); ingest_token/0 stays global-pinned. **RED-FIRST proof is real**: the core leak assertion red on unmodified a7cd8650c for the STORE reason (`reveal(scope: ws_b.id)` returned `{:ok,"ws-a-value"}`), green after the wall — the isolation test's assertions are load-bearing (cross-tier not_found/nil, coexistence count=3, per-tier dup→changeset, audit workspace_id stamping). **Ledger audit: exemplary, no fixes** — task in_progress DONE-pending-merge, crits 0-3 met with run-evidence, crit 4 (MERGE GATE) met=False empty, correctly left for the LEAD; three follow-ups filed at Decide all exist + open. **Grade held to A (not A+)**: the wall is SQL-only by explicit decision (Cloak AAD constant — filed `connectors-secrets-aad-binding`), so per-workspace isolation is not yet cryptographic; the `down` migration ships unproven (CI runs `up` only) and deletes scoped rows (documented, low-risk); read paths take scope as a raw binary, so the scoped-admin follow-up MUST guard request input with `Ecto.UUID.cast` (binary_id CastError gotcha) — none are defects in what shipped, all are honestly-named boundaries. **NEXT WAVE**: `connectors-secrets-scoped-admin-http` (p2) is now the ONE named exception to D187's exhausted-vein statement — design-ratified (D197), offline-buildable in a wave allowed to touch router.ex; guard scope input with Ecto.UUID.cast. Then first-scoped-consumer (p3) and aad-binding (p3). Knob-4 stays blocked (D148, NOT D125). LEAD must close crit 4 on merge. Paper: `connectors-wave-21-2026-07-17`.
## Wave 22 — decisions (D199–D204, DECIDED with 5 verify assignments ALL carrying run-proofs; the router.ex wave — the W21 wall gets its DOOR (scoped-admin HTTP + CLI) and its PHYSICS (AAD binding); the consumer honestly DEFERRED)

- **D199 [DECIDED — the scoped door: ONE sibling router block; SecretController serves BOTH routes; scope resolved ONCE via `Repo.uuid_or_nil/1` up front; garbage scope = opaque 404, NEVER a 500.]** New `scope "/w/:workspace_slug/p/:project_slug"` block on `[:scoped_api, :scoped_admin]` mounting the SAME `SecretController` (WebhookController's one-controller-both-routes precedent; note ScopeHelpers' keyword shape mismatches Secrets' bare `:scope` opt — the controller translates at ONE seam). Guard mechanics RUN-PROVEN at Verify: ALL FIVE Secrets verbs raise `Ecto.Query.CastError` at secrets.ex:228 on a non-UUID binary scope (probe 6/6 green, incl. put via do_put→fetch — put does NOT self-protect garbage binaries); `Repo.uuid_or_nil("not-a-uuid")==nil` folds into scope_secrets' fail-closed arm (get→nil, list→[], reveal/delete→not_found, put→:invalid_scope). LANDMINE (proven, load-bearing): `{:error, :invalid_scope}` is a LIVE 500 on both paths — `Content.Errors.build/1` has no clause (atom misses the is_binary arm at errors.ex:495, hits catch-all :498 → internal_error 500, test-proven errors_test.exs:60-67) and `SecretController.update/2` has no matching clause (CaseClauseError). THEREFORE the controller resolves scope up front — `case Repo.uuid_or_nil(ws.id) do nil -> {:error, {:not_found, "secret not found"}}; uuid -> …scope: uuid end` — so garbage yields 404 on EVERY verb including create and `:invalid_scope` never reaches the wire. Do NOT copy Token/ChatToken's nil-workspace 422 shape (wrong contract for opaque-404, and dead code — ResolveWorkspace always assigns a DB-resolved struct or halts). RED-FIRST protective test = controller-level FORGED-ASSIGNS (forge `assign(conn, :current_workspace, %Tenancy.Workspace{id: "not-a-uuid", …})` — no live HTTP path can produce a bad UUID, so the guard is defense-in-depth and the red test must forge, never fuzz): 500 on naive passthrough → 404 guarded. Context-level CastError witness tests (green today, documenting the context contract) land alongside. Cross-tenant red-first: ws-B workspace-admin listing/revealing/mutating ws-A's secrets via the scoped route → opaque 404/empty; flat `/v1/secrets` stays byte-identical global-tier admin (no scope opt → `:global` default — zero flat-controller changes needed for back-compat).
- **D200 [DECIDED — CLI manifest: FOUR NEW scoped verb ids with slugs BAKED into `http.path_template`; token.create's flat-template+`scoped_prefix` shape REJECTED as proven-broken; flat `secret.*` entries byte-identical.]** Run-proven: `ScopedMirror` is globally inert (url.go:39 gates the prepend; ZERO production setters — grep-proven), so token.create's shape dispatches to the FLAT URL, which does NOT exist server-side (router.ex registers `POST /v1/tokens` ONLY inside the scoped block) — `bp token create` 404s LIVE (dry-run URL flat despite -w/-p flags; real attempt 404; curl with valid admin bearer 404 identical → route-miss, not auth). That exact bug is independently pre-filed (`bp-token-create-scoped-prefix-404`, #3197, site-spawner epic) — NOT fixed this wave (outside the wish's scope), but its pattern is BANNED for the new verbs. The safe shape is workspace.project-create / cycle.show: bake `/w/:workspace_slug/p/:project_slug` directly into path_template — run-proven to resolve from ctx.Workspace/Project defaults AND explicit arg override (probe tests green; full internal/manifest suite green; go build+vet clean). New verbs: `secret.scoped-ls` / `secret.scoped-get` / `secret.scoped-set` / `secret.scoped-rm`, auth_tier `scoped_admin`, NO scoped_prefix key, ZERO Go changes (resolvePlaceholder already handles both slugs). A literal in-place tier flip of the existing 4 ids is DEAD — one verb id cannot carry two auth semantics/paths, and the flat surface must stay byte-identical. Enumeration for the record: 42 commands carry scoped_prefix; 41 are safe (flat route genuinely exists); token.create is the sole scoped-only exception.
- **D201 [DECIDED — AAD slice = OPTION C: scoped-rows-only FieldCipher/DataKeys; the global tier stays Cloak BYTE-IDENTICAL; workspace bound via per-(workspace,scope) DEK SELECTION, never stuffed into the AAD string; NO migration, NO tenancy-catalog edit.]** Premise run-proven: the swap-attack (`Repo.update_all` relocating a scoped row's workspace_id to ws-B) reveals ws-A's plaintext under ws-B TODAY — probe 2/2 green, and mutating the leak assertion to the post-fix form reds with the leaked value (non-vacuous). The leak reaches BOTH `reveal/2` and the cheap `get/2` resolver — the fix seals both. Zero scoped rows exist in prod (exhaustive grep: exactly 2 production callers of Barkpark.Secrets, neither passes scope) → nothing to re-encrypt, nothing to dual-read. Option A (one-shot re-encrypt) REJECTED — needs live-prod ingest_token coordination, not offline-provable; option B (dual-read) REJECTED — no Elixir precedent (the bpc1|/bpc2| seam is Node-only, D38 closed door). MECHANISM: `SecretRecord.value` comes off the automatic `Barkpark.EncryptedBinary` Ecto type (Ecto applies ONE type per field — scoped rows cannot silently take a different cipher); the Secrets context does an explicit per-tier codec — GLOBAL rows keep the Cloak/Vault path byte-identical on disk (Vault.encrypt/decrypt is exactly what EncryptedBinary delegated to), SCOPED rows store the FieldCipher envelope via `FieldCipher.encrypt(value, "run-secrets", workspace_id)`; reads branch on the row's workspace_id (nil → Cloak, non-nil → FieldCipher); ANY decrypt failure → :error → opaque not_found/nil. The workspace binding is DEK selection (crypto_test's proven per-workspace-isolation mechanism, 28/28 green); FieldCipher's crypto AAD stays the scope string — do NOT conflate. Red test = the DELIVERED swap-attack probe (banked at the scratchpad paths named in the wave Paper) with its two leak assertions FLIPPED to `{:error, :not_found}`/nil at land time; the own-scope survivor assertion holds in both worlds. MANDATORY regression: a pre-existing Cloak-format GLOBAL row still round-trips post-refactor (ingest_token is live prod auth).
- **D202 [DECIDED — `connectors-secrets-first-scoped-consumer` DEFERRED with evidence; task stays OPEN as a consumer-arrival gate, re-annotated, NOT built and NOT cancelled.]** Exhaustive caller walk (grep of every Barkpark.Secrets call site on origin/main): exactly 2 production callers — SecretController (the CRUD surface itself) and RequireIngestToken via global-pinned get/1. Every named candidate rejected with evidence: studio_chat remote_secrets = in-memory turn-scoped GenServer, zero relation to the store; site-deploy env = request-body closed allow-list, wiring Secrets in = NEW product design, not a missing reader; webhook signing = per-Webhook-document field + `media_signing_secret` app-config, neither Secrets-backed; github plugin creds = plugin_settings instance-global BY DESIGN (D196c); connectors HMAC = D37/D38 wall; ANTHROPIC_API_KEY = D110/D23 ban. There is no "need exists, only the reader is missing" case — a manufactured consumer is worse than none (D196d predicted this). The task re-opens for building the day a real feature genuinely reads a workspace-scoped secret; until then it counts among the epic's gated children (arrival gate).

## Wave 23 — decisions (D205–D207, DECIDED with 5 verify assignments carrying run-proofs incl. an EXECUTED red-first probe; the missing-consumer wave — D148(2)'s per-workspace execution profile gets its decision record AND its build)

- **D205 [DECIDED — per-workspace execution profile RATIFIED (supplies the fresh decision record D148(2) demanded): storage = `workspaces.settings["chat"]["execution_profile"]`; resolved ONCE per Session spawn in `Session.init/1` feeding BOTH consumers; fail-safe default to :self_hosted; per-workspace WINS when set; `:command` stays the outermost bypass.]** STORAGE: the existing `workspaces.settings` jsonb (migration 20260709200000) grows a `"chat"` namespace — `settings["chat"]["execution_profile"]` ∈ `"cloud" | "self_hosted"` — via a NEW dedicated Tenancy accessor pair mirroring the theme/plugins pairs byte-for-byte in shape (`workspace_chat_settings/1` fail-safe read + `set_workspace_chat_settings/2` merge-in-place write, tenancy.ex:341-464 pattern; no generic settings writer exists and none is added). NO migration, NO new table, NO tenancy-catalog edit — `workspaces` is `@root_table` (catalog.ex:76), outside E1/E2/E3, so the W21 WorkspaceBundleTest trap is structurally dodged (verified against assert_partition!'s three drift triggers). The similarly-named `plugin_settings` TABLE is a global encrypted credential store keyed only by plugin_name — NOT a candidate (survey correction). READ PATH: resolve the profile ONCE inside `Session.init/1` (claude_chat.ex:1124-1230) from `session_opts[:workspace_id]` — already threaded unconditionally on EVERY turn by D110 (runtime/claude.ex:65), zero new plumbing — with ONE workspace lookup feeding BOTH consumers: `command/2`'s profile branch (:128) and `maybe_thread_cloud_tool_descriptors/1` (:1524 — SAME FILE, nested Session submodule; five scouts independently corrected the runtime/claude.ex guess). Switching only command/2 is BANNED: a cloud-profiled workspace would get a cloud turn with NO tool descriptors (silent knob-3 capability loss). A plain `Tenancy.get_workspace_by_id/1` there is safe — per-session human-cadence init, no Registry self-call hazard, and `Enablement.effective/1` already does the identical uncached read at per-render frequency. FAIL-SAFE DEFAULT: nil workspace_id (host-admin/:global sessions stamp SQL NULL per owner_ws_from_scope), absent key, malformed settings, unknown value, or ANY lookup failure → fall through to the global `:execution_profile` config → `@default_execution_profile :self_hosted`. Failure mode prevented: defaulting to :cloud would route every workspace's turns into a sandbox needing keys/binaries that exist nowhere (config grep: NOTHING sets :execution_profile in any config/deploy/cloud path — only :self_hosted has ever been live) → instance-wide dead chat. BUT an EXPLICIT `"cloud"` that cannot execute must FAIL THE TURN LOUDLY, never silently fall back to self-hosted — a silent fallback hands host access to exactly the tenant that opted into isolation. The three existing loud-failure shapes, named honestly: missing runner binary → fast-loud `{:stop, :binary_not_found}` (claude_chat.ex:1157-1162); bad/absent ANTHROPIC key → loud 401 `type:result is_error:true` frame (D23/D113c, live-proven); misconfigured/unauthenticated `vercel` CLI → loud-but-SLOW, bounded only by the shim's mandatory 10m create backstop (cloud-sandbox-runner.mjs:79) — accepted and named, not hidden. COMPOSITION: per-workspace WINS in BOTH directions when set; the global config is the unset default, NOT a ceiling (a ceiling is a second policy knob with zero consumers — the same premature-abstraction smell W15 killed); the `:command` config tuple stays the outermost verbatim bypass (claude_chat.ex:123, checked BEFORE the profile branch — test-only escape hatch, untouched). D107 RECONCILED: D107 rejected a persisted per-SESSION `execution_target` enum — a per-session USER choice, the wrong axis. Per-workspace is a coarser tenancy axis (many sessions inherit one workspace's deployment-like property): it generalizes D107's "instance property" one tenancy level down without reopening the rejected mechanism; a cloud session still carries `execution_target=managed`. FLIP CADENCE (run-proven asymmetry): profile resolves once per Session SPAWN. `:cloud` sessions respawn EVERY turn — the shim is one-shot by design (cloud-sandbox-runner.mjs:51-60) and ANY exit_status stops the Recorder (recorder.ex:585-599; proven live, recorder_test.exs:260 green) — so a flip bites on the workspace's very next turn. `:self_hosted` Sessions hold their Port across turns (send_user_message writes into the live Port, claude_chat.ex:1238-1250) — a flip bites on the next Session spawn (start / 30-min idle reap / explicit close), NOT retroactively mid-conversation. SCOPE FENCES: Codex is explicitly OUT (no parallel argv seam, zero execution_profile consumption — structurally unaffected); knob-4/per-workspace KEYS stay blocked (D148(1)/D198 — quote verbatim if ever touched); the ANTHROPIC key stays flat ambient env (D110/D23) — per-workspace PROFILE ≠ per-workspace KEYS, and the wish's implied run-secrets→sandbox-key link is FALSE; the LIVE cloud turn stays human-gated (`connectors-hg-live-isolated-cloud-turn`) — W23 proves ROUTING at the config/argv layer exactly as W11-W16 did.
- **D206 [DECIDED — workspace-identity sourcing LAW for chat turns: a turn threads the session's persisted `owner_workspace_id` and NOTHING else; chat_live.ex's ambient-assign sourcing is a PROVEN leak and gets fixed inside W23-1, not deferred.]** Verified divergence between the only two entry paths (funnel closed by grep: exactly 2 `Recorder.ensure` call sites, 1 `Runtime.open`, 1 `ClaudeChat.start_session` — no third entry anywhere in lib/): chat_controller.ex:576 threads `session.owner_workspace_id` (scope-authoritative, re-read via fetch_scoped every call) — CORRECT; chat_live.ex:3792-3793 threads `socket.assigns[:current_workspace] && ....id` — the AMBIENT Studio-tab assign, applied unconditionally to create AND resume. On the flat `/studio/chat` admin route, `current_workspace` is never nil — StudioChrome's `default_scope_fallback/1` (studio_chrome.ex:449-463) PINS it to the seeded Default workspace. Meanwhile chat_live's own create logic stamps `owner_workspace_id=nil` (:global) for every non-registered_host session (chat_live.ex:3734-3739). Under a naive W23 resolver, a genuinely-:global flat-route session would silently inherit the Default workspace's profile — the exact mis-routing class this wave exists to prevent, on the PRIMARY surface (Studio LiveView). FIX (in W23-1): `spawn_session/3` sources `workspace_id` from the loaded session struct's `owner_workspace_id` (mirror the controller), never from `current_workspace`. This makes the wave's own goal ("a workspace flips ONE setting, only that workspace's turns change") true on day one for every route.
- **D207 [DECIDED — the write surface: SettingsLive toggle, a `.bp_switch` plugin-pattern clone, ZERO router.ex; a per-write `workspace_admin?/2` re-gate is REQUIRED because the mount gate is coarser than the wish assumed.]** Surface: `BarkparkWeb.Studio.SettingsLive` at the already-mounted canonical scoped route `/w/:ws/p/:proj/studio/settings` (router.ex:1210-1237, admin+MFA on_mount) — one new render section + one `handle_event` cloning the plugin toggle (settings_live.ex:258-274, 511-593) writing through the new D205 Tenancy pair; ~75-125 lines across 2 files, zero migrations, zero router.ex. GATE PRECISION (verified, CORRECTING the wish): `LiveAuth :admin` does NOT enforce workspace-admin of the URL's target workspace — `on_mount(:admin, _params, …)` discards params (live_auth.ex:58); api tokens carry a FLAT global "admin" permission (auth.ex:760-762); user sessions check admin against the hardcoded DEFAULT workspace (live_auth.ex:172-184). The only per-target check in the pipeline is LiveScope READ-membership. Effective gate = (global-admin token OR Default-workspace-admin user) AND read-member-of-target. Two consequences: a Default-admin with mere read-membership can write a FOREIGN workspace's settings (pre-existing for theme/plugins); a genuine ws-B admin without Default-admin is blocked from their own workspace. Because execution_profile is routing-security-relevant (it routes a tenant's turns into a sandbox sharing ONE flat instance key), W23-2 adds a per-write `workspace_admin?/2` re-gate on the toggle event — the documented ConnectorsLive precedent (router.ex:1232 comment: deliberately stricter than the mount gate). Theme/plugin toggles keep today's behavior; the LiveAuth coarseness itself is filed as backlog (`connectors-liveauth-admin-target-workspace`), NOT absorbed into this wave. Never copy the wish's "admin of the target workspace" wording into any test name or doc — it is factually wrong about today's mount gate.

## Wave 24 — decisions (D208–D212, DECIDED with 4 verify assignments — two run-proofs incl. an EXECUTED escalation probe against SettingsLive; the finish-the-capability wave: W23-2 builds as the whole of W24)

- **D208 [DECIDED — W23-2 is UNBLOCKED and re-scoped as the entirety of Wave 24; every W23-era sequencing instruction is OBSOLETE; the true test baseline is 35, not 39.]** W23-1 (#3960) is an ancestor of origin/main (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 284293bae origin/main` passes; `Tenancy.workspace_chat_settings/1` + `set_workspace_chat_settings/2` live at tenancy.ex:487-534) — the prior builder's BLOCKED stamp (base 945c54650 lacked the pair) is MOOT. Consequences: the W23 slice plan's "branch off `loop-epic/connectors-w23-integration` after W23-1 lands there" is DEAD — the W24 builder branches off CURRENT origin/main directly; integration branch is `loop-epic/connectors-w24-integration` (fresh verified worktree, never the primary checkout). RECORD CORRECTION: the W23 slice plan's "(39-test file green dry-run at Decide)" was STALE — the file was RUN at W24 Verify AND re-run at W24 Decide: **35 tests, 0 failures** both times. 35 is the baseline; the builder still RUNS the file before editing (never trusts a count from prose). Zero router.ex diff re-confirmed: SettingsLive already rides live_session `:scoped_admin_studio` beside ConnectorsLive (router.ex:1210-1237) — no route, no blocker to file.
- **D209 [DECIDED — control shape: a NEW `render_execution_profile_section/1` (.bp_card of its own, placed after `render_theme_section/1`, before plugins) + `handle_event("toggle_execution_profile", …)` — a `.bp_switch` phx-click clone per D207, next value derived SERVER-SIDE, whitelist enforced in the handler.]** Markup: clone the plugin-row switch idiom (settings_live.ex:558-566) inside a theme-section-shaped card — `<.bp_switch checked={@execution_profile == "cloud"} on_label="Cloud" off_label="Self-hosted" phx-click="toggle_execution_profile" phx-value-ws={@current_workspace.id} aria-label=…/>`; D207 explicitly names the plugin toggle (phx-click), NOT the theme picker's phx-change form. Assigns: seed the default in `assign_page/1` (nil), project the real value in `handle_params/3` only `if connected?` via `Tenancy.workspace_chat_settings(current_workspace)["execution_profile"]` — exactly how `:bp_theme` is fed. VALUE DOMAIN: the handler derives the next value by flipping the CURRENT stored profile ("cloud" → "self_hosted", anything else → "cloud") — no client-supplied profile value is trusted; the write path still guards `value in ~w(cloud self_hosted)` before calling Tenancy (mirror of toggle_plugin's forged-name refusal; Tenancy itself has NO whitelist — verified). Flash voice matches the page: "Execution profile set to cloud for this workspace." / error flashes mirror the existing ones. UI copy ("Execution profile", label text) is builder latitude — D207 pins no literal strings; match the plugin-row voice.
- **D210 [DECIDED — write freshness law: READ-MERGE off the socket struct, WRITE BY ID.]** `set_workspace_chat_settings/2`'s struct arity merges against the PASSED struct's settings (no re-fetch — a stale `socket.assigns[:current_workspace]` would clobber sibling top-level keys written concurrently); its binary-id arity re-fetches via `get_workspace_by_id/1` then merges fresh. BOTH existing SettingsLive write sites already pass the id (`Tenancy.set_workspace_theme(ws.id, …)` ~L354; `set_workspace_plugin_settings(ws_id, …)` ~L898). The toggle does the same: read `chat = Tenancy.workspace_chat_settings(ws)` off the socket struct (fine — it only seeds the one key being edited), `Map.put(chat, "execution_profile", next)`, then **`Tenancy.set_workspace_chat_settings(ws.id, merged)`** — id, never the struct. This preserves sibling chat sub-keys (the setter replaces the whole "chat" submap — verified) AND sibling top-level keys (theme/plugins) under concurrency.
- **D211 [DECIDED — the per-write re-gate + red-first fixture, ratified from an EXECUTED escalation probe: the hole is REAL on SettingsLive today, and the ConnectorsLive outsider recipe transfers VERBATIM.]** A throwaway 3-test probe RUN at Verify proved: an outsider token (global perms `["read","write","admin"]`, target-ws membership role "member") (1) is NOT `workspace_admin?` of the target, (2) MOUNTS SettingsLive at the scoped route (mount gate = global perm), and (3) drives `toggle_plugin` with the CORRECT ws id — `guard_bound_ws` passes (scope matches), NO admin re-gate exists, and the write PERSISTS. That is the exact escalation W24 closes and the exact RED-FIRST shape: correct ws.id (so scope-guard is NOT what refuses) + non-admin actor (so ONLY the new re-gate can refuse). HANDLER SHAPE: compose `guard_bound_ws/3` (kept, unchanged) WITH a ConnectorsLive-style authorize (connectors_live.ex:419-425): `principal = socket.assigns[:api_token] || socket.assigns[:current_user]` (no unified principal assign exists; `workspace_admin?/2` accepts either struct directly), `TenancyAuth.workspace_admin?(principal, ws.id)` fail-closed; refusal = `put_flash(:error, …owner or admin of this workspace…)` + `{:noreply, socket}`, no redirect, setting UNCHANGED. FIXTURE (proven working verbatim): two tokens both `["read","write","admin"]`; admin gets `create_membership(ws.id, admin.id, "admin")`, outsider gets `create_membership(ws.id, outsider.id)` (default role "member", auth.ex:87); route needs a real dataset (`create_project_with_dataset`); session via `init_test_session(conn, %{"api_token" => raw})`. The red-first test MUST red if the re-gate is deleted — cloning the existing forged-ws SCOPE tests is vacuous (they refuse on scope mismatch, never admin identity). D207's naming ban stands: never write "admin of the target workspace" about the MOUNT gate. This slice's re-gate is the ONLY gate — the general LiveAuth fix stays backlog (`connectors-liveauth-admin-target-workspace`, #3946, open/unclaimed); theme/plugin handlers keep today's behavior, untouched.
- **D212 [DECIDED — ledger + merge discipline for W24: claim-before-PR-open is a LIVE gate requirement; main's gate is HEALTHY (both recent reds = the named QueueTest CTE flake); D166 merge-past applies with a pre-merge re-poll.]** The task doc_id is literally `connectors-execution-profile-settings-toggle` (`_id == _publishedId`, `_draft:false`, no drafts. shadow — lifecycle writes land straight in the published perspective pr-task-gate reads anonymously). It reads `lifecycle_status:"open"`, `claim.worker:null`, stale epoch 4 — a PR opened now FAILS pr-task-gate. Builder MUST: `bp task claim connectors-execution-profile-settings-toggle <worker> --yes` BEFORE PR-open (read the fresh `claim.epoch` from the response — never reuse 4), pulse ≤45min (task_lease_ttl 2700s) through review, stamp criteria 0-3 the moment proven, NEVER stamp/close criterion 4 (MERGE-GATED — lead closes), PR body carries the literal line `Task: connectors-execution-profile-settings-toggle`. GATE HEALTH at Decide: last 5 main elixir.yml runs = 3 green, 2 red, and BOTH reds are the identical QueueTest CTE failure (queue_test.exs:462, "Actual Loops"==200 planner variance) — a named member of #3558's 4-flake rotation (D204). ## Wave 25 — decisions (D213–D218, DECIDED from 5 verify assignments ALL carrying run output — the cloud credential config-embed design RATIFIED: shim = headersHelper-executor, `vercel sandbox copy` transport, ticket-in-payload; paper `connectors-wave-25-2026-07-17`)

- **D213 [DECIDED — ARCH: the shim is the cloud headersHelper-executor. HOST-side, per-turn fetch of FINISHED headers from the EXISTING bridge tool-headers loopback route; Elixir emits ONLY the ticket; no sealed ref ever crosses any boundary.]** The shim (host Node process, same box as the bridge) fetches, once per provider entry, `POST {CLOUD_SANDBOX_BRIDGE_URL:-http://127.0.0.1:4020/connectors}/connect/tool-headers/<provider>` with JSON body `{"ticket":"<tool-session ticket>"}` — the byte-exact route/shape D71 built (provider rides the PATH, ticket rides the BODY; webhook-server.ts parseConnectRoute + connect.ts toolHeaders). The bridge returns the FINISHED `{"Authorization":"Bearer <v>"}` — `bearerFromCredentialRef` (connect.ts:456-472) stays the SOLE owner of the W9 dual-shape (bare GitHub PAT vs Linear OAuth bundle); the shim NEVER receives `credential_ref` (sealed or raw) and never re-derives a bearer (a Linear bundle would 401 live while GitHub masked the bug). RECORD CORRECTION to the wish's framing: no "sealed ref" is emitted by Elixir at all — the install selector is (provider path segment + ticket's HMAC-proven workspace); the seal never leaves the bridge, which is STRONGER than the wish's sealed-ref-forwarding frame. D38 held by construction: Elixir threads only the non-secret ticket it already mints. REJECTED: in-VM fetch (needs egress widening through the deny-all wall — exactly D5/D24; only conceivable after `connectors-cloud-egress-allowlist-widening`, a separate named task); DEK-shipping into the VM (worst blast radius; bridge owns decryption); shim-side bearer derivation. The toolHeaders route's provider-owned refresh branch (D90-D92) runs bridge-side exactly as in the local flow — the shim is indifferent; the OFFLINE stub bridge must use a registry WITHOUT refresh deps so byte-for-byte assertions stay honest. FAILURE = LOUD, FAIL-CLOSED, EARLY: any fetch error/refusal hard-fails the turn BEFORE any vercel invocation (exit nonzero, zero sandbox creates) with a FIXED-STRING error — never fold response bodies/config/credential bytes into `Error.message` (the stderr tail is broadcast VERBATIM to every viewer's ChatLive: claude_chat.ex:1267/1668-1689 → recorder.ex:585-594 → chat_live.ex:4512).
- **D214 [DECIDED — TICKET: the existing tool-session ConnectTicket AS-IS (TTL-bound REUSABLE, 8h, workspace-wide), carried as a TOP-LEVEL b64-payload key `bpConnectorTicket`; CloudPolicy untouched; ticket argv exposure ACCEPTED; ticket STRIPPED before materialization and NEVER inside a headers map.]** Run-proven facts that settle this: the ticket is NOT single-use (no replay/nonce store on either side — connect_ticket.ex + ticket.ts full reads) and NOT provider-scoped (`sign_tool` pins the reserved provider `tool-session`, connect_ticket.ex:59 — ONE ticket opens ALL of a workspace's tool connectors; the concrete provider rides the route path). The direction's "per-turn minted scoped key" is therefore refined to: minted fresh per turn (freshness free — the shim is one-shot per turn), reusable within its 8h window, workspace-HMAC-bound; NO new one-shot ticket type is chartered (a replay store would be net-new bridge state for no marginal wall — anything on-host that could replay a ps-scraped ticket is already inside the trust boundary that holds the plaintext in shim memory). PLACEMENT: Elixir's `cloud_mcp_config_args/1` (claude_chat.ex:329-341, the SOLE caller of `cloud_mcp_servers/2`) adds the top-level sibling key `"bpConnectorTicket":"<ticket>"` beside `"mcpServers"` — ONLY when the servers map is non-empty (0-install argv stays byte-identical; the W12 belt survives). Per-entry placement REJECTED on evidence: it reds BOTH modules' test layers, forces a ticket param into CloudPolicy (breaking its documented no-bridge purity, cloud_policy.ex:155-158), and buys zero isolation since the ticket is workspace-scoped anyway. The SAME ticket already minted in `tool_descriptors_for/1` (sign_tool at claude_chat.ex:1565) is carried via session_opts and reused — mint ONCE per turn, never twice. Ticket-in-argv is ACCEPTED as non-secret-class: redeemable ONLY from host loopback (any `x-forwarded-*` → opaque 404 BEFORE the body is read, webhook-server.ts:706-720), TTL-bounded, same exposure class as the shim-memory plaintext it authorizes. `CONNECTORS_CONNECT_SECRET` stays scrubbed from shim env (claude_chat.ex:763) — the ticket, never the secret, crosses. The shim STRIPS `bpConnectorTicket` from the final materialized config (claude tolerates unknown siblings — proven — so stripping is hygiene the PROOF enforces, not claude), and NOTHING but the real `Authorization` value ever goes inside a `headers` map: PROVEN that claude transmits EVERY headers-map key to the connector endpoint (a ticket there would leak to GitHub/Linear).
- **D215 [DECIDED — TRANSPORT: `vercel sandbox copy` carries the credential-bearing config into the VM; argv-b64 (the D127 transport) is retained ONLY for credential-less payloads. Stdin is DEAD — proven three independent ways.]** Verify killed the stdin option definitively: the pinned CLI 54.13.0's `sandbox exec` maps to SDK `runCommand` whose COMPLETE input surface (RunCommandParams, session.d.ts:20-67) has NO stdin/input field (stdout/stderr are Writable OUTPUT sinks); `exec --help` — `--tty` is "a no-op", `--interactive` refuses non-TTY and cannot combine with `--timeout` (the shim mandates both non-TTY and --timeout); D108's live probe (piped 6 bytes, wc -c read 0) reconfirmed. VERSION-DRIFT NOTE: D108 cited @vercel/sandbox 2.6.1; CLI 54.13.0 bundles 2.1.1 — BOTH stdin-less; any future CLI bump must re-probe exec/copy surfaces (backlogged on the live-reconfirm task). And today's channel PROVABLY leaks: a planted credential in mcpConfigB64 lands in the local vercel child's argv in base64 form (exact whole-blob match in the captured `bash -lc` element; RAW form does NOT leak today — the blob is never decoded host-side). THE CHANNEL: when credentials are embedded, the shim writes the final config to a host tmpfile (mode 0600), runs `vercel sandbox copy <tmpfile> <sandbox>:/tmp/bp-cloud-mcp.json` — content streams via the SDK filesystem writeFile API body; ONLY THE PATH is argv — then unlinks the tmpfile in a `finally` immediately after. The in-VM script then SKIPS the mcp `printf|base64 -d` line but keeps `--mcp-config /tmp/bp-cloud-mcp.json --strict-mcp-config` (D127's exactly-one---env pin and the D109 host-path hard-fail both survive untouched). EMBED TARGET RATIFIED BY PROBE: claude 2.1.212 PROVEN to send a static `headers` map's `Authorization` on EVERY request (initialize, notifications, SSE poll, tools/list) to a `type:http` MCP server under `--strict-mcp-config` — the design's load-bearing assumption holds; re-confirm on the sandbox's 2.1.211 at the live gate. `--strict-mcp-config` is NOT a schema validator (unknown top-level and sibling keys pass silently) — so the strip law is proof-enforced. Ticket-less payloads (and the turn frame, always) keep today's argv-b64 path byte-identical. HOST-TMPFILE RESIDUAL chartered honestly: a transient 0600 host file is the SAME confinement class as the shim already holding plaintext in memory; the proof adds a lifetime assertion (absent post-run) because argv+env capture alone cannot see host disk. ORDERING LAW: parse argv → fetch+merge headers (fail-closed) → create/reuse sandbox → copy config → exec turn.
- **D216 [DECIDED — PROOF FORM: new red-first offline script `scripts/connectors/w25-credential-embed-proof.sh` with a THIRD fake (stub bridge via new env `CLOUD_SANDBOX_BRIDGE_URL`); assertion technique = decode-every-base64-token-then-grep-raw; w12+w14 stay green; plus a Linear-bundle leg in the REAL vitest suite.]** The proof stands up (1) the w12-style fake vercel (`CLOUD_SANDBOX_VERCEL_BIN`) capturing NUL-delimited argv + full env per invocation AND snapshotting the src-file content of any `sandbox copy` call, and (2) a stub bridge `http.createServer` on an ephemeral loopback port (Node ≥18 global fetch — the shim gains its first network code, zero new deps; env var mirrors Elixir's `CONNECTORS_BRIDGE_URL` convention, default `http://127.0.0.1:4020/connectors`), serving canned `{"Authorization":"Bearer <planted>"}` for the matching workspace ticket and an opaque 404 for a mismatched one. ASSERTIONS: (a) planted credential ABSENT from ALL captured argv+env, raw form AND via decode-every-base64-token-then-grep-raw — the naive 0/1/2-pad phase-shift grep is PROVEN INEFFECTIVE (base64 grouping anchors to the buffer start, not the substring offset; the offset-agnostic decode-scan deterministically found today's leak) — never charter the naive form; (b) exactly-one `--env ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` pin intact; (c) the copied config carries `headers.Authorization` per provider and NO `bpConnectorTicket` key; (d) CROSS-WORKSPACE: mismatched-ticket leg → stub 404 → shim exits nonzero with ZERO vercel invocations (w12 `assert_gate_rejects` idiom); (e) FRESHNESS across create→reuse: two turns with two different stub credentials — each turn's copied config carries its OWN value (FORBIDDEN SHAPE: gating the fetch inside `createSandbox()` — the fetch lives in the per-turn path, both branches); (f) host tmpfile absent post-run; (g) refusal stderr carries fixed strings only — no credential/response bytes. RED-FIRST: the script REDs against origin/main's shim (no ticket path exists) — the commit sequence shows it failing before the runner change. `w12-shim-confinement-proof.sh` + `w14-session-sandbox-proof.sh` MUST stay green on the modified runner; the new script joins connectors.yml's shim-confinement job (shellcheck -S error clean). REAL-BRIDGE side: cross-workspace refusal is ALREADY test-pinned green (tool-descriptors.test.ts:397-415 — WS_B's own valid tool ticket vs WS_A's GitHub install → opaque 404), but ONLY for the bare-PAT shape — the wave ADDS a Linear-OAuth-bundle leg (seed `{access_token,refresh_token,expires_at}` via `upsertInstall`, registry without refresh deps): WS_A ticket → `{"Authorization":"Bearer at_…"}` (access_token extraction through the REAL route), WS_B ticket → opaque 404.
- **D217 [DECIDED — E-SLICE SHAPE: one function, one test file; CloudPolicy untouched; the decoded-argv assertions are the red-first signal.]** The Elixir slice touches ONLY `cloud_mcp_config_args/1` + the session_opts plumbing that carries the already-minted ticket from `tool_descriptors_for/1` (store the ticket beside the descriptors in session_opts at `maybe_thread_cloud_tool_descriptors/1`, claude_chat.ex:1596-1603; consume in `cloud_build_args/2` → `cloud_mcp_config_args/1`). `CloudPolicy.cloud_mcp_servers/2` / `cloud_tool_server_entry/2` and their 3 exact-match unit assertions (cloud_policy_test.exs:171-176/178-187/197-220) stay UNTOUCHED — purity preserved. The decoded-argv belt assertions (cloud_policy_test.exs:296-303/313-320, `decode_mcp_flag/1` in the same file at :271) are UPDATED to expect `%{"mcpServers" => …, "bpConnectorTicket" => <ticket>}` — they RED first, then green. Add one NEW assertion: 0-install argv contains NO `--mcp-config-b64` flag and therefore no ticket (already the existing omission law — re-pin it against the new key). Baseline run-proven at Verify: 22 tests, 0 failures at 42e2caffe (builder RE-RUNS at build time — counts in prose are never trusted, D183/D208 law).
- **D218 [DECIDED — GATE + MERGE LANGUAGE for W25 (supersedes D212's #3558 citation).]** elixir.yml is GREEN at the verify-time tip (run 29614548921 @ 2026-07-17T21:23:26Z, 42e2caffe). The QueueTest CTE red (queue_test.exs:462) is TRULY fixed as of commit `edda689085f` / PR #3985 — CITE THE COMMIT, never issue #3558's close (closed at 17:01:34Z short of its own 10-consecutive-green bar, with a red inside its own window). A single, UNFILED, UNRECURRED DeployRunnerTest race (deploy_runner_test.exs:1030, `GenServer.stop(pid,:normal,:infinity)` exit, run 29593711674; 12 consecutive greens since) stays unfiled per the single-sighting convention — if it recurs before the E slice merges, FILE a flake task citing run ids BEFORE merging past it; never fold it into the QueueTest precedent (different test, different signature). ### Wave 2026-07-18 — Wave 25 DECIDE — the cloud credential path RATIFIED from run-proofs: stdin is dead, `sandbox copy` is the channel, the ticket rides the payload, the bridge keeps the seal
Five verify assignments ALL carried run output (paper `connectors-wave-25-2026-07-17`): stdin to `vercel sandbox exec` killed three independent ways (SDK RunCommandParams has no input surface; --help probes; D108's live wc -c=0 reconfirmed) and the non-argv channel FOUND — `vercel sandbox copy` streams file CONTENT via the API with only the path in argv (D215); claude PROVEN to honor a static headers map under `--strict-mcp-config` (Authorization sent on every MCP request; headers-map keys are TRANSMITTED to the endpoint — hence the never-a-ticket-in-headers law; strictness is not a validator — hence the proof-enforced strip, D214/D215); today's leak REPRODUCED (planted credential found in the local vercel child's argv in b64 form; naive phase-shift grep proven ineffective, decode-every-token proven deterministic — D216's assertion form); the cross-workspace wall found ALREADY test-pinned green at the real bridge (opaque 404 for WS_B's valid ticket) needing only the Linear-bundle leg; the Elixir seam run-proven green (22 tests) with placement settled by evidence — top-level key, CloudPolicy untouched, the ticket is workspace-wide (`tool-session`) so per-entry placement buys nothing (D214/D217); gate language corrected — cite fix commit edda689085f never #3558's premature close, DeployRunnerTest:1030 single-sighting stays unfiled unless it recurs (D218). TWO slices: W25-E on OPUS (one function, one test file), W25-N on FABLE (the shim's first network code + transport swap + 7-assertion red-first proof). Live serving stays on `connectors-hg-live-isolated-cloud-turn`. Paper: `connectors-wave-25-2026-07-17`.

## Wave 26 — decisions (D219–D224, DECIDED from 6 verify assignments ALL carrying run output incl. an EXECUTED write-escalation probe + a live query-param spoof probe; the SECURITY DESIGN+BUILD wave: the W24-proven privilege escalation gets closed AT THE MOUNT for every scoped-admin LiveView; paper `connectors-wave-26-2026-07-18`)

- **D219 [DECIDED — THE CRUX, run-settled and it REFUTES the strategic direction's tentative "fix only the user arm": fix BOTH arms, made target-workspace-aware on scoped sessions via a NEW distinct on_mount hook atom `{LiveAuth, :scoped_admin}` — NOT params-presence keying inside the shared `:admin` clause (proven spoofable).]** Two independent run-proofs settle this. (1) TOKEN ARM MUST BE FIXED: both executed escalation probes (W5/D49 ConnectorsLive, W24/D211 SettingsLive) AND all three shipped scoped-admin outsider tests construct the outsider as an API TOKEN with flat perms `["read","write","admin"]` + membership role `"member"` of the target — which today mounts via the TOKEN arm (`Auth.has_permission?(token,"admin")`, live_auth.ex:152, a flat array test with ZERO workspace resolution). The user arm (authorize_user, live_auth.ex:172-184) is NEVER reached for these outsiders, so a user-arm-only fix leaves the proven hole wide open (it just relocates to whichever sibling lacks a per-write re-gate — SettingsLive theme/plugin writes, all of ChatLive). PROBE-C run-proof: an outsider token (flat admin, member-only of ws B) mounts SettingsLive at `/w/:ws/p/:proj/studio/settings` and flips the workspace theme, and the write PERSISTS (before="evergreen", outsider-wrote="charple", workspace_admin?=false) — `guard_bound_ws/3` is a stale-SCOPE check, NOT an authority gate. (2) KEYING MECHANISM — a distinct hook atom, NOT params-presence: run-proof (flat-route-query-param-spoof) mounted the FLAT route `/studio/org-admin?workspace_slug=evil-injected-ws` and on_mount received `params = %{"workspace_slug" => "evil-injected-ws", ...}` VERBATIM — a gate that resolves+authorizes whenever `params["workspace_slug"]` is present would let a ws-B admin self-select ws-B authorization on a flat Default-semantics surface (a NEW under-tight hole). The only in-hook route-shape signal (`live_session_name`) is ABSENT on the dead (HTTP) render (proven: `PROBE_ADMIN_LIVE_SESSION=nil` on the first of two on_mount invocations), so it cannot discriminate. THE FIX: register `{BarkparkWeb.LiveAuth, :scoped_admin}` (new clause) ONLY on the two scoped-needs-fix live_sessions; its `params["workspace_slug"]` is then guaranteed PATH-derived (path params win over same-name query params ONLY for keys the matched route declares — proven: scoped route with `?workspace_slug=evil` still saw the path value `"default"`). The new clause tries the TOKEN arm target-aware (`workspace_admin?(api_token, target_ws.id)`, dev-fallback exempt) then the USER arm target-aware (`workspace_admin?(user, target_ws.id)`), fail-closed. Flat sessions keep `{LiveAuth, :admin}` byte-identical.
- **D220 [DECIDED — FUNCTION: the mount gate authorizes via `Tenancy.Auth.workspace_admin?/2` (role-only), NOT `authorize/3(:admin)`. This partially REFUTES the direction's "reuse authorize(user, ws, :admin)" phrasing — authorize/3(:admin) does NOT close the token-arm hole.]** PROBE-D run-proof: the two functions DIVERGE. For a token with membership role `"admin"` but flat perms only `[read,write]`, `workspace_admin?/2 => true` while `authorize/3(:admin) => {:error, :forbidden}` (authorize/3's token arm ALSO requires the flat `"admin"` perm via `permits?`, auth.ex:291-292). authorize/3(:admin) is WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS for tokens: UNDER-TIGHT (a member-role token with global admin perm — the exact W5/W24 outsider — PASSES it, leaving the hole) and OVER-TIGHT (a legit role-admin token without the flat admin perm is LOCKED OUT). `workspace_admin?/2` is right in both, IS the written tenancy law (`docs/contracts/tenancy.md` §Roles: "the grant role — not global permissions — decides workspace-admin authority (`workspace_admin?/2`, true for owner/admin)"), and is EXACTLY the function all three shipped per-write re-gates already call (connectors_live.ex:423, settings_live.ex:420, chat_hosts_live.ex:63) — so mount and per-write become semantically identical and W24's re-gate becomes true zero-gap belt-and-suspenders. RESIDUAL documented, not an oversight: `workspace_admin?/2` is CUSTOM-ROLE-BLIND (hardcoded `@admin_roles ~w(owner admin)`, ignores `role_permissions`), an over-tight edge for a workspace defining a custom :admin-granting role — but it is PRE-EXISTING and IDENTICAL in the per-write gates, so choosing it keeps mount+write consistent (choosing authorize/3 would let such a user mount then fail every write). The gate MUST pass the principal STRUCT (`socket.assigns[:api_token] || socket.assigns[:current_user]`), never a raw id (the raw-binary clause defaults principal_type "api_token", auth.ex:142).
- **D221 [DECIDED — RESOLVER: reuse `Tenancy.get_workspace_by_slug/1` read DIRECTLY from the raw `params` argument (NOT an assign), fail-closed redirect on nil.]** In BOTH scoped-needs-fix sessions the workspace-resolving hook (LiveScope `:resolve` for `:scoped_admin_studio`; PluginScopeSession `:scope` for `:scoped_plugin_admin`) runs AFTER `{LiveAuth,:admin}` in the on_mount list — so the new `:scoped_admin` clause CANNOT read `socket.assigns[:current_workspace]` (it doesn't exist yet). It must call `Tenancy.get_workspace_by_slug(params["workspace_slug"])` itself — the SAME slug-only, fail-closed-nil resolver LiveScope calls at live_scope.ex:86 (a second parallel resolver is exactly the wrong-key silent-fallback failure the charter bans). On nil (unresolvable slug), on missing param, or on a non-admin principal: `{:halt, redirect to "/studio", flash "Admin access required"}` (mirror LiveScope's fail-closed redirect UX; do NOT mimic the API JSON envelope). This double lookup (also resolved again by LiveScope two hooks later) is a per-mount cost, not a correctness divergence, PROVIDED it calls the identical function.
- **D222 [DECIDED — BLAST RADIUS, SCOPE-OUT, and the OWNED behavioral change. Exactly 6 live_sessions carry `{LiveAuth,:admin}` on origin/main (095fa723): 3 FLAT stay byte-identical, 2 SCOPED flip to `:scoped_admin`, 1 SCOPED is the documented scope-OUT and KEEPS `:admin`.]** FLAT (untouched, no `:workspace_slug` in path → Default-workspace semantics per tenancy's flat posture): `:admin_studio` (router.ex:841), `:admin_swatch` (879), `:plugin_admin` (952). FLIP to `{LiveAuth,:scoped_admin}`: `:scoped_plugin_admin` (atom at router.ex:1215 — PingLive/InboxLive) and `:scoped_admin_studio` (atom at router.ex:1239 — SettingsLive/ChatHostsLive/ChatLive/ConnectorsLive). KEEPS `:admin` — the SOLE documented scope-out: `:scoped_admin_studio_dataset` (atom at router.ex:1340 — PluginsLive/PluginSettingsLive); its router comment (1324-1334) declares it INTENTIONALLY flat/global: "the surface is the global plugin registry scoped by dataset, not a per-workspace substance" — gating it on the URL workspace's membership would be a category error, and its existing tests use Default-admin tokens (would silently over-tighten under a blanket fix). ChatLive is dual-mounted (flat `:admin_studio` + scoped `:scoped_admin_studio`) — the two-atom split gives it the correct semantics in EACH session automatically (flat → Default; scoped → target-ws), which params-keying could not. DEV-HOST / self-hosted UNAFFECTED (`local-dev-host-is-admin` held): `dev_browser_token_fallback/0` is `Mix.env()==:dev`-guarded and unset in config/test.exs — structurally inert offline (proven: plugins_live_test redirect-without-admin-token tests pass in :test); the `:scoped_admin` token arm EXEMPTS the dev fallback so the seeded dev token (member of Default only) still administers every workspace locally. OWNED BEHAVIORAL CHANGE (intended by the wish, stated explicitly): the connectors catalog / settings / chat-hosts / chat panels become admin-only AT MOUNT for the target workspace, so a read-member can no longer VIEW them — the 4 existing tests that encode "member mounts, then per-write refused" (chat_hosts_live_test.exs:68, settings_live_test.exs:810, connectors_live_test.exs:326 + :697) become "member REJECTED at mount" (run-proven: a target-aware token arm reds EXACTLY these 4 via mount-time redirect, and 87 legit-admin/flat/dataset-scope-out tests stay GREEN). W24's per-write `workspace_admin?/2` re-gate STAYS as belt-and-suspenders — W26 composes with it, never removes it.
- **D223 [DECIDED — @canonical marker + adjacent backlog. The mount gate earns a discoverability marker; three real adjacent holes exploration surfaced are FILED, not folded.]** Add `# @canonical capability:workspace-admin-authority aka:is_admin,workspace_admin,mount_gate,scoped_admin doc:docs/contracts/tenancy.md` directly above `workspace_admin?/2` (tenancy/auth.ex) — the textbook decoy case (`authorize/3(:admin)` looks like it answers "is this principal admin of this workspace" but diverges on the token arm; no existing marker covers workspace-admin authority). ADJACENT BACKLOG (survive the mount fix, out of W26 scope): (a) `connectors-tickets-keys-workspace-blind-idor` (p2 security) — `Keys.rotate/pause/unpause` route through `get_ticket_key/1` (keys.ex:200-212) which filters ONLY by id+kind, no workspace_id predicate: any admin who can mount InboxLive for ws A can rotate/pause/revoke a ticket key by UUID belonging to ws B; (b) `connectors-chatlive-per-write-belt` (p2 security) — ChatLive's ~40 write handlers (incl. arm-bypass, set-execution-host/target) have ZERO per-write authority re-gate; after W26's mount fix, add the ConnectorsLive-style belt for defense-in-depth parity; (c) `connectors-settingslive-theme-plugin-per-write-belt` (p3 security) — SettingsLive `set_workspace_theme`/`toggle_plugin`/`set_plugin_placement` route only through `guard_bound_ws` (stale-scope, not authority); add the `workspace_admin?/2` re-gate for parity with the exec-profile toggle.
- **D224 [DECIDED — GATE + MERGE for W26. elixir.yml required-clean; the cross-cutting test trap is the wave's #1 risk; router.ex is IN-BOUNDS for the atom flip ONLY.]** elixir.yml Test job is GREEN on origin/main HEAD (095fa723) and the last 5 completed runs (verify recheck). No open PR or recent commit touches live_auth.ex or the scoped-admin LiveViews (only unrelated PR #2907). CROSS-CUTTING TEST TRAP (bit W21 AND W25): live_auth.ex fronts many LiveViews — the W26-1 builder MUST rewrite the 4 sibling reds IN THE SAME PR (a targeted run is green but full-suite CI reds on a sibling asserting the OLD mount behavior). FLAKE ROTATION: a DIFFERENT-single-test-per-run the wave doesn't touch is the flake signature (recent rotation: studio_anon_lockdown_test.exs:67, runtime_admission_test.exs:38, deploy_runner_test.exs:999 — NOT ChatLiveTest/QueueTest per the stale digest); if ChatLiveTest reds during W26 that is far more likely a REAL break (ChatLive has zero scoped coverage today), re-run the SAME commit to disambiguate. Full `mix test` OOMs locally — TARGETED files only. ROUTER.EX is IN-BOUNDS for W26-1 for the two-atom flip ONLY (D219 proved the target-workspace resolution genuinely requires it — the wish's own escape clause); theme/paper/golden/deploy_runner/openapi OFF-LIMITS. MERGE DISCIPLINE (proven W11–W25): integration branch `loop-epic/connectors-w26-integration` in a FRESH VERIFIED worktree off CURRENT origin/main (NEVER the primary checkout); builders branch off origin/main NEVER local HEAD (W26-2 off the integration branch AFTER W26-1 lands there); claim BEFORE PR-open (re-claim if a fix-cycle lapses the lease); PR body carries `Task: <slug>` per slice; builders stamp evidence but NEVER close the merge-gated criterion — the LEAD closes on merge. This charter commit lands on LOCAL main — cherry-pick it into the integration PR (rotating-slot conflict: keep BOTH sequential sections; never pipe cherry-pick through `&&`-tails that mask the conflict exit).

### Wave 2026-07-18 — Wave 26 DECIDE — the mount hole closes for EVERY scoped-admin LiveView: both arms target-aware via a distinct hook atom, run-proven regression-safe
Six verify assignments ALL carried run output (paper `connectors-wave-26-2026-07-18`). THE CRUX settled AGAINST the tentative direction: a user-arm-only fix does NOT close the W24-proven escalation — both executed probes and all three shipped outsider tests are TOKEN principals riding the token arm, and PROBE-C proved the write is LIVE today (outsider token flips SettingsLive theme, persists, workspace_admin?=false). So BOTH arms get fixed, target-aware, via a NEW `{LiveAuth,:scoped_admin}` hook atom — because raw params-presence keying is SPOOFABLE on flat routes (run-proof: `?workspace_slug=evil` leaks verbatim into the flat `:admin` on_mount; `live_session_name` is nil on the dead render, so the only route-shape signal is the router atom) — D219. FUNCTION settled by PROBE-D: `workspace_admin?/2` (role-only), NOT `authorize/3(:admin)` which diverges wrong in BOTH directions on tokens (leaves the hole open for member+global-admin; locks out role-admin without the flat perm) and contradicts tenancy.md law — D220. RESOLVER: reuse `get_workspace_by_slug/1` off raw params (LiveAuth runs before LiveScope), fail-closed — D221. BLAST RADIUS mapped to origin/main: 6 sessions, 3 flat untouched, 2 flip, 1 (`:scoped_admin_studio_dataset`) the documented global-registry scope-out KEEPS `:admin`; dev-host exempt; the 4 sibling reds run-proven to be EXACTLY the ones that flip (member→rejected-at-mount) while 87 legit/flat/scope-out tests stay green — D222. `@canonical` on workspace_admin?/2 + three adjacent holes FILED not folded (Keys IDOR, ChatLive belt, SettingsLive belt) — D223. Gate green, cross-cutting test trap is the #1 risk (rewrite the 4 sibling reds in-PR), router.ex in-bounds for the two-atom flip only — D224. TWO slices: W26-1 on FABLE (the fix + 4 sibling rewrites + new dual-direction proof), W26-2 on OPUS (additive ChatLive+InboxLive scoped-mount proof, sequenced). Paper: `connectors-wave-26-2026-07-18`.

### Wave 2026-07-18 — Wave 26 BUILT + REVIEWED — the mount hole is CLOSED at the chokepoint for every scoped-admin LiveView (grade A)
Both slices land the wish's general fix. **W26-1** (`connectors-liveauth-admin-target-workspace`, final branch `loop-epic/w26-1-liveauth-scoped-admin-mount-gate-t-0-r`): the new `on_mount(:scoped_admin, params, session, socket)` clause resolves the target ws from `params["workspace_slug"]` via the SAME `Tenancy.get_workspace_by_slug/1` LiveScope uses and authorizes BOTH principal arms role-only via `workspace_admin?/2` (struct in, never a raw id), fail-closed to flash + `/studio` redirect; dev fallback instance-root-exempt (inert outside `:dev`, local host untouched). `on_mount(:admin,…)` byte-identical; router.ex exactly the two atom flips (`:scoped_plugin_admin` + `:scoped_admin_studio`), `:scoped_admin_studio_dataset` correctly kept on `:admin` (D222). `@canonical capability:workspace-admin-authority` marker added (docs-anchors-check PASS). **W26-2** (`connectors-w26-chatlive-inbox-scoped-mount-proof`, final branch `loop-epic/w26-2-scoped-mount-rejection-proof-for-t-1`): two NEW additive test files covering ChatLive's scoped mount (~40 write handlers, dual-mounted, previously zero scoped coverage) + Tickets InboxLive (`mint_key` mints a live credential, zero per-write re-gate); vacuous-green guarded by an enabled fake chat runtime. REVIEWER re-verified GREEN on the REAL integration state: W26-1 gate **147/0**, W26-2 gate **303/0** against the actual W26-1 fix (not the reconstruction). RED-FIRST re-proven: reverting the two router atoms to `:admin` reds 6/9 of the new W26-1 suite — pinning BOTH the security fix (outsiders rejected) AND the D207 over-tight lockout fix (legit target admins mount). NO reviewer code fixes needed; format clean. Ledger clean — both tasks `in_progress`, merge-gated criteria (W26-1 AC5, W26-2 AC4) left OPEN for the lead; the 3 D223 adjacent holes filed as open child tasks. STALLED: nothing. LEAD MERGE: land W26-1 first (PR body `Task: connectors-liveauth-admin-target-workspace`; charter D219-D224 already cherry-picked as 55d44e99d), then cherry-pick W26-2's `87f52cc50` and DROP the reconstruction base `ae019e843`; both `.ex/.exs` → wait for the Elixir Test gate. NEXT: the epic frontier is the named human gates (live isolated cloud turn, sandbox-runner deploy); highest-leverage non-gate follow-up is the D223 per-write belt parity (chatlive + settingslive) now that the mount is fixed, plus a ConnectorsLive/SettingsLive moduledoc touch-up (they still describe the pre-W26 "mount gate is NOT the mint gate" posture).
